# Complete UPSC Indian Polity Flashcard Deck
> **Coverage:** All 395+ Articles grouped by Part (including insertions and omissions), All 12 Schedules, and Top 26 Constitutional Amendments.
> **Format:** Strict two-sided flashcards (`flashcard N:`, `frontside:`, `backside:`), Anki-import ready, uncompressed, fact-checked against the official Constitution of India and landmark Supreme Court jurisprudence.
> **Last Updated:** 2026 | Grounded in official Law Ministry Constitution text and UPSC syllabus.

 - -

# SECTION 1: THE ARTICLES OF THE CONSTITUTION (PARTS I TO XXII)

## PART I - THE UNION AND ITS TERRITORY

flashcard 1:
frontside:
Article 1 - PART I - THE UNION AND ITS TERRITORY
Prompt: What does Article 1 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Name and territory of the Union
Status: In Force
Mandate: Declares that India, that is Bharat, shall be a Union of States. Territory comprises: (a) Territories of the States; (b) Union territories specified in the First Schedule; (c) Such other territories as may be acquired.
Key Provisions: 'Union of States' was preferred by Dr. Ambedkar over 'Federation of States' because: (1) Indian federation is not the result of an agreement among states; (2) States have no right to secede from the federation. Hence, India is an indestructible union of destructible states.
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: 'Territory of India' is a wider expression than 'Union of India'. Union includes only States sharing federal power; Territory includes States, UTs, and acquired territories.

flashcard 2:
frontside:
Article 2 - PART I - THE UNION AND ITS TERRITORY
Prompt: What does Article 2 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Admission or establishment of new States
Status: In Force
Mandate: Empowers Parliament by law to admit into the Union, or establish, new States on such terms and conditions as it thinks fit.
Key Provisions: Relates to admission or establishment of new states that are NOT part of the existing territory of India (e.g. admission of Sikkim initially or acquiring foreign territory and creating a state).
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Article 2 relates to external territories becoming new States. Article 3 relates to internal readjustment/reorganisation of existing Indian States.

flashcard 3:
frontside:
Article 2A - PART I - THE UNION AND ITS TERRITORY
Prompt: What does Article 2A provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Sikkim to be associated with the Union - [Omitted]
Status: [Omitted / Repealed]
Mandate: Inserted by 35th Amendment Act, 1974 to give Sikkim the unique status of an 'Associate State' with representation in Parliament.
Key Provisions: Omitted by the 36th Constitutional Amendment Act, 1975, which made Sikkim a full-fledged 22nd State of the Indian Union and inserted Article 371F.
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Associate State status was an experiment lasting only ~1 year (1974-1975). Sikkim is now a full state under the First Schedule.

flashcard 4:
frontside:
Article 3 - PART I - THE UNION AND ITS TERRITORY
Prompt: What does Article 3 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Formation of new States and alteration of areas, boundaries or names of existing States
Status: In Force
Mandate: Parliament may by law: (a) form a new State by separation, uniting states, or uniting parts; (b) increase area of any State; (c) diminish area of any State; (d) alter boundaries of any State; (e) alter name of any State.
Key Provisions: Mandatory procedure: (1) Bill introduced ONLY on recommendation of President; (2) President refers bill to state legislature concerned for expressing views within specified time. Parliament is NOT bound by state legislature's views.
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Ceding Indian territory to a foreign nation CANNOT be done under Article 3; it requires a Constitutional Amendment under Article 368 (Berubari Union case, 1960). Settlement of boundary disputes without cession, however, can be done by executive action (1969 SC ruling).

flashcard 5:
frontside:
Article 4 - PART I - THE UNION AND ITS TERRITORY
Prompt: What does Article 4 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Laws made under articles 2 and 3 to provide for the amendment of the First and the Fourth Schedules and supplemental, incidental and consequential matters
Status: In Force
Mandate: Laws passed under Article 2 or 3 for reorganisation of states and amending First/Fourth Schedules are NOT deemed to be amendments of the Constitution under Article 368.
Key Provisions: Such laws can be passed by Parliament by a simple majority and ordinary legislative process.
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: State reorganisation bills require simple majority, not special majority under Art 368.

## PART II - CITIZENSHIP

flashcard 6:
frontside:
Article 5 - PART II - CITIZENSHIP
Prompt: What does Article 5 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Citizenship at the commencement of the Constitution
Status: In Force
Mandate: Conferred citizenship on 26 January 1950 to every person who had domicile in India and: (a) was born in India; OR (b) either of whose parents was born in India; OR (c) ordinarily resident in India for >= 5 years immediately preceding commencement.
Key Provisions: Requires two elements: (1) Domicile in India (permanent home) + (2) Any one of the three conditions (birth, parents' birth, or 5-year ordinary residence).
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Domicile alone does not confer citizenship under Art 5; it must be coupled with birth, parentage, or 5-year residence.

flashcard 7:
frontside:
Article 6 - PART II - CITIZENSHIP
Prompt: What does Article 6 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Rights of citizenship of certain persons who have migrated to India from Pakistan
Status: In Force
Mandate: Dealt with citizenship of migrants from Pakistan before/after 19 July 1948 (permit system introduction date).
Key Provisions: If migrated before 19 July 1948: citizen if ordinarily resident since migration. If migrated on or after 19 July 1948: must register after residing for at least 6 months before applying.
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: The cutoff date in Art 6 is 19 July 1948, not 15 August 1947.

flashcard 8:
frontside:
Article 7 - PART II - CITIZENSHIP
Prompt: What does Article 7 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Rights of citizenship of certain migrants to Pakistan
Status: In Force
Mandate: A person who migrated to Pakistan from India after 1 March 1947 ceased to be an Indian citizen.
Key Provisions: Exception: If such a person returned to India under a permit for resettlement or permanent return, they could register as a citizen after 6 months residence.
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Art 7 overrides Art 5 and 6 ('Notwithstanding anything in articles 5 and 6').

flashcard 9:
frontside:
Article 8 - PART II - CITIZENSHIP
Prompt: What does Article 8 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Rights of citizenship of certain persons of Indian origin residing outside India
Status: In Force
Mandate: Any person who, or either of whose parents or grandparents was born in undivided India (as in 1935 Act), residing outside undivided India, can register as an Indian citizen with the diplomatic/consular representative of India.
Key Provisions: Created specifically to cover overseas Indians (diaspora) who wished to acquire Indian citizenship at commencement.
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Registration must be done before diplomatic or consular representative of India in the country where resident.

flashcard 10:
frontside:
Article 9 - PART II - CITIZENSHIP
Prompt: What does Article 9 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Persons voluntarily acquiring citizenship of a foreign State not to be citizens
Status: In Force
Mandate: No person shall be a citizen of India if they have voluntarily acquired the citizenship of any foreign State.
Key Provisions: Constitutional basis of India's single citizenship principle. The Constitution does NOT allow dual citizenship.
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Overseas Citizen of India (OCI) card is a statutory status under Citizenship Act 1955, NOT dual constitutional citizenship.

flashcard 11:
frontside:
Article 10 - PART II - CITIZENSHIP
Prompt: What does Article 10 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Continuance of the rights of citizenship
Status: In Force
Mandate: Every person who is or is deemed to be a citizen under Articles 5-9 shall continue to be a citizen, subject to any law made by Parliament.
Key Provisions: Ensures citizenship cannot be arbitrarily taken away by executive fiat; can only be regulated or terminated by law made by Parliament.
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Citizenship rights are subject to parliamentary legislation, not executive discretion.

flashcard 12:
frontside:
Article 11 - PART II - CITIZENSHIP
Prompt: What does Article 11 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Parliament to regulate the right of citizenship by law
Status: In Force
Mandate: Grants plenary power to Parliament to make any provision regarding acquisition and termination of citizenship and all matters relating thereto.
Key Provisions: Parliament enacted the Citizenship Act, 1955 under this Article, providing 5 modes of acquisition (Birth, Descent, Registration, Naturalisation, Incorporation of Territory) and 3 modes of loss (Renunciation, Termination, Deprivation).
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Articles 5-10 only dealt with citizenship at the commencement (26 Jan 1950). All post-1950 citizenship matters are governed by Parliamentary statute under Art 11.

## PART III - FUNDAMENTAL RIGHTS

flashcard 13:
frontside:
Article 12 - PART III - FUNDAMENTAL RIGHTS
Prompt: What does Article 12 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Definition
Status: In Force
Mandate: Defines 'State' for Part III to include: (1) Government and Parliament of India; (2) Government and Legislature of each of the States; (3) All local authorities (municipalities, panchayats, district boards); (4) Other authorities within India or under control of GoI.
Key Provisions: 'Other authorities' expanded by Supreme Court (Ajay Hasia, RD Shetty cases) to include bodies financially, functionally, or administratively dominated by government (e.g. LIC, ONGC, SAIL, statutory corporations). Judiciary in its administrative capacity is State; purely judicial acts are not.
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: NCERT and BCCI have been held NOT to be 'State' under Art 12 because they lack deep and pervasive state control or public/statutory monopoly.

flashcard 14:
frontside:
Article 13 - PART III - FUNDAMENTAL RIGHTS
Prompt: What does Article 13 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Laws inconsistent with or in derogation of the fundamental rights. Right to Equality
Status: In Force
Mandate: Constitutional touchstone of judicial review: (1) Pre-constitutional laws inconsistent with FRs are void to the extent of inconsistency; (2) State shall not make any law taking away/abridging FRs, and any law made in contravention shall be void.
Key Provisions: Doctrines flowing from Art 13: Doctrine of Severability, Doctrine of Eclipse (applies to pre-constitutional laws against citizens), and Doctrine of Waiver (citizens cannot waive Fundamental Rights - Behram Khurshed, Basheshar Nath cases).
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Does 'law' under Art 13 include Constitutional Amendments under Art 368? Shankari Prasad (1951) & Sajjan Singh (1965) said NO; Golak Nath (1967) said YES; 24th Amdt added Art 13(4) saying Art 13 does not apply to Art 368; Kesavananda Bharati (1973) upheld 13(4) but created the Basic Structure doctrine.

flashcard 15:
frontside:
Article 14 - PART III - FUNDAMENTAL RIGHTS
Prompt: What does Article 14 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Equality before law
Status: In Force
Mandate: The State shall not deny to any person equality before the law or the equal protection of the laws within the territory of India.
Key Provisions: Two concepts: (1) 'Equality before law' (British origin, negative concept - absence of special privilege); (2) 'Equal protection of laws' (US origin, positive concept - equal treatment in equal circumstances). Permits reasonable classification (intelligible differentia + rational nexus). EP Royappa (1974) introduced the 'new doctrine': equality is antithetic to arbitrariness.
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Available to 'any person' (citizens, foreigners, legal persons/corporations). Exceptions: President & Governors (Art 361), foreign diplomats/sovereigns, MP/MLA parliamentary privileges (Art 105/194).

flashcard 16:
frontside:
Article 15 - PART III - FUNDAMENTAL RIGHTS
Prompt: What does Article 15 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Prohibition of discrimination on grounds of religion, race, caste, sex or place of birth
Status: In Force
Mandate: 15(1): State shall not discriminate against any citizen on grounds ONLY of religion, race, caste, sex, place of birth or any of them. 15(2): Prohibits private discrimination in access to shops, public restaurants, hotels, public wells, tanks, roads.
Key Provisions: Enabling clauses: 15(3) special provisions for women and children; 15(4) (1st Amdt 1951) advancement of socially and educationally backward classes (SEBCs) or SCs/STs; 15(5) (93rd Amdt 2005) reservation in educational institutions including private unaided (except minority institutions); 15(6) (103rd Amdt 2019) up to 10% EWS reservation in educational institutions.
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: 15(1) applies ONLY to citizens. Note the operative word 'ONLY' - discrimination based on religion/sex PLUS other factors (e.g. physical suitability) is permissible.

flashcard 17:
frontside:
Article 16 - PART III - FUNDAMENTAL RIGHTS
Prompt: What does Article 16 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Equality of opportunity in matters of public employment
Status: In Force
Mandate: 16(1): Equality of opportunity for all citizens in employment under State. 16(2): No citizen shall be discriminated against or ineligible on grounds ONLY of religion, race, caste, sex, descent, place of birth, residence.
Key Provisions: Exceptions: 16(3) Parliament can prescribe residence requirement in a State/UT; 16(4) reservation for backward classes not adequately represented; 16(4A) (77th/85th Amdt) reservation in promotion with consequential seniority for SCs/STs; 16(4B) (81st Amdt) carry-forward rule exceeding 50% for unfilled reserved vacancies; 16(6) (103rd Amdt) 10% EWS reservation.
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Art 16(2) includes 'descent' and 'residence' which are NOT listed in Art 15(1). Only Parliament (not State legislatures) can prescribe residence as a condition under Art 16(3).

flashcard 18:
frontside:
Article 17 - PART III - FUNDAMENTAL RIGHTS
Prompt: What does Article 17 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Abolition of Untouchability
Status: In Force
Mandate: 'Untouchability' is abolished and its practice in any form is forbidden. The enforcement of any disability arising out of 'Untouchability' shall be an offence punishable in accordance with law.
Key Provisions: Absolute right (no exceptions). Operates against both State and private individuals. Parliament enacted Protection of Civil Rights Act, 1955 (originally Untouchability Offences Act) and SC/ST (Prevention of Atrocities) Act, 1989.
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: 'Untouchability' is NOT defined anywhere in the Constitution or the Protection of Civil Rights Act. Mysore HC (Devrajiah case) held it refers to historical social practice based on caste disability, not social boycott.

flashcard 19:
frontside:
Article 18 - PART III - FUNDAMENTAL RIGHTS
Prompt: What does Article 18 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Abolition of titles. Right to Freedom
Status: In Force
Mandate: 18(1): No title, not being a military or academic distinction, shall be conferred by State. 18(2): No citizen shall accept title from foreign State. 18(3)-(4): Non-citizens holding office of profit under State need President's consent to accept foreign titles/presents.
Key Provisions: National awards (Bharat Ratna, Padma awards instituted in 1954) were upheld by SC in Balaji Raghavan case (1996) as decorations, not hereditary titles of nobility. However, they CANNOT be used as suffixes or prefixes to the name (doing so forfeits the award).
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Article 18 is merely directory; it does not prescribe any criminal penalty for accepting a prohibited title. Parliament has not enacted penal law under Art 35 for Art 18.

flashcard 20:
frontside:
Article 19 - PART III - FUNDAMENTAL RIGHTS
Prompt: What does Article 19 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Protection of certain rights regarding freedom of speech, etc
Status: In Force
Mandate: Guarantees 6 democratic freedoms to citizens: 19(1)(a) speech & expression; 19(1)(b) assemble peaceably and without arms; 19(1)(c) form associations/unions/co-operative societies; 19(1)(d) move freely throughout territory of India; 19(1)(e) reside and settle in any part of India; 19(1)(g) practise any profession, trade or business. [19(1)(f) property omitted by 44th Amdt].
Key Provisions: Freedoms are NOT absolute; subject to reasonable restrictions: 19(2) speech: 8 grounds (sovereignty & integrity, security of State, friendly relations with foreign states, public order, decency/morality, contempt of court, defamation, incitement to an offence). 'Sovereignty and integrity' added by 16th Amdt (1963).
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Available ONLY to citizens, not aliens or corporations (STC case). Right to strike is NOT a fundamental right under 19(1)(c). Freedom of Press is an implicit right under 19(1)(a) (Indian Express, Sakal Papers), not expressly mentioned.

flashcard 21:
frontside:
Article 20 - PART III - FUNDAMENTAL RIGHTS
Prompt: What does Article 20 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Protection in respect of conviction for offences
Status: In Force
Mandate: Provides 3 critical criminal justice protections to citizens and foreigners: 20(1) Protection against ex-post facto laws (criminal laws cannot be retroactive; civil laws can); 20(2) Protection against Double Jeopardy (no person prosecuted and punished twice for same offence); 20(3) Protection against Self-Incrimination (no accused compelled to be a witness against himself).
Key Provisions: 44th Amdt 1978: Article 20 CANNOT be suspended even during a National Emergency under Article 359. Selvi v State of Karnataka (2010): involuntary narco-analysis, polygraph, and brain-mapping violate Art 20(3).
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Double Jeopardy under 20(2) protects only against prosecution before a court of law/judicial tribunal; does NOT bar departmental or administrative punishment following criminal trial (or vice-versa).

flashcard 22:
frontside:
Article 21 - PART III - FUNDAMENTAL RIGHTS
Prompt: What does Article 21 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Protection of life and personal liberty
Status: In Force
Mandate: No person shall be deprived of his life or personal liberty except according to procedure established by law.
Key Provisions: AK Gopalan (1950) took narrow literal view. Maneka Gandhi (1978) overruled it: procedure must be 'just, fair and reasonable' (incorporating American substantive 'due process'). Art 21 expanded to include: right to live with human dignity, clean environment (MC Mehta), privacy (Puttaswamy 2017), livelihood (Olga Tellis), speedy trial (Hussainara Khatoon), legal aid, medical care (Paramanand Katara).
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Cannot be suspended during National Emergency (44th Amdt). Applies to citizens AND non-citizens.

flashcard 23:
frontside:
Article 21A - PART III - FUNDAMENTAL RIGHTS
Prompt: What does Article 21A provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Right to education
Status: In Force
Mandate: The State shall provide free and compulsory education to all children of the age of six to fourteen years in such manner as the State may, by law, determine.
Key Provisions: Inserted by the 86th Constitutional Amendment Act, 2002. Parliament enacted the Right of Children to Free and Compulsory Education (RTE) Act, 2009, operationalised on 1 April 2010.
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Applies only to children aged 6 to 14 years. Early childhood care below 6 years remains in DPSP (Art 45 amended by 86th Amdt).

flashcard 24:
frontside:
Article 22 - PART III - FUNDAMENTAL RIGHTS
Prompt: What does Article 22 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Protection against arrest and detention in certain cases. Right against Exploitation
Status: In Force
Mandate: Deals with two types of detention: (A) Punitive detention (ordinary criminal law): right to be informed of grounds, right to consult/defended by legal practitioner, right to be produced before magistrate within 24 hours (excluding journey time). (B) Preventive detention: max detention without Advisory Board review is 3 months.
Key Provisions: Preventive detention safeguards: Detenu must be informed of grounds (except facts against public interest) and given earliest opportunity of making representation. Advisory Board consists of persons qualified to be High Court judges.
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Punitive safeguards (production in 24 hours, lawyer) do NOT apply to enemy aliens OR persons arrested under preventive detention law. India is unique in giving preventive detention constitutional sanction in peacetime.

flashcard 25:
frontside:
Article 23 - PART III - FUNDAMENTAL RIGHTS
Prompt: What does Article 23 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Prohibition of traffic in human beings and forced labour
Status: In Force
Mandate: 23(1): Traffic in human beings and begar and other similar forms of forced labour are prohibited and any contravention shall be an offence punishable in accordance with law.
Key Provisions: Operates against State AND private individuals. PUDR v Union of India (Asiad Workers case, 1982): paying less than minimum wage amounts to 'forced labour' violating Art 23. 23(2) allows State to impose compulsory service for public purposes (e.g. military/social service) without discrimination on religion, race, caste or class.
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Compulsory public service under 23(2) permits discrimination on ground of 'sex' (e.g. military draft for men only), as sex is omitted from the non-discrimination list in 23(2).

flashcard 26:
frontside:
Article 24 - PART III - FUNDAMENTAL RIGHTS
Prompt: What does Article 24 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Prohibition of employment of children in factories, etc. Right to Freedom of Religion
Status: In Force
Mandate: No child below the age of fourteen years shall be employed to work in any factory or mine or engaged in any other hazardous employment.
Key Provisions: Absolute prohibition in hazardous employment. Child and Adolescent Labour (Prohibition and Regulation) Act, 1986 (amended in 2016) banned employment of children (<14) in all occupations/processes, with narrow family enterprise exception outside school hours.
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Art 24 originally banned children only in 'hazardous' employment; the complete ban in all commercial work is a statutory development under the 2016 Act.

flashcard 27:
frontside:
Article 25 - PART III - FUNDAMENTAL RIGHTS
Prompt: What does Article 25 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Freedom of conscience and free profession, practice and propagation of religion
Status: In Force
Mandate: 25(1): Subject to public order, morality and health and other Part III provisions, all persons are equally entitled to freedom of conscience and right freely to profess, practise and propagate religion.
Key Provisions: Propagation does NOT include the right to forcibly convert another person (Rev Stanislaus case 1977). 25(2): State can regulate secular economic/financial/political activities associated with religion, and provide for social welfare/reform or opening Hindu religious institutions of public character to all classes/sections of Hindus.
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Explanation II specifies that the reference to Hindus includes Sikhs, Jains and Buddhists. Wearing and carrying of Kirpans is deemed included in the profession of the Sikh religion (Explanation I).

flashcard 28:
frontside:
Article 26 - PART III - FUNDAMENTAL RIGHTS
Prompt: What does Article 26 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Freedom to manage religious affairs
Status: In Force
Mandate: Subject to public order, morality and health, every religious denomination or section thereof has the right: (a) establish and maintain institutions for religious and charitable purposes; (b) manage its own affairs in matters of religion; (c) own and acquire property; (d) administer such property in accordance with law.
Key Provisions: Protects collective religious freedom of denominations (e.g. Ramakrishna Mission, Anand Marg). 'Matters of religion' includes essential religious practices (Doctrine of Essentiality - Shirur Mutt case 1954).
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Art 25 is subject to 'all other fundamental rights'; Art 26 is subject ONLY to public order, morality, and health (does not mention other FRs). Also, right to administer property is 'in accordance with law' (State can regulate administration, unlike religious rituals).

flashcard 29:
frontside:
Article 27 - PART III - FUNDAMENTAL RIGHTS
Prompt: What does Article 27 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Freedom as to payment of taxes for promotion of any particular religion
Status: In Force
Mandate: No person shall be compelled to pay any taxes, the proceeds of which are specifically appropriated in payment of expenses for the promotion or maintenance of any particular religion or religious denomination.
Key Provisions: Secular principle: State funds cannot be used to promote one specific religion. Prohibits levy of a TAX, but does NOT prohibit the levy of a FEE to provide secular services or regulation for pilgrims (e.g. pilgrimage regulation fee, shrine board fee).
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Prohibits taxes, not fees. Taxes collected for general public revenues can be used for cultural/secular infrastructure of all communities.

flashcard 30:
frontside:
Article 28 - PART III - FUNDAMENTAL RIGHTS
Prompt: What does Article 28 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Freedom as to attendance at religious instruction or religious worship in certain educational institutions. Cultural and Educational Rights
Status: In Force
Mandate: 28(1): No religious instruction shall be provided in any educational institution wholly maintained out of State funds. 28(2): Exception: institutions administered by State but established under an endowment/trust requiring religious instruction.
Key Provisions: Four categories of institutions: (1) Wholly state-maintained: religious instruction completely prohibited; (2) Administered by State under trust: permitted; (3) Recognised by State: voluntary (consent of student/guardian required); (4) Receiving state aid: voluntary (consent required).
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Religious instruction is banned only in institutions 'wholly maintained out of State funds'. In state-aided or recognised institutions, it is permitted on a voluntary basis.

flashcard 31:
frontside:
Article 29 - PART III - FUNDAMENTAL RIGHTS
Prompt: What does Article 29 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Protection of interests of minorities
Status: In Force
Mandate: 29(1): Any section of citizens residing in India having a distinct language, script or culture of its own has the right to conserve the same. 29(2): No citizen shall be denied admission into any educational institution maintained by State or receiving aid on grounds ONLY of religion, race, caste, language.
Key Provisions: Scope: 29(1) uses 'any section of citizens' - Supreme Court held it protects BOTH minority and majority linguistic/cultural groups. 29(2) is an individual citizen right against discrimination in state-aided colleges.
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Article 29 heading refers to 'minorities', but text of 29(1) protects 'any section of the citizens' (including majority groups with distinct dialect/culture).

flashcard 32:
frontside:
Article 30 - PART III - FUNDAMENTAL RIGHTS
Prompt: What does Article 30 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Right of minorities to establish and administer educational institutions
Status: In Force
Mandate: 30(1): All minorities, whether based on religion or language, shall have the right to establish and administer educational institutions of their choice. 30(1A) (44th Amdt): compulsory acquisition of minority institution property requires compensation that does not abridge this right. 30(2): State shall not discriminate in giving aid.
Key Provisions: Available ONLY to religious and linguistic minorities (not cultural minorities). Landmark rulings: St. Stephen's (1992), TMA Pai Foundation (2002), PA Inamdar (2005) - minority institutions have administrative autonomy, but State can enforce academic standards, teacher qualifications, and syllabus.
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Art 30 rights are NOT absolute; regulatory measures for academic excellence, health, hygiene, and national security apply to minority institutions as well.

flashcard 33:
frontside:
Article 31 - PART III - FUNDAMENTAL RIGHTS
Prompt: What does Article 31 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Compulsory acquisition of property - [Omitted].] Saving of Certain Laws
Status: [Omitted / Repealed]
Mandate: Originally guaranteed Right to Property as a Fundamental Right. Subject to extensive litigation and amendments (1st, 4th, 25th, 42nd).
Key Provisions: Completely repealed as a Fundamental Right by the 44th Constitutional Amendment Act, 1978. Re-inserted as a legal/constitutional right in Part XII under Article 300A.
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Property is still a constitutional right under Art 300A, but no longer a Fundamental Right (cannot approach SC directly under Art 32 for property infringement).

flashcard 34:
frontside:
Article 31A - PART III - FUNDAMENTAL RIGHTS
Prompt: What does Article 31A provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Saving of Laws providing for acquisition of estates, etc
Status: In Force
Mandate: Inserted by 1st Amendment Act, 1951 (and widened by 4th, 17th, 44th Amdt) to protect 5 categories of agrarian reform and economic management laws from challenge under Article 14 and 19.
Key Provisions: 5 categories: acquisition of estates/rights; taking over management of property; amalgamation of corporations; extinguishing/modifying rights of managing agents/shareholders; extinguishing/modifying mining leases.
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: State law under 31A is protected ONLY IF it was reserved for the President's consideration and received Presidential assent.

flashcard 35:
frontside:
Article 31B - PART III - FUNDAMENTAL RIGHTS
Prompt: What does Article 31B provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Validation of certain Acts and Regulations
Status: In Force
Mandate: Inserted by 1st Amendment, 1951: None of the Acts and Regulations specified in the Ninth Schedule shall be deemed void on ground of inconsistency with Fundamental Rights.
Key Provisions: Created a constitutional protective umbrella. Originally 13 Acts; now 284 Acts. Landmark judgment: I.R. Coelho v State of Tamil Nadu (2007) held that Ninth Schedule immunity is NOT absolute. Any Act added to 9th Schedule AFTER 24 April 1973 (Kesavananda date) can be challenged if it violates the Basic Structure of the Constitution.
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Article 31B is independent of Article 31A. An Act invalid under 31A can still be saved if placed in 9th Schedule under 31B, subject to Coelho basic structure review.

flashcard 36:
frontside:
Article 31C - PART III - FUNDAMENTAL RIGHTS
Prompt: What does Article 31C provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Saving of laws giving effect to certain directive principles
Status: In Force
Mandate: Inserted by 25th Amdt 1971: No law giving effect to policy of State towards securing Directive Principles in Article 39(b) and 39(c) shall be void for inconsistency with Article 14 or 19.
Key Provisions: Kesavananda Bharati (1973) upheld the protection for 39(b)-(c), but struck down the second part ('no law containing a declaration that it is for giving effect to such policy shall be called in question in any court'). 42nd Amdt tried to extend 31C protection to ALL DPSPs; Minerva Mills (1980) struck down that extension, restoring protection only for 39(b) and (c).
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Art 31C prioritises ONLY Art 39(b) and 39(c) over Art 14 and 19. It does NOT protect laws against other Fundamental Rights (e.g. Art 21).

flashcard 37:
frontside:
Article 31D - PART III - FUNDAMENTAL RIGHTS
Prompt: What does Article 31D provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Saving of laws in respect of anti-national activities - [Omitted].] Right to Constitutional Remedies
Status: [Omitted / Repealed]
Mandate: Inserted by 42nd Amendment Act, 1976 during the Emergency to shield laws against 'anti-national activities' from fundamental rights challenges.
Key Provisions: Omitted by the 43rd Constitutional Amendment Act, 1977 by the Janata Government.
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Article 31D was an Emergency-era insertion that survived only for ~1 year.

flashcard 38:
frontside:
Article 32 - PART III - FUNDAMENTAL RIGHTS
Prompt: What does Article 32 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Remedies for enforcement of rights conferred by this Part
Status: In Force
Mandate: 32(1): Right to move Supreme Court by appropriate proceedings for enforcement of FRs. 32(2): Supreme Court has power to issue directions, orders, or writs (Habeas Corpus, Mandamus, Prohibition, Quo Warranto, Certiorari).
Key Provisions: Dr. Ambedkar called Article 32 'the very soul of the Constitution and the very heart of it'. Article 32 is ITSELF a Fundamental Right (cannot be refused by SC, unlike HC's discretionary jurisdiction under Art 226). Basic structure of the Constitution (Fertilizer Corporation, L. Chandra Kumar).
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Article 32 can be invoked ONLY for the enforcement of Fundamental Rights (Part III), not ordinary legal rights. In contrast, Article 226 of High Courts can be invoked for FRs AND 'for any other purpose'.

flashcard 39:
frontside:
Article 32A - PART III - FUNDAMENTAL RIGHTS
Prompt: What does Article 32A provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Constitutional validity of State laws not to be considered in proceedings under article 32 - [Omitted]
Status: [Omitted / Repealed]
Mandate: Inserted by 42nd Amendment, 1976 to bar Supreme Court from considering validity of State laws under Art 32 unless Central laws were also questioned.
Key Provisions: Omitted by the 43rd Constitutional Amendment Act, 1977.
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Another 42nd Amendment restriction quickly repealed post-Emergency.

flashcard 40:
frontside:
Article 33 - PART III - FUNDAMENTAL RIGHTS
Prompt: What does Article 33 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Power of Parliament to modify the rights conferred by this Part in their application to Forces, etc
Status: In Force
Mandate: Empowers Parliament to determine by law to what extent Fundamental Rights shall be restricted or abrogated in their application to: (a) members of Armed Forces; (b) forces charged with public order (police); (c) intelligence/counter-intelligence personnel; (d) telecommunication systems for forces/intelligence.
Key Provisions: Objective: Ensure proper discharge of duties and maintenance of discipline. Laws enacted: Army Act, Navy Act, Air Force Act, Police Forces (Restriction of Rights) Act.
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Only PARLIAMENT (not State legislatures) has the power to make laws under Article 33. Such parliamentary laws cannot be challenged on ground of contravening Part III.

flashcard 41:
frontside:
Article 34 - PART III - FUNDAMENTAL RIGHTS
Prompt: What does Article 34 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Restriction on rights conferred by this Part while martial law is in force in any area
Status: In Force
Mandate: Empowers Parliament to indemnify any person in the service of the Union or a State for acts done in connection with the maintenance or restoration of order in any area where martial law was in force, and validate any sentence passed.
Key Provisions: Martial law means military rule invoked in extraordinary situations (war, invasion, rebellion). An Act of Indemnity passed by Parliament cannot be challenged in court on ground of violation of FRs.
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: 'Martial law' is NOT defined in the Constitution. Martial law is distinct from National Emergency under Art 352 (Martial law affects only fundamental rights and ordinary law courts; emergency affects federal structure and legislative powers).

flashcard 42:
frontside:
Article 35 - PART III - FUNDAMENTAL RIGHTS
Prompt: What does Article 35 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Legislation to give effect to the provisions of this Part
Status: In Force
Mandate: Vests exclusive power in Parliament (to the exclusion of State legislatures) to make laws: (1) prescribing residence under Art 16(3); (2) empowering subordinate courts to issue writs under Art 32(3); (3) restricting FRs of forces under Art 33; (4) indemnifying acts under martial law under Art 34; (5) prescribing punishment for untouchability (Art 17) and forced labour (Art 23).
Key Provisions: Ensures uniformity throughout India in the operation and penalties for violation of Fundamental Rights.
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: State legislatures CANNOT make penal laws for Articles 17 and 23, even though police and public order are State list subjects. That power belongs exclusively to Parliament under Art 35.

## PART IV - DIRECTIVE PRINCIPLES OF STATE POLICY

flashcard 43:
frontside:
Article 36 - PART IV - DIRECTIVE PRINCIPLES OF STATE POLICY
Prompt: What does Article 36 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Definition
Status: In Force
Mandate: States that unless the context otherwise requires, 'the State' in Part IV has the same meaning as defined in Article 12 of Part III.
Key Provisions: Includes Government and Parliament of India, State governments and legislatures, all local authorities, and statutory/other authorities.
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Directive Principles are directed against the same 'State' authorities that are bound by Fundamental Rights.

flashcard 44:
frontside:
Article 37 - PART IV - DIRECTIVE PRINCIPLES OF STATE POLICY
Prompt: What does Article 37 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Application of the principles contained in this Part
Status: In Force
Mandate: Declares that provisions contained in Part IV: (1) Shall NOT be enforceable by any court (non-justiciable); BUT (2) Are nevertheless fundamental in the governance of the country; and (3) It shall be the duty of the State to apply these principles in making laws.
Key Provisions: Creates moral and political obligation on the executive and legislature. Dr. Ambedkar stated that the government cannot ignore DPSPs because it will have to answer to the electorate at election time.
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Non-justiciability does not mean inferiority. Minerva Mills (1980): The Constitution is founded on the bedrock of the balance between Part III and Part IV.

flashcard 45:
frontside:
Article 38 - PART IV - DIRECTIVE PRINCIPLES OF STATE POLICY
Prompt: What does Article 38 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: State to secure a social order for the promotion of welfare of the people
Status: In Force
Mandate: 38(1): State shall strive to promote welfare of people by securing a social order permeated by justice - social, economic and political. 38(2) (added by 44th Amdt 1978): State shall minimise inequalities in income and eliminate inequalities in status, facilities and opportunities.
Key Provisions: Constitutional blueprint for a 'Welfare State'. Article 38(2) was the only DPSP added by the 44th Amendment Act, 1978.
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Note the amendment origin: 38(1) is original; 38(2) (inequalities in income/status) was added by 44th Amendment, 1978, NOT 42nd Amendment.

flashcard 46:
frontside:
Article 39 - PART IV - DIRECTIVE PRINCIPLES OF STATE POLICY
Prompt: What does Article 39 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Certain principles of policy to be followed by the State
Status: In Force
Mandate: Directs State policy towards securing: 39(a) Adequate means of livelihood for all citizens; 39(b) Distribution of ownership and control of material resources to best subserve common good; 39(c) Prevention of concentration of wealth and means of production; 39(d) Equal pay for equal work for both men and women; 39(e) Health and strength of workers and children not abused; 39(f) Children given opportunities to develop in healthy manner (amended by 42nd Amdt).
Key Provisions: Articles 39(b) and 39(c) enjoy special constitutional supremacy over Articles 14 and 19 under Article 31C (upheld in Kesavananda Bharati). 'Material resources' includes both private and public resources (Sanjeev Coke case).
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Equal pay for equal work (Art 39(d)) is a DPSP, NOT a distinct Fundamental Right, though enforceable when linked to Art 14 (Randhir Singh case).

flashcard 47:
frontside:
Article 39A - PART IV - DIRECTIVE PRINCIPLES OF STATE POLICY
Prompt: What does Article 39A provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Equal justice and free legal aid
Status: In Force
Mandate: State shall secure that the operation of the legal system promotes justice on a basis of equal opportunity, and shall provide free legal aid to ensure that opportunities for securing justice are not denied to any citizen by reason of economic or other disabilities.
Key Provisions: Inserted by 42nd Amendment Act, 1976. Led to enactment of Legal Services Authorities Act, 1987, creating NALSA, SALSA, and Lok Adalats.
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Right to free legal aid for an accused has been declared a Fundamental Right under Art 21 (Hussainara Khatoon, Khatri cases), derived from Art 39A.

flashcard 48:
frontside:
Article 40 - PART IV - DIRECTIVE PRINCIPLES OF STATE POLICY
Prompt: What does Article 40 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Organisation of village panchayats
Status: In Force
Mandate: State shall take steps to organise village panchayats and endow them with such powers and authority as may be necessary to enable them to function as units of self-government.
Key Provisions: Gandhian directive principle. Given constitutional status and binding institutional framework through the 73rd Constitutional Amendment Act, 1992 (Part IX, Schedule 11).
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Article 40 was merely enabling/directory; mandatory 3-tier panchayats with regular elections, reservation and finance commissions came through Part IX (Art 243-243O).

flashcard 49:
frontside:
Article 41 - PART IV - DIRECTIVE PRINCIPLES OF STATE POLICY
Prompt: What does Article 41 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Right to work, to education and to public assistance in certain cases
Status: In Force
Mandate: State shall, within limits of its economic capacity and development, make effective provision for securing right to work, education and public assistance in cases of unemployment, old age, sickness and disablement.
Key Provisions: Socialistic principle. Constitutional basis for schemes like MGNREGA (right to work), National Social Assistance Programme (NSAP pensions), and disability relief.
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Expressly conditioned upon 'the limits of economic capacity and development of the State'.

flashcard 50:
frontside:
Article 42 - PART IV - DIRECTIVE PRINCIPLES OF STATE POLICY
Prompt: What does Article 42 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Provision for just and humane conditions of work and maternity relief
Status: In Force
Mandate: State shall make provision for securing just and humane conditions of work and for maternity relief.
Key Provisions: Constitutional basis for the Maternity Benefit Act, 1961 (amended 2017 to provide 26 weeks paid leave), Factories Act safety norms, and occupational safety codes.
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Directly relevant to GS-II and GS-I women empowerment and labour welfare questions.

flashcard 51:
frontside:
Article 43 - PART IV - DIRECTIVE PRINCIPLES OF STATE POLICY
Prompt: What does Article 43 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Living wage, etc., for workers
Status: In Force
Mandate: State shall endeavour to secure to all workers a living wage, conditions of work ensuring a decent standard of life, full enjoyment of leisure and social and cultural opportunities, and promote cottage industries on an individual or co-operative basis in rural areas.
Key Provisions: Three wage concepts: (1) Minimum wage (bare subsistence); (2) Fair wage (above minimum, below living); (3) Living wage (highest ideal: food, clothing, housing, education, insurance). Art 43 aims for living wage.
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Promoting cottage industries is Gandhian; living wage and worker conditions are Socialistic.

flashcard 52:
frontside:
Article 43A - PART IV - DIRECTIVE PRINCIPLES OF STATE POLICY
Prompt: What does Article 43A provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Participation of workers in management of Industries
Status: In Force
Mandate: State shall take steps, by suitable legislation or in any other way, to secure participation of workers in the management of undertakings, establishments or other organisations engaged in any industry.
Key Provisions: Inserted by the 42nd Constitutional Amendment Act, 1976. Socialistic principle promoting industrial democracy.
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Added by 42nd Amendment, 1976 along with 39A, 48A, and 51A.

flashcard 53:
frontside:
Article 43B - PART IV - DIRECTIVE PRINCIPLES OF STATE POLICY
Prompt: What does Article 43B provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Promotion of co-operative societies
Status: In Force
Mandate: State shall endeavour to promote voluntary formation, autonomous functioning, democratic control and professional management of co-operative societies.
Key Provisions: Inserted by the 97th Constitutional Amendment Act, 2011. The same amendment added right to form co-operatives in Art 19(1)(c) and inserted Part IXB.
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Supreme Court in Union of India v Rajendra Shah (2021) struck down Part IXB for state co-operatives for lack of state ratification under Art 368(2), but UPHELD Article 43B as a valid DPSP.

flashcard 54:
frontside:
Article 44 - PART IV - DIRECTIVE PRINCIPLES OF STATE POLICY
Prompt: What does Article 44 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Uniform civil code for the citizens
Status: In Force
Mandate: The State shall endeavour to secure for the citizens a Uniform Civil Code throughout the territory of India.
Key Provisions: Liberal-intellectual principle. Aims to replace diverse personal laws based on religious scriptures/customs with a common set of laws governing marriage, divorce, maintenance, inheritance, adoption, and succession. Goa is the only State with a common civil code (Portuguese Civil Code 1867); Uttarakhand enacted UCC in 2024. Supreme Court repeatedly urged UCC (Shah Bano 1985, Sarla Mudgal 1995, Shayara Bano 2017).
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Art 44 is an endeavour directed to the State, not a self-executing mandate. It deals with civil personal laws, NOT criminal laws (criminal laws and IPC/BNS are already uniform).

flashcard 55:
frontside:
Article 45 - PART IV - DIRECTIVE PRINCIPLES OF STATE POLICY
Prompt: What does Article 45 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Provision for early childhood care and education to children below the age of six years
Status: In Force
Mandate: State shall endeavour to provide early childhood care and education for all children until they complete the age of six years.
Key Provisions: Originally mandated free and compulsory education up to age 14. When 86th Amdt (2002) shifted age 6-14 to Article 21A as a Fundamental Right, it substituted Article 45 to focus on early childhood care below 6 years (Anganwadis, ICDS).
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Pre-school and under-6 care is in Art 45 (DPSP); 6-14 schooling is in Art 21A (FR); parent's duty is in Art 51A(k) (FD).

flashcard 56:
frontside:
Article 46 - PART IV - DIRECTIVE PRINCIPLES OF STATE POLICY
Prompt: What does Article 46 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Promotion of educational and economic interests of Scheduled Castes, Scheduled Tribes and other weaker sections
Status: In Force
Mandate: State shall promote with special care the educational and economic interests of the weaker sections of the people, and in particular, of the Scheduled Castes and the Scheduled Tribes, and shall protect them from social injustice and all forms of exploitation.
Key Provisions: Constitutional foundation for scholarship schemes, tribal development programmes, and statutory safeguards for SCs and STs.
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Mentioned as the constitutional objective supporting special reservation provisions under Articles 15(4) and 16(4).

flashcard 57:
frontside:
Article 47 - PART IV - DIRECTIVE PRINCIPLES OF STATE POLICY
Prompt: What does Article 47 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Duty of the State to raise the level of nutrition and the standard of living and to improve public health
Status: In Force
Mandate: State shall regard the raising of the level of nutrition and standard of living of its people and the improvement of public health as among its primary duties. State shall endeavour to bring about prohibition of the consumption (except for medicinal purposes) of intoxicating drinks and drugs injurious to health.
Key Provisions: Constitutional basis for liquor prohibition policies in States (e.g. Gujarat, Bihar) and national health/nutrition programmes (POSHAN Abhiyaan, NFSA, Ayushman Bharat).
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Right to health is derived as an integral part of Right to Life under Art 21, reading it with Art 47.

flashcard 58:
frontside:
Article 48 - PART IV - DIRECTIVE PRINCIPLES OF STATE POLICY
Prompt: What does Article 48 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Organisation of agriculture and animal husbandry
Status: In Force
Mandate: State shall endeavour to organise agriculture and animal husbandry on modern and scientific lines, and shall, in particular, take steps for preserving and improving the breeds, and prohibiting the slaughter of cows and calves and other milch and draught cattle.
Key Provisions: Gandhian and liberal-intellectual synthesis. Supreme Court (Hanif Qureshi 1958, Mirzapur Moti Kureshi 2005) upheld total ban on slaughter of cows and calves of all ages as constitutional under Art 48.
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Prohibition of cow slaughter is under Art 48 (Agriculture/Animal Husbandry), NOT under freedom of religion (Art 25).

flashcard 59:
frontside:
Article 48A - PART IV - DIRECTIVE PRINCIPLES OF STATE POLICY
Prompt: What does Article 48A provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Protection and improvement of environment and safeguarding of forests and wild life
Status: In Force
Mandate: The State shall endeavour to protect and improve the environment and to safeguard the forests and wild life of the country.
Key Provisions: Inserted by 42nd Amendment Act, 1976. Along with 51A(g), forms the constitutional charter of environmental law in India (Wild Life Protection Act, Forest Conservation Act, Environment Protection Act 1986).
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: State's duty to protect environment is in Art 48A; Citizen's duty to protect environment is in Art 51A(g). Both inserted by 42nd Amendment, 1976.

flashcard 60:
frontside:
Article 49 - PART IV - DIRECTIVE PRINCIPLES OF STATE POLICY
Prompt: What does Article 49 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Protection of monuments and places and objects of national importance
Status: In Force
Mandate: It shall be the obligation of the State to protect every monument or place or object of artistic or historic interest, declared by or under law made by Parliament to be of national importance, from spoliation, disfigurement, destruction, removal, disposal or export.
Key Provisions: Enforced through the Ancient Monuments and Archaeological Sites and Remains Act, 1958 and the Archaeological Survey of India (ASI).
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Applies to monuments declared by or under law made by PARLIAMENT to be of national importance, not purely local monuments.

flashcard 61:
frontside:
Article 50 - PART IV - DIRECTIVE PRINCIPLES OF STATE POLICY
Prompt: What does Article 50 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Separation of judiciary from executive
Status: In Force
Mandate: The State shall take steps to separate the judiciary from the executive in the public services of the State.
Key Provisions: Achieved structurally via the Code of Criminal Procedure (CrPC), 1973, which separated Judicial Magistrates (subordinate to High Court) from Executive Magistrates (subordinate to State Government/DM).
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Art 50 refers to separation of judiciary from executive in public services of the State; it does NOT mean a rigid presidential-style separation of powers between executive and legislative branches.

flashcard 62:
frontside:
Article 51 - PART IV - DIRECTIVE PRINCIPLES OF STATE POLICY
Prompt: What does Article 51 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Promotion of international peace and security
Status: In Force
Mandate: State shall endeavour to: (a) promote international peace and security; (b) maintain just and honourable relations between nations; (c) foster respect for international law and treaty obligations; (d) encourage settlement of international disputes by arbitration.
Key Provisions: Constitutional basis of Indian foreign policy (Non-Alignment, Panchsheel, UN peacekeeping, adherence to customary international law unless overridden by municipal statute).
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: International treaties signed by India are NOT self-executing into domestic law; Parliament must enact domestic enabling legislation under Article 253 to implement treaty obligations.

## PART IVA - FUNDAMENTAL DUTIES

flashcard 63:
frontside:
Article 51A - PART IVA - FUNDAMENTAL DUTIES
Prompt: What does Article 51A provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Fundamental duties
Status: In Force
Mandate: Prescribes 11 Fundamental Duties for every citizen of India: (a) abide by Constitution, respect National Flag and Anthem; (b) cherish noble ideals of national struggle for freedom; (c) uphold and protect sovereignty, unity and integrity of India; (d) defend country and render national service; (e) promote harmony, spirit of common brotherhood, renounce practices derogatory to dignity of women; (f) value and preserve rich heritage of composite culture; (g) protect and improve natural environment (forests, lakes, rivers, wildlife) and compassion for living creatures; (h) develop scientific temper, humanism, spirit of inquiry and reform; (i) safeguard public property and abjure violence; (j) strive towards excellence in all spheres; (k) parent/guardian to provide education opportunities to child aged 6-14.
Key Provisions: Inserted by 42nd Amendment Act, 1976 on recommendation of Swaran Singh Committee (which recommended 8 duties; 42nd Amdt added 10). Duty (k) added by 86th Amendment Act, 2002. Non-justiciable (no direct writ enforcement), but Verma Committee (1999) identified existing legal provisions enforcing several duties (Flag Code, Prevention of Insults to National Honour Act, IPC, Wildlife Act).
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Duties apply ONLY to citizens (not aliens). Swaran Singh Committee recommended duty to pay taxes and penal sanctions for non-compliance, but these were REJECTED and not included in the Constitution.

## PART V - THE UNION

flashcard 64:
frontside:
Article 52 - PART V - THE UNION | CHAPTER I. - THE EXECUTIVE
Prompt: What does Article 52 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: The President of India
Status: In Force
Mandate: Establishes that there shall be a President of India, who is the Head of State, first citizen, and formal executive head.
Key Provisions: Office of President can never be vacant; continuity is constitutionally mandatory.
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: President is Head of State (titular/de jure executive); PM is Head of Government (real/de facto executive).

flashcard 65:
frontside:
Article 53 - PART V - THE UNION | CHAPTER I. - THE EXECUTIVE
Prompt: What does Article 53 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Executive power of the Union
Status: In Force
Mandate: Executive power of the Union is vested in the President, exercised directly or through subordinate officers. Supreme command of Defence Forces is vested in President.
Key Provisions: All formal executive actions of GoI are taken in President's name (Art 77).
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Military command power is regulated by parliamentary law, not personal presidential prerogative.

flashcard 66:
frontside:
Article 54 - PART V - THE UNION | CHAPTER I. - THE EXECUTIVE
Prompt: What does Article 54 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Election of President
Status: In Force
Mandate: President is elected by an Electoral College consisting of: (a) Elected MPs of both Houses; (b) Elected MLAs of all States (plus Delhi, Puducherry, and J&K UT).
Key Provisions: Only ELECTED members vote. Nominated MPs, nominated MLAs, and ALL MLCs (Legislative Councils) are excluded.
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Nominated MPs CANNOT vote in President's election, but CAN vote in President's impeachment (Art 61).

flashcard 67:
frontside:
Article 55 - PART V - THE UNION | CHAPTER I. - THE EXECUTIVE
Prompt: What does Article 55 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Manner of election of President
Status: In Force
Mandate: Manner of Presidential election: Proportional representation by single transferable vote (PR-STV) and secret ballot, maintaining federal parity.
Key Provisions: MLA vote value = (State Pop / Elected MLAs) x (1/1000). MP vote value = Total MLA votes / Total Elected MPs. 1971 census population frozen until 2026.
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Winning candidate must secure an absolute electoral quota (>50% of valid polled votes), not a simple plurality.

flashcard 68:
frontside:
Article 56 - PART V - THE UNION | CHAPTER I. - THE EXECUTIVE
Prompt: What does Article 56 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Term of office of President
Status: In Force
Mandate: Term of office of President is 5 years from entering office. Resignation addressed to Vice-President (who notifies Speaker LS).
Key Provisions: May be removed by impeachment under Art 61 for 'violation of the Constitution'. Continues until successor enters office.
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Outgoing President continues until successor takes oath; Vice-President does NOT take over on term expiry.

flashcard 69:
frontside:
Article 57 - PART V - THE UNION | CHAPTER I. - THE EXECUTIVE
Prompt: What does Article 57 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Eligibility for re-election
Status: In Force
Mandate: Eligibility for re-election: A person holding or who has held office as President is eligible for re-election.
Key Provisions: Indian Constitution places NO limit on the number of terms a President may serve (unlike US 22nd Amendment's 2-term cap).
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Dr. Rajendra Prasad is the only President to serve two full terms.

flashcard 70:
frontside:
Article 58 - PART V - THE UNION | CHAPTER I. - THE EXECUTIVE
Prompt: What does Article 58 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Qualifications for election as President
Status: In Force
Mandate: Qualifications for election as President: Citizen of India, 35+ years of age, qualified for election as Lok Sabha MP, no office of profit.
Key Provisions: Offices of President, VP, Governor, and Union/State Ministers are not offices of profit.
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Naturalised citizen is fully eligible in India; US Constitution requires a 'natural-born citizen'.

flashcard 71:
frontside:
Article 59 - PART V - THE UNION | CHAPTER I. - THE EXECUTIVE
Prompt: What does Article 59 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Conditions of President - s office
Status: In Force
Mandate: Conditions of President's office: Cannot be an MP or MLA; entitled to official residence rent-free; emoluments fixed by law.
Key Provisions: Emoluments and allowances cannot be diminished during the President's term of office.
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: President's salary cannot be reduced even during a Financial Emergency under Article 360.

flashcard 72:
frontside:
Article 60 - PART V - THE UNION | CHAPTER I. - THE EXECUTIVE
Prompt: What does Article 60 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Oath or affirmation by the President
Status: In Force
Mandate: Oath administered by Chief Justice of India (or senior-most SC Judge). Swears to 'preserve, protect and defend the Constitution and the law'.
Key Provisions: Unique wording 'preserve, protect and defend' applies only to President and Governor (Art 159).
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: President's oath is in Article 60 itself, NOT in the Third Schedule.

flashcard 73:
frontside:
Article 61 - PART V - THE UNION | CHAPTER I. - THE EXECUTIVE
Prompt: What does Article 61 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Procedure for impeachment of the President
Status: In Force
Mandate: Impeachment procedure: Initiated in either House for 'violation of the Constitution'. 14 days notice signed by 1/4th members.
Key Provisions: Passed by a special majority of NOT LESS THAN 2/3RD OF TOTAL MEMBERSHIP of each House separately.
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Requires 2/3rd of TOTAL membership (strictest majority in Constitution). Nominated MPs vote; State MLAs do not.

flashcard 74:
frontside:
Article 62 - PART V - THE UNION | CHAPTER I. - THE EXECUTIVE
Prompt: What does Article 62 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Time of holding election to fill vacancy in the office of President and the term of office of person elected to fill casual vacancy
Status: In Force
Mandate: Election to fill term-expiry vacancy must be completed BEFORE expiry. Vacancy due to death/resignation/removal must be filled within 6 MONTHS.
Key Provisions: New President serves a full 5-year term from date of entering office.
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Vice-President acts as President only until new President is elected (within 6 months); does not serve remaining term.

flashcard 75:
frontside:
Article 63 - PART V - THE UNION | CHAPTER I. - THE EXECUTIVE
Prompt: What does Article 63 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: The Vice-President of India
Status: In Force
Mandate: There shall be a Vice-President of India, the second highest constitutional dignitary.
Key Provisions: Modelled on the American Vice-President; acts as ex officio Chairman of Rajya Sabha.
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Draws salary as Chairman of Rajya Sabha, not as Vice-President.

flashcard 76:
frontside:
Article 64 - PART V - THE UNION | CHAPTER I. - THE EXECUTIVE
Prompt: What does Article 64 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: The Vice-President to be ex officio Chairman of the Council of States
Status: In Force
Mandate: Vice-President is ex officio Chairman of the Council of States (Rajya Sabha) and holds no other office of profit.
Key Provisions: Does not discharge Chairman's duties or draw Chairman's salary while acting as President under Art 65.
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Deputy Chairman presides over Rajya Sabha when VP acts as President.

flashcard 77:
frontside:
Article 65 - PART V - THE UNION | CHAPTER I. - THE EXECUTIVE
Prompt: What does Article 65 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: The Vice -President to act as President or to discharge his functions during casual vacancies in the office, or during the absence, of President
Status: In Force
Mandate: Vice-President acts as President during vacancy (death/resignation/removal) or discharges functions during absence/illness.
Key Provisions: Under President (Discharge of Functions) Act 1969, if both President & VP vacant, CJI acts as President.
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: VP acts as President only for a maximum of 6 months, until elections are held.

flashcard 78:
frontside:
Article 66 - PART V - THE UNION | CHAPTER I. - THE EXECUTIVE
Prompt: What does Article 66 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Election of Vice-President
Status: In Force
Mandate: Election of Vice-President: Elected by Electoral College of ALL MPs of both Houses (PR-STV, secret ballot).
Key Provisions: Includes both ELECTED and NOMINATED MPs. State MLAs do NOT participate.
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Nominated MPs vote for VP (unlike President); State MLAs do NOT vote for VP (unlike President).

flashcard 79:
frontside:
Article 67 - PART V - THE UNION | CHAPTER I. - THE EXECUTIVE
Prompt: What does Article 67 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Term of office of Vice-President
Status: In Force
Mandate: Term of Vice-President is 5 years. Removed by resolution of Rajya Sabha passed by EFFECTIVE majority and agreed to by Lok Sabha (simple majority).
Key Provisions: 14 days notice required. No formal impeachment; Constitution specifies NO grounds for removal.
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Removal resolution can be initiated ONLY in Rajya Sabha, never in Lok Sabha.

flashcard 80:
frontside:
Article 68 - PART V - THE UNION | CHAPTER I. - THE EXECUTIVE
Prompt: What does Article 68 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Time of holding election to fill vacancy in the office of Vice-President and the term of office of person elected to fill casual vacancy
Status: In Force
Mandate: Election to fill term-expiry vacancy completed before expiry; vacancy by death/resignation/removal filled 'as soon as possible' for full 5-year term.
Key Provisions: No fixed 6-month deadline textually in Art 68; uses phrase 'as soon as possible'.
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: No constitutional provision exists for an 'Acting Vice-President'.

flashcard 81:
frontside:
Article 69 - PART V - THE UNION | CHAPTER I. - THE EXECUTIVE
Prompt: What does Article 69 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Oath or affirmation by the Vice-President
Status: In Force
Mandate: Oath of Vice-President administered by President or person appointed by him.
Key Provisions: Contained in Article 69 itself, NOT in the Third Schedule.
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Oath is before President, not Chief Justice of India.

flashcard 82:
frontside:
Article 70 - PART V - THE UNION | CHAPTER I. - THE EXECUTIVE
Prompt: What does Article 70 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Discharge of President - s functions in other contingencies
Status: In Force
Mandate: Parliament may make provisions for discharge of President's functions in unforeseen contingencies.
Key Provisions: Enabled the enactment of the President (Discharge of Functions) Act, 1969.
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Parliamentary statute is required, not executive regulation.

flashcard 83:
frontside:
Article 71 - PART V - THE UNION | CHAPTER I. - THE EXECUTIVE
Prompt: What does Article 71 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Matters relating to, or connected with, the election of a President or Vice-President
Status: In Force
Mandate: All doubts and disputes regarding election of President or Vice-President shall be decided exclusively by the SUPREME COURT.
Key Provisions: Election cannot be challenged on ground of vacancy in Electoral College (Art 71(4), 11th Amdt).
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Election petition lies only in Supreme Court, after election is concluded, not before.

flashcard 84:
frontside:
Article 72 - PART V - THE UNION | CHAPTER I. - THE EXECUTIVE
Prompt: What does Article 72 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Power of President to grant pardons, etc., and to suspend, remit or commute sentences in certain cases
Status: In Force
Mandate: President's power to grant pardons, reprieves, respites, remissions, or commute sentences in Union offences, court-martials, and death sentences.
Key Provisions: Pardon removes both punishment and guilt. Exercised on binding advice of Union Home Ministry/CoM. Judicial review limited (Kehar Singh, Epuru Sudhakar).
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Governor (Art 161) cannot pardon death sentences (can only suspend/remit/commute) and has no court-martial powers.

flashcard 85:
frontside:
Article 73 - PART V - THE UNION | CHAPTER I. - THE EXECUTIVE
Prompt: What does Article 73 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Extent of executive power of the Union. Council of Ministers
Status: In Force
Mandate: Executive power of Union extends to matters where Parliament can make laws, and treaty rights/obligations.
Key Provisions: In Concurrent List, executive power normally remains with States unless Parliament explicitly assumes it.
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Co-extensive with legislative power, but subject to State executive primacy in concurrent field.

flashcard 86:
frontside:
Article 74 - PART V - THE UNION | CHAPTER I. - THE EXECUTIVE
Prompt: What does Article 74 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Council of Ministers to aid and advise President
Status: In Force
Mandate: Council of Ministers with Prime Minister at head to aid and advise President, who shall act in accordance with such advice.
Key Provisions: 44th Amdt: President can send back advice ONCE for reconsideration; must accept advice tendered after reconsideration. Advice tendered is immune from judicial inquiry (Art 74(2)).
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Factual basis/material underlying advice CAN be examined by courts (Bommai case 1994), though the advice itself is privileged.

flashcard 87:
frontside:
Article 75 - PART V - THE UNION | CHAPTER I. - THE EXECUTIVE
Prompt: What does Article 75 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Other provisions as to Ministers. The Attorney-General for India
Status: In Force
Mandate: PM appointed by President; other Ministers on PM's advice. Total ministers capped at 15% of Lok Sabha (91st Amdt 2003). CoM collectively responsible to Lok Sabha.
Key Provisions: Collective responsibility (Art 75(3)) is to Lok Sabha alone. Non-member can be Minister for max 6 consecutive months.
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: CoM is collectively responsible to LOK SABHA, NOT to Rajya Sabha or Parliament as a whole.

flashcard 88:
frontside:
Article 76 - PART V - THE UNION | CHAPTER I. - THE EXECUTIVE
Prompt: What does Article 76 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Attorney-General for India. Conduct of Government Business
Status: In Force
Mandate: Attorney-General for India appointed by President; must be qualified as SC Judge. Chief legal adviser to GoI.
Key Provisions: Holds office during PLEASURE of President; no fixed tenure. Has right of audience in all Indian courts. Right to speak in Parliament without right to vote (Art 88).
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Not a government servant, not in Cabinet, not barred from private practice (cannot plead against GoI).

flashcard 89:
frontside:
Article 77 - PART V - THE UNION | CHAPTER I. - THE EXECUTIVE
Prompt: What does Article 77 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Conduct of business of the Government of India
Status: In Force
Mandate: Conduct of business: All executive action expressed in name of President. President makes Allocation and Transaction of Business Rules.
Key Provisions: Civil servants and ministers act under Transaction of Business Rules made under Art 77(3).
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Properly authenticated orders cannot be challenged on ground that President did not personally make them.

flashcard 90:
frontside:
Article 78 - PART V - THE UNION | CHAPTER I. - THE EXECUTIVE
Prompt: What does Article 78 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Duties of Prime Minister as respects the furnishing of information to the President, etc
Status: In Force
Mandate: Duties of PM: Communicate CoM decisions to President; furnish administrative/legislative info; submit single minister's decision to CoM if President requires.
Key Provisions: Constitutional communication bridge between executive Cabinet and titular Head of State.
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Furnishing information is a mandatory constitutional duty of the Prime Minister.

flashcard 91:
frontside:
Article 79 - PART V - THE UNION | CHAPTER II. - PARLIAMENT
Prompt: What does Article 79 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?
