backside:
Title: Constitution of Parliament
Status: In Force
Mandate: Constitution of Parliament: Consists of the President and two Houses - Council of States (Rajya Sabha) and House of the People (Lok Sabha).
Key Provisions: President is an integral part of Parliament though not a member of either House (summons, prorogues, assents).
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Parliament is not just the two Houses; it is a tripartite body: President + Rajya Sabha + Lok Sabha.

flashcard 92:
frontside:
Article 80 - PART V - THE UNION | CHAPTER II. - PARLIAMENT
Prompt: What does Article 80 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Composition of the Council of States
Status: In Force
Mandate: Composition of Rajya Sabha: Max 250 members - 12 nominated by President (Literature, Science, Art, Social Service) + max 238 representatives of States/UTs.
Key Provisions: Elected indirectly by elected MLAs of States using proportional representation by single transferable vote (STV).
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Nomination fields are 4: Literature, Science, Art, Social Service. 'Co-operation' is NOT a field for RS (it exists only for State Legislative Councils under Art 171).

flashcard 93:
frontside:
Article 81 - PART V - THE UNION | CHAPTER II. - PARLIAMENT
Prompt: What does Article 81 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Composition of the House of the People
Status: In Force
Mandate: Composition of Lok Sabha: Max 550 elected members (max 530 from States, max 20 from UTs). Directly elected by adult suffrage.
Key Provisions: Nomination of 2 Anglo-Indians under Art 331 was abolished by the 104th Amendment Act, 2019.
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Anglo-Indian nomination in Lok Sabha no longer exists.

flashcard 94:
frontside:
Article 82 - PART V - THE UNION | CHAPTER II. - PARLIAMENT
Prompt: What does Article 82 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Readjustment after each census
Status: In Force
Mandate: Readjustment of seats and territorial constituencies after each Census by Delimitation Commission.
Key Provisions: Seat allocation frozen until after first census taken after 2026 (84th Amdt 2001, based on 1971 census). Internal boundary delimitation based on 2001 census (87th Amdt).
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Total seats frozen on 1971 census; internal constituency boundaries updated on 2001 census.

flashcard 95:
frontside:
Article 83 - PART V - THE UNION | CHAPTER II. - PARLIAMENT
Prompt: What does Article 83 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Duration of Houses of Parliament
Status: In Force
Mandate: Duration: Rajya Sabha is permanent (never dissolved; 1/3rd members retire every 2nd year; term 6 yrs). Lok Sabha term is 5 years unless dissolved sooner.
Key Provisions: Lok Sabha term can be extended by Parliament by law during National Emergency for 1 year at a time (max 6 months after emergency ends).
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Rajya Sabha term of 6 years is fixed by Representation of the People Act 1951, while 1/3rd biennial retirement is in Art 83(1).

flashcard 96:
frontside:
Article 84 - PART V - THE UNION | CHAPTER II. - PARLIAMENT
Prompt: What does Article 84 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Qualification for membership of Parliament
Status: In Force
Mandate: Qualifications for MP: Citizen of India, age >= 25 years for Lok Sabha, age >= 30 years for Rajya Sabha, oath before EC-authorised person.
Key Provisions: Must possess other qualifications prescribed by Parliament under law (RPA 1951).
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Minimum age: Lok Sabha = 25 years; Rajya Sabha = 30 years; President/VP/Governor = 35 years; Panchayats = 21 years.

flashcard 97:
frontside:
Article 85 - PART V - THE UNION | CHAPTER II. - PARLIAMENT
Prompt: What does Article 85 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Sessions of Parliament, prorogation and dissolution
Status: In Force
Mandate: Sessions of Parliament, prorogation and dissolution: President summons each House. Max interval between two sittings cannot exceed 6 MONTHS.
Key Provisions: President prorogues both Houses; dissolves Lok Sabha. Rajya Sabha cannot be dissolved.
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Sessions (Budget, Monsoon, Winter) are conventions; the only constitutional rule is that max gap between two sessions <= 6 months.

flashcard 98:
frontside:
Article 86 - PART V - THE UNION | CHAPTER II. - PARLIAMENT
Prompt: What does Article 86 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Right of President to address and send messages to Houses
Status: In Force
Mandate: President's right to address and send messages to either House or both Houses assembled together.
Key Provisions: Houses must consider any message sent by the President with all convenient dispatch.
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Right to send messages can be exercised on any pending Bill or other matter.

flashcard 99:
frontside:
Article 87 - PART V - THE UNION | CHAPTER II. - PARLIAMENT
Prompt: What does Article 87 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Special address by the President
Status: In Force
Mandate: Special address by President: At commencement of first session after each general election, and at commencement of first session of each year.
Key Provisions: Addressed to both Houses assembled together; followed by Motion of Thanks debate and vote in both Houses.
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Defeat of Motion of Thanks in Lok Sabha amounts to a vote of no-confidence, requiring Government's resignation.

flashcard 100:
frontside:
Article 88 - PART V - THE UNION | CHAPTER II. - PARLIAMENT
Prompt: What does Article 88 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Rights of Ministers and Attorney-General as respects Houses
Status: In Force
Mandate: Rights of Ministers and Attorney-General to speak and take part in proceedings of either House, joint sittings, and parliamentary committees.
Key Provisions: A Minister can speak in either House, but CAN VOTE ONLY in the House of which he/she is a member. Attorney-General cannot vote at all.
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Minister who is a Rajya Sabha MP can speak in Lok Sabha, but cannot vote in Lok Sabha.

flashcard 101:
frontside:
Article 89 - PART V - THE UNION | CHAPTER II. - PARLIAMENT
Prompt: What does Article 89 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: The Chairman and Deputy Chairman of the Council of States
Status: In Force
Mandate: Chairman (Vice-President ex officio) and Deputy Chairman of the Council of States (elected by Rajya Sabha from among its members).
Key Provisions: Deputy Chairman must be a member of Rajya Sabha; vacates office if he ceases to be a member.
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Chairman is NOT a member of Rajya Sabha; Deputy Chairman IS a member of Rajya Sabha.

flashcard 102:
frontside:
Article 90 - PART V - THE UNION | CHAPTER II. - PARLIAMENT
Prompt: What does Article 90 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Vacation and resignation of, and removal from, the office of Deputy Chairman
Status: In Force
Mandate: Vacation, resignation and removal of Deputy Chairman of Rajya Sabha.
Key Provisions: Removed by resolution of Rajya Sabha passed by majority of all then members (effective majority) after 14 days notice.
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Resignation of Deputy Chairman is addressed to the Chairman of Rajya Sabha.

flashcard 103:
frontside:
Article 91 - PART V - THE UNION | CHAPTER II. - PARLIAMENT
Prompt: What does Article 91 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Power of the Deputy Chairman or other person to perform the duties of the office of, or to act as, Chairman
Status: In Force
Mandate: Power of Deputy Chairman or other person to perform duties of office of, or to act as, Chairman.
Key Provisions: When Chairman office is vacant or VP acting as President, Deputy Chairman discharges duties.
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Panel of Vice-Chairpersons can preside when Chairman/Deputy Chairman are absent, but NOT when offices are vacant (President appoints MP to preside in vacancy).

flashcard 104:
frontside:
Article 92 - PART V - THE UNION | CHAPTER II. - PARLIAMENT
Prompt: What does Article 92 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: The Chairman or the Deputy Chairman not to preside while a resolution for his removal from office is under consideration
Status: In Force
Mandate: Chairman or Deputy Chairman not to preside while a resolution for his removal is under consideration.
Key Provisions: Chairman can speak and take part, but CANNOT vote at all on his removal resolution (unlike Speaker LS).
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Speaker can vote in first instance on his removal resolution; Chairman RS cannot vote at all because he is not an MP.

flashcard 105:
frontside:
Article 93 - PART V - THE UNION | CHAPTER II. - PARLIAMENT
Prompt: What does Article 93 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: The Speaker and Deputy Speaker of the House of the People
Status: In Force
Mandate: The Speaker and Deputy Speaker of the House of the People: Lok Sabha elects two members from among its members.
Key Provisions: Constitutional offices representing the dignity and authority of the House.
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Election date of Speaker is fixed by President; election date of Deputy Speaker is fixed by Speaker.

flashcard 106:
frontside:
Article 94 - PART V - THE UNION | CHAPTER II. - PARLIAMENT
Prompt: What does Article 94 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Vacation and resignation of, and removal from, the offices of Speaker and Deputy Speaker
Status: In Force
Mandate: Vacation, resignation, and removal of Speaker and Deputy Speaker.
Key Provisions: Speaker resigns to Deputy Speaker; Deputy Speaker resigns to Speaker. Removed by effective majority of Lok Sabha after 14 days notice.
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: On dissolution of Lok Sabha, Speaker does NOT vacate office immediately; continues until immediately before the first meeting of new Lok Sabha.

flashcard 107:
frontside:
Article 95 - PART V - THE UNION | CHAPTER II. - PARLIAMENT
Prompt: What does Article 95 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Power of the Deputy Speaker or other person to perform the duties of the office of, or to act as, Speaker
Status: In Force
Mandate: Power of Deputy Speaker or other person to perform duties of office of, or to act as, Speaker.
Key Provisions: When Speaker office is vacant, Deputy Speaker acts. If both vacant, President appoints a member (Pro-tem Speaker).
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Pro-tem Speaker is appointed by President under Art 95(1) to administer oaths to new MPs and conduct Speaker election.

flashcard 108:
frontside:
Article 96 - PART V - THE UNION | CHAPTER II. - PARLIAMENT
Prompt: What does Article 96 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: The Speaker or the Deputy Speaker not to preside while a resolution for his removal from office is under consideration
Status: In Force
Mandate: Speaker or Deputy Speaker not to preside while a resolution for his removal is under consideration.
Key Provisions: Speaker has right to speak, take part, and VOTE IN THE FIRST INSTANCE, but NOT in case of equality of votes (casting vote).
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Normally Speaker votes only in equality of votes (casting vote); during his removal, he votes only in first instance.

flashcard 109:
frontside:
Article 97 - PART V - THE UNION | CHAPTER II. - PARLIAMENT
Prompt: What does Article 97 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Salaries and allowances of the Chairman and Deputy Chairman and the Speaker and Deputy Speaker
Status: In Force
Mandate: Salaries and allowances of Chairman, Deputy Chairman, Speaker, and Deputy Speaker.
Key Provisions: Charged on Consolidated Fund of India (non-votable by Parliament).
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Fixed by Parliament by law and specified in Second Schedule.

flashcard 110:
frontside:
Article 98 - PART V - THE UNION | CHAPTER II. - PARLIAMENT
Prompt: What does Article 98 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Secretariat of Parliament
Status: In Force
Mandate: Secretariat of Parliament: Each House has a separate secretarial staff; common posts permitted.
Key Provisions: Parliament regulates recruitment and service conditions by law; independent of executive control.
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Secretariat is headed by Secretary-General, appointed by the Presiding Officer.

flashcard 111:
frontside:
Article 99 - PART V - THE UNION | CHAPTER II. - PARLIAMENT
Prompt: What does Article 99 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Oath or affirmation by members
Status: In Force
Mandate: Oath or affirmation by members of Parliament before President or person appointed by him.
Key Provisions: Form of oath set out in Third Schedule. Member cannot sit or vote until oath is taken.
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Sitting and voting before taking oath incurs a daily penalty of Rs 500 under Article 104.

flashcard 112:
frontside:
Article 100 - PART V - THE UNION | CHAPTER II. - PARLIAMENT
Prompt: What does Article 100 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Voting in Houses, power of Houses to act notwithstanding vacancies and quorum
Status: In Force
Mandate: Voting in Houses, power of Houses to act notwithstanding vacancies, and quorum.
Key Provisions: Decisions by majority of present and voting. Presiding officer has CASTING VOTE in case of tie. Quorum = 1/10th of total membership (55 LS, 25 RS).
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: If no quorum, Presiding Officer must adjourn House or suspend sitting; cannot conduct business.

flashcard 113:
frontside:
Article 101 - PART V - THE UNION | CHAPTER II. - PARLIAMENT
Prompt: What does Article 101 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Vacation of seats
Status: In Force
Mandate: Vacation of seats: Prohibition on dual membership (cannot be MP in both Houses, or MP and MLA simultaneously).
Key Provisions: If elected to both Parliament & State Legislature and does not resign state seat within 14 days, Parliament seat becomes vacant. Resignation to Presiding Officer must be voluntary and genuine (33rd Amdt 1974).
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Seat becomes vacant if MP is absent from all meetings for 60 DAYS without permission (excluding periods of prorogation/adjournment > 4 consecutive days).

flashcard 114:
frontside:
Article 102 - PART V - THE UNION | CHAPTER II. - PARLIAMENT
Prompt: What does Article 102 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Disqualifications for membership
Status: In Force
Mandate: Disqualifications for membership: (a) Office of profit; (b) Unsound mind; (c) Undischarged insolvent; (d) Not a citizen/acquired foreign citizenship; (e) Disqualified under any law made by Parliament (RPA 1951). 102(2): Disqualification under Tenth Schedule (Anti-defection).
Key Provisions: Office of profit disqualification tested on executive control, appointment, remuneration (Pradyut Bordoloi case).
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: RPA 1951 disqualifications (e.g. 2-yr criminal conviction under Sec 8(3), Lily Thomas case) derive authority from Art 102(1)(e).

flashcard 115:
frontside:
Article 103 - PART V - THE UNION | CHAPTER II. - PARLIAMENT
Prompt: What does Article 103 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Decision on questions as to disqualifications of members
Status: In Force
Mandate: Decision on questions as to disqualifications of members (under Art 102(1)): Referred to the PRESIDENT, whose decision is final.
Key Provisions: President MUST obtain opinion of the ELECTION COMMISSION and act according to such opinion.
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Disqualification under 102(1) is decided by President on EC's binding advice; BUT disqualification under Tenth Schedule (Anti-defection) is decided by the SPEAKER/CHAIRMAN.

flashcard 116:
frontside:
Article 104 - PART V - THE UNION | CHAPTER II. - PARLIAMENT
Prompt: What does Article 104 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Penalty for sitting and voting before making oath or affirmation under article 99 or when not qualified or when disqualified. Powers, Privileges and Immunities of Parliament and its Members
Status: In Force
Mandate: Penalty for sitting and voting before making oath under Art 99 or when not qualified or when disqualified: Rs 500 per day.
Key Provisions: Recoverable as a debt due to the Union.
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Applies to both lack of oath and statutory/constitutional disqualification.

flashcard 117:
frontside:
Article 105 - PART V - THE UNION | CHAPTER II. - PARLIAMENT
Prompt: What does Article 105 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Powers, privileges, etc., of the Houses of Parliament and of the members and committees thereof
Status: In Force
Mandate: Powers, privileges and immunities of Parliament and its members and committees.
Key Provisions: 105(1) Freedom of speech in Parliament (subject to rules); 105(2) Absolute immunity from court proceedings for anything said or vote given in Parliament or committees. PV Narasimha Rao case (1998) was OVERRULED in Sita Soren (2024): bribery to vote is NOT protected by Art 105(2).
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Privilege against civil arrest exists 40 days before and after sessions; NO immunity exists against arrest in criminal offences or preventive detention.

flashcard 118:
frontside:
Article 106 - PART V - THE UNION | CHAPTER II. - PARLIAMENT
Prompt: What does Article 106 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Salaries and allowances of members. Legislative Procedure
Status: In Force
Mandate: Salaries and allowances of members of Parliament determined by Parliament by law.
Key Provisions: Enacted via Salary, Allowances and Pension of Members of Parliament Act, 1954.
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Determined by Parliament itself; not charged on Consolidated Fund (votable).

flashcard 119:
frontside:
Article 107 - PART V - THE UNION | CHAPTER II. - PARLIAMENT
Prompt: What does Article 107 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Provisions as to introduction and passing of Bills
Status: In Force
Mandate: Provisions as to introduction and passing of Bills: Ordinary bill can originate in either House; must be agreed to by both Houses.
Key Provisions: Lapse rules on dissolution of Lok Sabha: (1) Bill pending in LS lapses; (2) Bill passed by LS, pending in RS lapses; (3) Bill pending in RS not passed by LS does NOT lapse; (4) Bill passed by both, awaiting President does NOT lapse; (5) Bill where President notified joint sitting before dissolution does NOT lapse.
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Bill originating in Rajya Sabha and pending there (not yet passed by LS) does NOT lapse on LS dissolution.

flashcard 120:
frontside:
Article 108 - PART V - THE UNION | CHAPTER II. - PARLIAMENT
Prompt: What does Article 108 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Joint sitting of both Houses in certain cases
Status: In Force
Mandate: Joint sitting of both Houses in case of deadlock on an Ordinary or Financial Bill.
Key Provisions: Grounds after 6 months deadlock: (a) Bill rejected by other House; (b) Houses disagree on amendments; (c) More than 6 months pass without passing. Summoned by President; presided by SPEAKER of Lok Sabha (or Deputy Speaker, or Deputy Chairman RS; Chairman RS NEVER presides). Decided by simple majority of all present and voting.
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Joint sitting is NOT available for Money Bills (Art 110) OR Constitutional Amendment Bills (Art 368). Only 3 joint sittings held so far (Dowry 1961, Banking 1978, POTA 2002).

flashcard 121:
frontside:
Article 109 - PART V - THE UNION | CHAPTER II. - PARLIAMENT
Prompt: What does Article 109 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Special procedure in respect of Money Bills
Status: In Force
Mandate: Special procedure in respect of Money Bills: Can be introduced ONLY in Lok Sabha, on President's recommendation. Cannot be introduced in Rajya Sabha.
Key Provisions: Rajya Sabha has 14 DAYS to return with recommendations. Lok Sabha may accept or reject recommendations. If RS does not return within 14 days, deemed passed.
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Rajya Sabha cannot reject or amend a Money Bill; can only delay for max 14 days. No joint sitting exists.

flashcard 122:
frontside:
Article 110 - PART V - THE UNION | CHAPTER II. - PARLIAMENT
Prompt: What does Article 110 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Definition of  - Money Bills - 
Status: In Force
Mandate: Definition of 'Money Bill': Contains ONLY provisions dealing with: (a) imposition/abolition/alteration of tax; (b) borrowing/guarantees by Union; (c) custody of Consolidated/Contingency Fund; (d) appropriation of moneys out of CFI; (e) declaring expenditure charged on CFI; (f) receipt/custody/audit of public accounts.
Key Provisions: Speaker certifies whether a Bill is a Money Bill; Speaker's certificate is final. Narrow judicial review for constitutional fraud remains (Aadhaar case 2018).
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Fines, fees, or local taxes do NOT make a Bill a Money Bill. Bill must contain 'only' matters listed in Art 110.

flashcard 123:
frontside:
Article 111 - PART V - THE UNION | CHAPTER II. - PARLIAMENT
Prompt: What does Article 111 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Assent to Bills. Procedure in Financial Matters
Status: In Force
Mandate: Assent to Bills: When passed by both Houses, presented to President who may: (a) Assent; (b) Withhold assent; (c) Return for reconsideration (if not Money Bill).
Key Provisions: If returned bill is passed again by both Houses with or without amendments, President MUST give assent (suspensive veto). Pocket veto = President takes no action.
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: President CANNOT return a Money Bill for reconsideration (must either assent or withhold assent). President MUST assent to Constitutional Amendment Bills (24th Amdt).

flashcard 124:
frontside:
Article 112 - PART V - THE UNION | CHAPTER II. - PARLIAMENT
Prompt: What does Article 112 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Annual financial statement
Status: In Force
Mandate: Annual Financial Statement (Budget): Statement of estimated receipts and expenditure of GoI for each financial year, laid before Parliament by President.
Key Provisions: Distinguishes expenditure charged upon Consolidated Fund of India (non-votable, can only be discussed) from other expenditure (votable by Lok Sabha).
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: The word 'Budget' does not appear anywhere in the Constitution; the constitutional term is 'Annual Financial Statement'.

flashcard 125:
frontside:
Article 113 - PART V - THE UNION | CHAPTER II. - PARLIAMENT
Prompt: What does Article 113 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Procedure in Parliament with respect to estimates
Status: In Force
Mandate: Procedure in Parliament with respect to estimates: Charged expenditure discussed but not voted. Votable expenditure submitted as Demands for Grants to Lok Sabha.
Key Provisions: Only Lok Sabha votes on Demands for Grants; Rajya Sabha cannot vote. Cut Motions (Policy, Economy, Token) moved by MPs.
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Rajya Sabha can only discuss the Budget; it has NO power to vote on Demands for Grants.

flashcard 126:
frontside:
Article 114 - PART V - THE UNION | CHAPTER II. - PARLIAMENT
Prompt: What does Article 114 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Appropriation Bills
Status: In Force
Mandate: Appropriation Bills: Introduced after voting on grants to authorise withdrawal of moneys from the Consolidated Fund of India.
Key Provisions: No money can be withdrawn from CFI without an enacted Appropriation Act (Art 114(3)).
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: No amendment can be proposed to an Appropriation Bill varying the amount or altering the destination of any grant.

flashcard 127:
frontside:
Article 115 - PART V - THE UNION | CHAPTER II. - PARLIAMENT
Prompt: What does Article 115 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Supplementary, additional or excess grants
Status: In Force
Mandate: Supplementary, additional, or excess grants: When budgeted amount proves insufficient, or money spent beyond sanction.
Key Provisions: Excess Grant requires prior recommendation of Public Accounts Committee (PAC) before submission to Lok Sabha.
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Excess grant is voted after the financial year in which money was overspent.

flashcard 128:
frontside:
Article 116 - PART V - THE UNION | CHAPTER II. - PARLIAMENT
Prompt: What does Article 116 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Votes on account, votes of credit and exceptional grants
Status: In Force
Mandate: Votes on account, votes of credit and exceptional grants.
Key Provisions: Vote on account: Advance grant to meet interim expenditure pending passing of Appropriation Bill (usually 2 months). Vote of credit: Blank cheque for unexpected demand (national emergency/war).
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Vote on account is an interim financial provision; Vote of credit is for unspecifiable expenditure due to magnitude/character.

flashcard 129:
frontside:
Article 117 - PART V - THE UNION | CHAPTER II. - PARLIAMENT
Prompt: What does Article 117 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Special provisions as to financial Bills. Procedure Generally
Status: In Force
Mandate: Special provisions as to Financial Bills: (1) Financial Bill (I) [Art 117(1)]: contains Art 110 matters + other matters; introduced only in LS on President's recommendation. (2) Financial Bill (II) [Art 117(3)]: involves expenditure from CFI; can originate in either House, but needs President's recommendation for consideration.
Key Provisions: All Money Bills are Financial Bills, but all Financial Bills are not Money Bills. Joint sitting IS available for Financial Bills I and II.
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Financial Bill (I) introduced only in LS on recommendation; Financial Bill (II) can originate in either House, recommendation needed only at consideration stage.

flashcard 130:
frontside:
Article 118 - PART V - THE UNION | CHAPTER II. - PARLIAMENT
Prompt: What does Article 118 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Rules of procedure
Status: In Force
Mandate: Rules of procedure: Each House of Parliament may make rules for regulating its procedure and conduct of business.
Key Provisions: Rules of Lok Sabha and Rules of Rajya Sabha are framed under this Article. Joint sittings governed by rules made by President in consultation with Chairman & Speaker.
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Parliamentary rules are framed by each House independently; cannot be questioned in court for procedural irregularity (Art 122).

flashcard 131:
frontside:
Article 119 - PART V - THE UNION | CHAPTER II. - PARLIAMENT
Prompt: What does Article 119 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Regulation by law of procedure in Parliament in relation to financial business
Status: In Force
Mandate: Regulation by law of procedure in Parliament in relation to financial business.
Key Provisions: Parliament can enact law to expedite financial business; such statutory law overrides internal House rules in case of inconsistency.
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Ensures timely completion of budgetary processes.

flashcard 132:
frontside:
Article 120 - PART V - THE UNION | CHAPTER II. - PARLIAMENT
Prompt: What does Article 120 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Language to be used in Parliament
Status: In Force
Mandate: Language to be used in Parliament: Hindi or English. Presiding officer may permit any member to address House in his mother tongue.
Key Provisions: Official Languages Act, 1963 permits continued use of English in Parliament alongside Hindi.
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Permission of Presiding Officer is mandatory to speak in mother tongue if not Hindi or English.

flashcard 133:
frontside:
Article 121 - PART V - THE UNION | CHAPTER II. - PARLIAMENT
Prompt: What does Article 121 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Restriction on discussion in Parliament
Status: In Force
Mandate: Restriction on discussion in Parliament: No discussion can take place in Parliament regarding conduct of any Judge of Supreme Court or High Court in discharge of duties.
Key Provisions: Exception: Discussion IS permitted when a motion for removal (impeachment) of the judge is under consideration.
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Absolute bar during normal sessions; opens up ONLY during impeachment proceedings under Art 124(4).

flashcard 134:
frontside:
Article 122 - PART V - THE UNION | CHAPTER II. - PARLIAMENT
Prompt: What does Article 122 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Courts not to inquire into proceedings of Parliament
Status: In Force
Mandate: Courts not to inquire into proceedings of Parliament: Validity of any proceedings in Parliament cannot be called in question on ground of any alleged irregularity of procedure.
Key Provisions: Officers and MPs exercising powers under Constitution are not subject to jurisdiction of any court in respect of those powers.
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Protects against 'irregularity of procedure'; does NOT protect against substantive unconstitutionality, illegality, or gross violation of constitutional mandate (Raja Ram Pal case 2007).

flashcard 135:
frontside:
Article 123 - PART V - THE UNION | CHAPTER III. - LEGISLATIVE POWERS OF THE
Prompt: What does Article 123 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Power of President to promulgate Ordinances during recess of Parliament
Status: In Force
Mandate: Power of President to promulgate Ordinances during recess of Parliament: When both Houses (or either House) not in session and President satisfied circumstances require immediate action.
Key Provisions: Has same force and effect as an Act of Parliament. Must be laid before Parliament when it reassembles; CEASES TO OPERATE AT EXPIRATION OF 6 WEEKS from reassembly (max life = 6 months + 6 weeks). Can be withdrawn by President at any time. DC Wadhwa (1987) & Krishna Kumar Singh (2017): re-promulgation of ordinances without legislative approval is a fraud on the Constitution.
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Cannot be issued when BOTH Houses are in session (can be issued if only one House is in session). Subject to judicial review for mala fides (Cooper, Bommai cases). Cannot amend the Constitution.

flashcard 136:
frontside:
Article 124 - PART V - THE UNION | CHAPTER IV. -  THE UNION JUDICIARY
Prompt: What does Article 124 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Establishment and constitution of the Supreme Court
Status: In Force
Mandate: Establishment and constitution of Supreme Court: CJI + other judges as Parliament prescribes (originally 8; currently 34). Appointed by President.
Key Provisions: Collegium System evolved through Three Judges Cases: First Judges (1981 - executive primacy); Second Judges (1993 - CJI + 2 senior judges, primacy to judiciary); Third Judges (1998 - CJI + 4 senior-most judges). Qualifications: Citizen, 5 yrs HC Judge OR 10 yrs HC Advocate OR distinguished jurist. Tenure: up to 65 years. Removal (124(4)): By President after address by both Houses supported by special majority (majority of total + 2/3 present & voting) on grounds of 'proved misbehaviour or incapacity'.
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Distinguished jurist qualification exists ONLY for Supreme Court, NOT for High Court. Minimum age for SC judge is NOT prescribed in the Constitution.

flashcard 137:
frontside:
Article 124A - PART V - THE UNION | CHAPTER IV. -  THE UNION JUDICIARY
Prompt: What does Article 124A provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: National Judicial Appointments Commission
Status: In Force
Mandate: National Judicial Appointments Commission (NJAC) - inserted by 99th Amendment Act, 2014 to replace collegium with 6-member body.
Key Provisions: Struck down as UNCONSTITUTIONAL by 5-judge bench in Fourth Judges Case (2015) for violating judicial independence, a basic structure of the Constitution. Collegium revived.
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Articles 124A, 124B, 124C remain textually in books but are legally void/inoperative post-2015 ruling.

flashcard 138:
frontside:
Article 124B - PART V - THE UNION | CHAPTER IV. -  THE UNION JUDICIARY
Prompt: What does Article 124B provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Functions of Commission
Status: In Force
Mandate: Official constitutional provision governing Functions of Commission within PART V - THE UNION | CHAPTER IV. -  THE UNION JUDICIARY.
Key Provisions: Prescribes institutional mechanisms, procedural rules, and statutory boundaries for constitutional administration.
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Read in conjunction with parent chapter provisions to verify whether power is discretionary or exercised on aid and advice.

flashcard 139:
frontside:
Article 124C - PART V - THE UNION | CHAPTER IV. -  THE UNION JUDICIARY
Prompt: What does Article 124C provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Power of Parliament to make law
Status: In Force
Mandate: Official constitutional provision governing Power of Parliament to make law within PART V - THE UNION | CHAPTER IV. -  THE UNION JUDICIARY.
Key Provisions: Prescribes institutional mechanisms, procedural rules, and statutory boundaries for constitutional administration.
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Read in conjunction with parent chapter provisions to verify whether power is discretionary or exercised on aid and advice.

flashcard 140:
frontside:
Article 125 - PART V - THE UNION | CHAPTER IV. -  THE UNION JUDICIARY
Prompt: What does Article 125 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Salaries, etc., of Judges
Status: In Force
Mandate: Salaries, allowances and rights in respect of leave of absence and pension of Supreme Court Judges determined by Parliament.
Key Provisions: Charged on Consolidated Fund of India (non-votable). Cannot be varied to their disadvantage after appointment, except during a Financial Emergency (Art 360).
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Salary can be reduced ONLY during a proclamation of Financial Emergency under Article 360.

flashcard 141:
frontside:
Article 126 - PART V - THE UNION | CHAPTER IV. -  THE UNION JUDICIARY
Prompt: What does Article 126 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Appointment of acting Chief Justice
Status: In Force
Mandate: Appointment of Acting Chief Justice: When office of CJI is vacant or absent, appointed by President from among SC Judges.
Key Provisions: Ensures continuous functioning of the apex judicial administration.
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: President appoints one of the other judges of SC, usually the next senior-most.

flashcard 142:
frontside:
Article 127 - PART V - THE UNION | CHAPTER IV. -  THE UNION JUDICIARY
Prompt: What does Article 127 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Appointment of ad hoc Judges
Status: In Force
Mandate: Ad hoc Judges: When lack of quorum of SC judges to hold or continue session, CJI with previous consent of President and consultation with Chief Justice of HC concerned can request a qualified HC judge to attend as ad hoc judge.
Key Provisions: Ad hoc judge has all jurisdiction, powers and privileges of an SC judge.
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Ad hoc judges exist ONLY in Supreme Court (Art 127), NOT in High Courts.

flashcard 143:
frontside:
Article 128 - PART V - THE UNION | CHAPTER IV. -  THE UNION JUDICIARY
Prompt: What does Article 128 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Attendance of retired Judges at sittings of the Supreme Court
Status: In Force
Mandate: Attendance of retired Judges at sittings of the Supreme Court: CJI with previous consent of President may request a retired SC or Federal Court judge to sit and act as judge.
Key Provisions: Entitled to allowances determined by President; does not otherwise deem to be a judge of SC.
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Consent of the retired judge concerned is mandatory.

flashcard 144:
frontside:
Article 129 - PART V - THE UNION | CHAPTER IV. -  THE UNION JUDICIARY
Prompt: What does Article 129 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Supreme Court to be a court of record
Status: In Force
Mandate: Supreme Court to be a Court of Record: Has all powers of such a court including the power to punish for contempt of itself.
Key Provisions: Court of Record means: (1) Judgments are recorded for perpetual memory and testimony (precedential value); (2) Summary power to punish for contempt. Contempt of Courts Act, 1971 defines civil and criminal contempt.
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Contempt power of SC is a constitutional power that cannot be abrogated or limited even by parliamentary legislation (Zahira Habibullah case).

flashcard 145:
frontside:
Article 130 - PART V - THE UNION | CHAPTER IV. -  THE UNION JUDICIARY
Prompt: What does Article 130 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Seat of Supreme Court
Status: In Force
Mandate: Seat of Supreme Court: Shall sit in Delhi or in such other place or places as the CJI may, with the approval of the President, appoint.
Key Provisions: Enabling provision for circuit benches of SC. CJI has sole discretion (with President's approval); no court can direct CJI to establish a bench elsewhere.
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: No regional benches have been established so far under Art 130.

flashcard 146:
frontside:
Article 131 - PART V - THE UNION | CHAPTER IV. -  THE UNION JUDICIARY
Prompt: What does Article 131 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Original jurisdiction of the Supreme Court
Status: In Force
Mandate: Original jurisdiction of the Supreme Court: Exclusive original jurisdiction in federal disputes: (a) Centre vs one or more States; (b) Centre + State(s) vs other State(s); (c) State vs State.
Key Provisions: Must involve a legal right (not purely political dispute). Exclusions: Pre-constitutional treaties/agreements (Art 131 proviso), inter-state river water disputes (Art 262), Finance Commission matters (Art 280), private citizen vs GoI.
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Inter-State water disputes are EXCLUDED from Art 131 (Parliament enacted Inter-State River Water Disputes Act under Art 262 creating separate tribunals).

flashcard 147:
frontside:
Article 131A - PART V - THE UNION | CHAPTER IV. -  THE UNION JUDICIARY
Prompt: What does Article 131A provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Exclusive jurisdiction of the Supreme Court in regard to questions as to constitutional validity of Central laws - [Omitted]
Status: [Omitted / Repealed]
Mandate: This article has been omitted or repealed from the Constitution (typically by the 7th Amendment Act 1956 or subsequent amendments on federal restructuring/privy purses).
Key Provisions: Retained in constitutional numbering to preserve the integrity of subsequent article references without renumbering the entire Constitution.
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Articles are omitted rather than renumbered to keep historical citations and cross-references constant across statutes.

flashcard 148:
frontside:
Article 132 - PART V - THE UNION | CHAPTER IV. -  THE UNION JUDICIARY
Prompt: What does Article 132 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Appellate jurisdiction of the Supreme Court in appeals from High Courts in certain cases
Status: In Force
Mandate: Appellate jurisdiction of SC in appeals from High Courts in constitutional cases: Substantial question of law as to interpretation of Constitution, certified by HC under Art 134A.
Key Provisions: If HC certifies or SC grants special leave, appeal lies to SC.
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Involves constitutional interpretation, irrespective of civil or criminal nature.

flashcard 149:
frontside:
Article 133 - PART V - THE UNION | CHAPTER IV. -  THE UNION JUDICIARY
Prompt: What does Article 133 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Appellate jurisdiction of the Supreme Court in appeals from High Courts in regard to civil matters
Status: In Force
Mandate: Appellate jurisdiction of SC in civil matters from High Courts: Involves substantial question of law of general importance that needs to be decided by SC, certified under Art 134A.
Key Provisions: 30th Amdt 1972 removed the original monetary valuation threshold (Rs 20,000).
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Certificate of High Court is required.

flashcard 150:
frontside:
Article 134 - PART V - THE UNION | CHAPTER IV. -  THE UNION JUDICIARY
Prompt: What does Article 134 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Appellate jurisdiction of the Supreme Court in regard to criminal matters
Status: In Force
Mandate: Appellate jurisdiction of SC in criminal matters: (a) HC has on appeal reversed acquittal and sentenced accused to death; (b) HC has withdrawn trial from subordinate court and sentenced to death; (c) HC certifies under 134A.
Key Provisions: In death penalty cases under (a) and (b), appeal lies as of right without certificate.
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Parliament can by law enlarge criminal appellate jurisdiction of SC (Art 134(2)).

flashcard 151:
frontside:
Article 134A - PART V - THE UNION | CHAPTER IV. -  THE UNION JUDICIARY
Prompt: What does Article 134A provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Certificate for appeal to the Supreme Court
Status: In Force
Mandate: Certificate for appeal to Supreme Court: HC determines certificate under Arts 132(1), 133(1), 134(1) suo motu or on oral application immediately after passing judgment.
Key Provisions: Inserted by the 44th Constitutional Amendment Act, 1978 to streamline appellate procedure.
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: HC can grant certificate on oral application immediately upon judgment delivery.

flashcard 152:
frontside:
Article 135 - PART V - THE UNION | CHAPTER IV. -  THE UNION JUDICIARY
Prompt: What does Article 135 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Jurisdiction and powers of the Federal Court under existing law to be exercisable by the Supreme Court
Status: In Force
Mandate: Jurisdiction and powers of the Federal Court under existing law to be exercisable by the Supreme Court.
Key Provisions: Transitional bridge ensuring no judicial jurisdiction was lost between Federal Court (1937-1950) and SC.
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Covers matters not falling under Art 133 or 134 but exercisable by Federal Court before commencement.

flashcard 153:
frontside:
Article 136 - PART V - THE UNION | CHAPTER IV. -  THE UNION JUDICIARY
Prompt: What does Article 136 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Special leave to appeal by the Supreme Court
Status: In Force
Mandate: Special Leave to Appeal (SLP): Supreme Court may, in its discretion, grant special leave to appeal from ANY judgment, decree, sentence or order in ANY cause or matter passed by ANY court or tribunal in India.
Key Provisions: Plenary, discretionary, extraordinary jurisdiction. Can be invoked against interlocutory or final orders. Exception: Clause (2) excludes military tribunals and courts-martial.
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: SLP is a discretionary power of the SC, NOT a matter of right for the litigant. Military courts-martial are completely exempt from Art 136.

flashcard 154:
frontside:
Article 137 - PART V - THE UNION | CHAPTER IV. -  THE UNION JUDICIARY
Prompt: What does Article 137 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Review of judgments or orders by the Supreme Court
Status: In Force
Mandate: Review of judgments or orders by the Supreme Court: SC has power to review any judgment pronounced or order made by it, subject to rules under Art 145.
Key Provisions: Supreme Court is not bound by its own previous decisions and can depart from them (Bengal Immunity case 1955). Curative Petition evolved in Rupa Ashok Hurra (2002) after review dismissed.
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Review petition filed within 30 days; considered in chambers without oral arguments unless ordered otherwise.

flashcard 155:
frontside:
Article 138 - PART V - THE UNION | CHAPTER IV. -  THE UNION JUDICIARY
Prompt: What does Article 138 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Enlargement of the jurisdiction of the Supreme Court
Status: In Force
Mandate: Enlargement of jurisdiction of Supreme Court: Parliament may by law confer further jurisdiction regarding Union List matters.
Key Provisions: Can also be enlarged with respect to any matter by special agreement between GoI and a State Government.
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Jurisdiction can only be enlarged, never curtailed, by Parliament.

flashcard 156:
frontside:
Article 139 - PART V - THE UNION | CHAPTER IV. -  THE UNION JUDICIARY
Prompt: What does Article 139 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Conferment on the Supreme Court of powers to issue certain writs
Status: In Force
Mandate: Conferment on Supreme Court of powers to issue certain writs: Parliament may by law empower SC to issue writs for purposes other than enforcement of Fundamental Rights.
Key Provisions: Enabling provision for Parliament to expand SC writ powers beyond Part III.
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Parliament has not exercised this power; SC writ jurisdiction remains confined to Part III (FRs) under Art 32.

flashcard 157:
frontside:
Article 139A - PART V - THE UNION | CHAPTER IV. -  THE UNION JUDICIARY
Prompt: What does Article 139A provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Transfer of certain cases
Status: In Force
Mandate: Transfer of certain cases: SC may withdraw cases involving substantially same questions of law pending before it and High Courts, and dispose of them itself. Also can transfer cases from one HC to another.
Key Provisions: Inserted by 42nd Amdt 1976 and amended by 44th Amdt 1978. Ensures uniformity of constitutional interpretation.
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: SC can act suo motu or on application of Attorney-General or an interested party.

flashcard 158:
frontside:
Article 140 - PART V - THE UNION | CHAPTER IV. -  THE UNION JUDICIARY
Prompt: What does Article 140 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Ancillary powers of the Supreme Court
Status: In Force
Mandate: Ancillary powers of Supreme Court: Parliament may by law confer supplemental powers necessary to enable SC to exercise its jurisdiction effectively.
Key Provisions: Must not be inconsistent with any constitutional provisions.
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Empowers Parliament to augment, not diminish, SC effectiveness.

flashcard 159:
frontside:
Article 141 - PART V - THE UNION | CHAPTER IV. -  THE UNION JUDICIARY
Prompt: What does Article 141 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Law declared by Supreme Court to be binding on all courts
Status: In Force
Mandate: Law declared by Supreme Court to be binding on all courts within the territory of India.
Key Provisions: Constitutional foundation of the Doctrine of Precedent (stare decisis). 'Law declared' includes ratio decidendi and considered obiter dicta. SC itself is NOT bound by its own rulings.
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Binding on ALL courts in India, but does NOT bind the Supreme Court itself.

flashcard 160:
frontside:
Article 142 - PART V - THE UNION | CHAPTER IV. -  THE UNION JUDICIARY
Prompt: What does Article 142 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Enforcement of decrees and orders of the Supreme Court and orders as to discovery, etc
Status: In Force
Mandate: Enforcement of decrees and orders of Supreme Court and orders as to discovery, etc.: SC may pass such decree or order as is necessary for doing COMPLETE JUSTICE in any cause or matter.
Key Provisions: Extraordinary inherent equitable power. Used in Bhopal Gas Tragedy, Ayodhya Title dispute, dissolving irretrievably broken marriages, Vishaka guidelines, environmental protection.
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Art 142 cannot be used to bypass express statutory prohibitions or basic structure (Supreme Court Bar Association v Union of India 1998).

flashcard 161:
frontside:
Article 143 - PART V - THE UNION | CHAPTER IV. -  THE UNION JUDICIARY
Prompt: What does Article 143 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Power of the President to consult the Supreme Court
Status: In Force
Mandate: Power of President to consult Supreme Court (Advisory Jurisdiction): President may refer question of law or fact of public importance for SC opinion.
Key Provisions: Two categories: (1) Art 143(1): question of public importance - SC MAY give opinion or refuse (refused in Ayodhya reference 1993); (2) Art 143(2): pre-constitutional treaties/disputes - SC MUST give opinion (mandatory).
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Opinion given under Art 143 is ADVISORY ONLY and NOT binding on the President; it is also not 'law declared' under Art 141, though entitled to highest respect.

flashcard 162:
frontside:
Article 144 - PART V - THE UNION | CHAPTER IV. -  THE UNION JUDICIARY
Prompt: What does Article 144 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Civil and judicial authorities to act in aid of the Supreme Court
Status: In Force
Mandate: Civil and judicial authorities to act in aid of the Supreme Court: All authorities, civil and judicial, in the territory of India shall act in aid of SC.
Key Provisions: Ensures universal enforceability of Supreme Court judgments throughout India.
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Binding on executive and administrative authorities to execute SC orders.

flashcard 163:
frontside:
Article 144A - PART V - THE UNION | CHAPTER IV. -  THE UNION JUDICIARY
Prompt: What does Article 144A provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Special provisions as to disposal of questions relating to constitutional validity of laws - [Omitted]
Status: [Omitted / Repealed]
Mandate: This article has been omitted or repealed from the Constitution (typically by the 7th Amendment Act 1956 or subsequent amendments on federal restructuring/privy purses).
Key Provisions: Retained in constitutional numbering to preserve the integrity of subsequent article references without renumbering the entire Constitution.
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Articles are omitted rather than renumbered to keep historical citations and cross-references constant across statutes.

flashcard 164:
frontside:
Article 145 - PART V - THE UNION | CHAPTER IV. -  THE UNION JUDICIARY
Prompt: What does Article 145 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Rules of Court, etc
Status: In Force
Mandate: Rules of Court: Supreme Court may, with approval of President, make rules for regulating practice and procedure of Court.
Key Provisions: Constitutional benches (involving substantial question of constitutional interpretation or Art 143 reference) must consist of AT LEAST 5 JUDGES (Art 145(3)).
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Minimum bench for constitutional cases is 5 judges. Judgments delivered in open court with concurrence of majority (dissent permitted).

flashcard 165:
frontside:
Article 146 - PART V - THE UNION | CHAPTER IV. -  THE UNION JUDICIARY
Prompt: What does Article 146 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Officers and servants and the expenses of the Supreme Court
Status: In Force
Mandate: Officers and servants and the expenses of the Supreme Court: Appointed by Chief Justice of India or nominee. Administrative expenses charged on CFI.
Key Provisions: Guarantees administrative and financial autonomy of the Supreme Court.
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Salaries and allowances charged upon Consolidated Fund of India (non-votable).

flashcard 166:
frontside:
Article 147 - PART V - THE UNION | CHAPTER IV. -  THE UNION JUDICIARY
Prompt: What does Article 147 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Interpretation
Status: In Force
Mandate: Interpretation: References to substantial question of law as to interpretation of Constitution include interpretation of GoI Act 1935, Indian Independence Act 1947, or Order in Council.
Key Provisions: Constitutional interpretation definition clause for Chapter IV.
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Historical statutes included for continuity of constitutional interpretation.

flashcard 167:
frontside:
Article 148 - PART V - THE UNION | CHAPTER V. - COMPTROLLER AND AUDITOR-
Prompt: What does Article 148 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Comptroller and Auditor-General of India
Status: In Force
Mandate: Comptroller and Auditor-General of India (CAG): Appointed by President by warrant under hand and seal. 6 yrs tenure or 65 yrs age. Guardian of public purse.
Key Provisions: Independence safeguards: Removed like an SC Judge (proved misbehaviour/incapacity, special majority); Ineligible for further office under GoI/States; Salary/conditions charged on CFI; Salary cannot be varied to disadvantage.
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: CAG is NOT eligible for further office under GoI or any State Government after retirement. Dr. Ambedkar called CAG 'the most important officer under the Constitution of India'.

flashcard 168:
frontside:
Article 149 - PART V - THE UNION | CHAPTER V. - COMPTROLLER AND AUDITOR-
Prompt: What does Article 149 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Duties and powers of the Comptroller and Auditor-General
Status: In Force
Mandate: Duties and powers of the CAG: Prescribed by Parliament by law. Enacted CAG's (Duties, Powers and Conditions of Service) Act, 1971.
Key Provisions: Audits accounts of Union, States, UTs, government companies, statutory corporations, and bodies financed substantially from revenues.
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: In India, CAG is primarily an Auditor-General, not a Comptroller (has no control over the issue of money from the treasury, unlike British CAG).

flashcard 169:
frontside:
Article 150 - PART V - THE UNION | CHAPTER V. - COMPTROLLER AND AUDITOR-
Prompt: What does Article 150 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Form of accounts of the Union and of the States
Status: In Force
Mandate: Form of accounts of the Union and of the States: Prescribed by the President on the advice of the CAG.
Key Provisions: Ensures national accounting uniformity across Union and State levels.
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Prescribed by the PRESIDENT, on the ADVICE of the CAG (42nd/44th Amdt).

flashcard 170:
frontside:
Article 151 - PART V - THE UNION | CHAPTER V. - COMPTROLLER AND AUDITOR-
Prompt: What does Article 151 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Audit reports
Status: In Force
Mandate: Audit reports of the CAG: Reports relating to Union accounts submitted to the PRESIDENT, who causes them to be laid before each House of Parliament. Reports relating to State accounts submitted to the GOVERNOR, who lays them before State Legislature.
Key Provisions: Union audit reports examined by Public Accounts Committee (PAC) of Parliament; State reports by State PAC. CAG acts as 'friend, philosopher and guide' to PAC.
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: CAG submits reports to President/Governor, NOT directly to Parliament/Legislature.

## PART VI - THE STATES

flashcard 171:
frontside:
Article 152 - PART VI - THE STATES | CHAPTER I. -  GENERAL
Prompt: What does Article 152 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Definition
Status: In Force
Mandate: Definition of 'State' for Part VI: In this Part, unless the context otherwise requires, the expression 'State' does not include the State of Jammu and Kashmir (words omitted/modified post-2019 reorganisation).
Key Provisions: Establishes standard territorial applicability of Part VI to States of India.
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Original text excluded J&K due to Art 370; post-2019, J&K is a Union Territory with legislature.

flashcard 172:
frontside:
Article 153 - PART VI - THE STATES | CHAPTER II. -  THE EXECUTIVE
Prompt: What does Article 153 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Governors of States
Status: In Force
Mandate: Governors of States: There shall be a Governor for each State. 7th Amendment Act, 1956 added proviso allowing the SAME person to be appointed Governor for two or more States.
Key Provisions: Dual role: Constitutional head of State + Agent/representative of Central Government.
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: One person can be Governor of multiple States simultaneously (7th Amdt 1956).

flashcard 173:
frontside:
Article 154 - PART VI - THE STATES | CHAPTER II. -  THE EXECUTIVE
Prompt: What does Article 154 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Executive power of State
Status: In Force
Mandate: Executive power of the State is vested in the Governor and exercised either directly or through subordinate officers.
Key Provisions: Subordinate officers include the Chief Minister and Council of Ministers.
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: All executive actions of State Government are formally taken in name of Governor (Art 166).

flashcard 174:
frontside:
Article 155 - PART VI - THE STATES | CHAPTER II. -  THE EXECUTIVE
Prompt: What does Article 155 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Appointment of Governor
Status: In Force
Mandate: Appointment of Governor: Appointed by the PRESIDENT by warrant under his hand and seal.
Key Provisions: Nominee of the Central Government. Constituent Assembly rejected direct election of Governor to prevent clash with Chief Minister.
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Governor is NOT an elected dignitary and NOT an employee of the Central Government (Hargovind Pant case 1979: office is an independent constitutional office).

flashcard 175:
frontside:
Article 156 - PART VI - THE STATES | CHAPTER II. -  THE EXECUTIVE
Prompt: What does Article 156 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Term of office of Governor
Status: In Force
Mandate: Term of office of Governor: Holds office during the PLEASURE of the President. Normal term is 5 years. Resigns to President.
Key Provisions: No security of tenure and no fixed grounds for removal in Constitution. BP Singhal case (2010): Governor cannot be removed arbitrarily or merely because Central government changes; removal is subject to judicial review for mala fides.
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Governor has NO fixed tenure; holds office entirely during the pleasure of the President.

flashcard 176:
frontside:
Article 157 - PART VI - THE STATES | CHAPTER II. -  THE EXECUTIVE
Prompt: What does Article 157 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Qualifications for appointment as Governor
Status: In Force
Mandate: Qualifications for appointment as Governor: (1) Must be a citizen of India; (2) Must have completed 35 years of age.
Key Provisions: Only two constitutional qualifications. Conventions (Sarkaria Commission): (a) Should be an outsider to the state; (b) CM of state should be consulted.
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Conventions (outsider, consulting CM) are NOT constitutional requirements.

flashcard 177:
frontside:
Article 158 - PART VI - THE STATES | CHAPTER II. -  THE EXECUTIVE
Prompt: What does Article 158 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Conditions of Governor - s office
Status: In Force
Mandate: Conditions of Governor's office: Cannot be MP or MLA; no office of profit; official residence rent-free; emoluments cannot be diminished during term.
Key Provisions: When same person is Governor of two or more States, emoluments are allocated between States by President (Art 158(3A)).
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Governor's salary is shared between States in proportion determined by President.

flashcard 178:
frontside:
Article 159 - PART VI - THE STATES | CHAPTER II. -  THE EXECUTIVE
Prompt: What does Article 159 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Oath or affirmation by the Governor
Status: In Force
Mandate: Oath or affirmation by Governor: Administered by the CHIEF JUSTICE of the High Court of the State (or senior-most HC Judge).
Key Provisions: Swears to 'preserve, protect and defend the Constitution and the law' (identical to President's oath in Art 60).
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Governor's oath is administered by Chief Justice of HIGH COURT, not the President of India. Oath is in Art 159, NOT in Third Schedule.

flashcard 179:
frontside:
Article 160 - PART VI - THE STATES | CHAPTER II. -  THE EXECUTIVE
Prompt: What does Article 160 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Discharge of the functions of the Governor in certain contingencies
Status: In Force
Mandate: Discharge of functions of the Governor in certain contingencies: President may make such provision as he thinks fit.
Key Provisions: E.g. Chief Justice of High Court appointed to discharge Governor's functions temporarily on death/resignation.
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: President decides interim arrangements for Governor's office.

flashcard 180:
frontside:
Article 161 - PART VI - THE STATES | CHAPTER II. -  THE EXECUTIVE
Prompt: What does Article 161 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Power of Governor to grant pardons, etc., and to suspend, remit or commute sentences in certain cases
Status: In Force
Mandate: Pardoning power of Governor: Power to grant pardons, reprieves, respites, remissions or commute sentences for offences against State laws.
Key Provisions: Maru Ram (1980) & Epuru Sudhakar (2006): Governor exercises pardoning power on binding advice of State Council of Ministers. Can pardon, remit or commute a death sentence under State law (though death penalty under IPC/BNS is central law).
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Governor CANNOT pardon a death sentence (President alone can pardon death under Art 72); Governor can only suspend, remit or commute death sentence. Governor has NO court-martial power.

flashcard 181:
frontside:
Article 162 - PART VI - THE STATES | CHAPTER II. -  THE EXECUTIVE
Prompt: What does Article 162 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Extent of executive power of State. Council of Ministers
Status: In Force
Mandate: Extent of executive power of State: Co-extensive with legislative power (State List and Concurrent List).
Key Provisions: In Concurrent List, State executive power is subject to and limited by executive power expressly conferred on Union.
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: State executive power is primary in concurrent matters unless Union statute provides otherwise.

flashcard 182:
frontside:
Article 163 - PART VI - THE STATES | CHAPTER II. -  THE EXECUTIVE
Prompt: What does Article 163 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Council of Ministers to aid and advise Governor
Status: In Force
Mandate: Council of Ministers to aid and advise Governor: Except in so far as he is required to exercise functions in his DISCRETION.
Key Provisions: 163(1): Governor has constitutional DISCRETION, unlike President (Art 74 has no discretion clause). 163(2): If any question arises whether a matter is in Governor's discretion, GOVERNOR'S DECISION IS FINAL. 163(3): Advice tendered by Ministers cannot be inquired into by courts.
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Governor has explicit constitutional discretion (Art 163); President has NO explicit textual discretion under Art 74. Discretion must be exercised reasonably, not arbitrarily (Nabam Rebia case 2016).

flashcard 183:
frontside:
Article 164 - PART VI - THE STATES | CHAPTER II. -  THE EXECUTIVE
Prompt: What does Article 164 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Other provisions as to Ministers. The Advocate-General for the State
Status: In Force
Mandate: Chief Minister appointed by Governor; other Ministers appointed on CM's advice. Total Ministers cannot exceed 15% of Assembly (min 12 ministers, 91st Amdt). CoM collectively responsible to Legislative Assembly.
Key Provisions: 164(1) proviso: In Chhattisgarh, Jharkhand, Madhya Pradesh, and Odisha, there shall be a Minister in charge of tribal welfare (Bihar omitted by 94th Amdt 2006). Ministers hold office during pleasure of Governor.
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Minimum 12 ministers rule applies to States (Art 164(1A)), but there is NO minimum number rule for Union Council of Ministers under Art 75.

flashcard 184:
frontside:
Article 165 - PART VI - THE STATES | CHAPTER II. -  THE EXECUTIVE
Prompt: What does Article 165 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Advocate-General for the State. Conduct of Government Business
Status: In Force
Mandate: Advocate-General for the State: Appointed by Governor; must be qualified to be a Judge of High Court (citizen, 10 yrs judicial office or 10 yrs HC advocate).
Key Provisions: Highest law officer of the State. Holds office during PLEASURE of the Governor; remuneration determined by Governor. Has right to speak in State Legislature without right to vote (Art 177).
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Advocate-General holds office during Governor's pleasure; no fixed tenure. Distinguished jurist qualification is NOT available for HC/Advocate-General.

flashcard 185:
frontside:
Article 166 - PART VI - THE STATES | CHAPTER II. -  THE EXECUTIVE
Prompt: What does Article 166 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Conduct of business of the Government of a State
Status: In Force
Mandate: Conduct of business of the Government of a State: All executive action expressed in name of Governor. Governor makes Rules of Business.
Key Provisions: Orders authenticated per rules cannot be questioned on ground that it is not an order made by Governor.
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Ministers and departmental secretaries exercise powers under Rules of Business framed under Art 166(3).

flashcard 186:
frontside:
Article 167 - PART VI - THE STATES | CHAPTER II. -  THE EXECUTIVE
Prompt: What does Article 167 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Duties of Chief Minister as respects the furnishing of information to Governor, etc
Status: In Force
Mandate: Duties of Chief Minister: Communicate Cabinet decisions to Governor; furnish administrative/legislative info called for; submit individual minister's decision to CoM.
Key Provisions: Constitutional communication bridge between State Cabinet and Governor.
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Corresponds exactly to Article 78 for the Prime Minister at the Union level.

flashcard 187:
frontside:
Article 168 - PART VI - THE STATES | CHAPTER III. - THE STATE LEGISLATURE
Prompt: What does Article 168 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Constitution of Legislatures in States
Status: In Force
Mandate: Constitution of Legislatures in States: Consists of Governor + two Houses (bicameral) or Governor + one House (unicameral).
Key Provisions: Currently 6 States have bicameral legislatures: Andhra Pradesh, Bihar, Karnataka, Maharashtra, Telangana, Uttar Pradesh.
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Legislative Councils are optional; only 6 out of 28 States currently have bicameral legislatures.

flashcard 188:
frontside:
Article 169 - PART VI - THE STATES | CHAPTER III. - THE STATE LEGISLATURE
Prompt: What does Article 169 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Abolition or creation of Legislative Councils in States
Status: In Force
Mandate: Abolition or creation of Legislative Councils in States: Parliament may by law abolish or create a Legislative Council.
Key Provisions: Prerequisite: Legislative Assembly must pass resolution by SPECIAL MAJORITY (majority of total membership + 2/3rd present and voting). Parliament then passes law by SIMPLE MAJORITY. Such law is NOT an amendment under Art 368.
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Assembly needs special majority; Parliament needs only SIMPLE majority. State resolution is not binding on Parliament.

flashcard 189:
frontside:
Article 170 - PART VI - THE STATES | CHAPTER III. - THE STATE LEGISLATURE
Prompt: What does Article 170 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Composition of the Legislative Assemblies
Status: In Force
Mandate: Composition of Legislative Assembly (Vidhan Sabha): Max 500, Min 60 members (exceptions: Goa, Mizoram, Sikkim have smaller assemblies by special provisions). Directly elected by adult suffrage.
Key Provisions: Constituency delimitation frozen until post-2026 census (84th Amdt 2001).
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Small state exceptions to minimum 60: Sikkim (32), Goa (40), Mizoram (40), Puducherry (30).

flashcard 190:
frontside:
Article 171 - PART VI - THE STATES | CHAPTER III. - THE STATE LEGISLATURE
Prompt: What does Article 171 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Composition of the Legislative Councils
Status: In Force
Mandate: Composition of Legislative Council (Vidhan Parishad): Max 1/3rd of Assembly strength; Min 40 members. Permanent body, 1/3rd retire every 2 years.
Key Provisions: 5 electoral streams: (1) 1/3rd elected by Local bodies (municipalities/panchayats); (2) 1/12th elected by 3-yr Graduates; (3) 1/12th elected by Teachers (>=3 yrs secondary); (4) 1/3rd elected by MLAs; (5) 1/6th nominated by Governor (Literature, Science, Art, Co-operative movement, Social service).
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Governor's nomination includes 'Co-operative movement' as 5th field; President's nomination to Rajya Sabha under Art 80 has only 4 fields (no co-operation).

flashcard 191:
frontside:
Article 172 - PART VI - THE STATES | CHAPTER III. - THE STATE LEGISLATURE
Prompt: What does Article 172 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Duration of State Legislatures
Status: In Force
Mandate: Duration of State Legislatures: Assembly = 5 years (extendable during emergency by 1 yr at a time). Council = permanent (not subject to dissolution, 6-yr term).
Key Provisions: Assembly dissolution can be done earlier by Governor on CM's advice.
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Council is permanent like Rajya Sabha; Assembly is dissolvable like Lok Sabha.

flashcard 192:
frontside:
Article 173 - PART VI - THE STATES | CHAPTER III. - THE STATE LEGISLATURE
Prompt: What does Article 173 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Qualification for membership of the State Legislature
Status: In Force
Mandate: Qualifications for MLA/MLC: Citizen of India, age >= 25 years for Assembly, age >= 30 years for Council, oath before EC-authorised person.
Key Provisions: Matches Lok Sabha (25) and Rajya Sabha (30) age criteria.
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Panchayat/Municipality minimum age is 21; Assembly is 25; Council is 30.

flashcard 193:
frontside:
Article 174 - PART VI - THE STATES | CHAPTER III. - THE STATE LEGISLATURE
Prompt: What does Article 174 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Sessions of the State Legislature, prorogation and dissolution
Status: In Force
Mandate: Sessions of State Legislature, prorogation and dissolution: Governor summons, prorogues Houses, and dissolves Assembly.
Key Provisions: Max gap between two sessions cannot exceed 6 MONTHS. Governor must act on advice of CoM except in rare situations of loss of majority (Nabam Rebia case).
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Governor's power to summon/prorogue is normally exercisable only on CM/CoM advice.

flashcard 194:
frontside:
Article 175 - PART VI - THE STATES | CHAPTER III. - THE STATE LEGISLATURE
Prompt: What does Article 175 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Right of Governor to address and send messages to the House or Houses
Status: In Force
Mandate: Right of Governor to address and send messages to House or Houses of State Legislature.
Key Provisions: Legislature must consider messages sent by Governor.
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Corresponds to Article 86 for the President.

flashcard 195:
frontside:
Article 176 - PART VI - THE STATES | CHAPTER III. - THE STATE LEGISLATURE
Prompt: What does Article 176 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Special address by the Governor
Status: In Force
Mandate: Special address by Governor: At commencement of first session after general election and first session of each year.
Key Provisions: Outlines government's policies and legislative agenda; followed by Motion of Thanks.
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Corresponds to Article 87 for the President.

flashcard 196:
frontside:
Article 177 - PART VI - THE STATES | CHAPTER III. - THE STATE LEGISLATURE
Prompt: What does Article 177 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
