Title: Rights of Ministers and Advocate-General as respects the Houses. Officers of the State Legislature
Status: In Force
Mandate: Rights of Ministers and Advocate-General as respects the Houses: Can speak and participate in either House or committee, but CANNOT vote unless a member.
Key Provisions: Advocate-General can speak in both Assembly and Council, but has NO right to vote.
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Matches Article 88 of Parliament.

flashcard 197:
frontside:
Article 178 - PART VI - THE STATES | CHAPTER III. - THE STATE LEGISLATURE
Prompt: What does Article 178 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: The Speaker and Deputy Speaker of the Legislative Assembly
Status: In Force
Mandate: Speaker and Deputy Speaker of Legislative Assembly: Elected by Assembly from among its members.
Key Provisions: Presiding officers representing the Assembly.
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Corresponds to Article 93 of Lok Sabha.

flashcard 198:
frontside:
Article 179 - PART VI - THE STATES | CHAPTER III. - THE STATE LEGISLATURE
Prompt: What does Article 179 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Vacation and resignation of, and removal from, the offices of Speaker and Deputy Speaker
Status: In Force
Mandate: Vacation, resignation and removal of Speaker and Deputy Speaker of Assembly.
Key Provisions: Speaker resigns to Deputy Speaker; removed by effective majority after 14 days notice. Does not vacate on dissolution until first meeting of new Assembly.
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Matches Article 94 rules for Lok Sabha Speaker.

flashcard 199:
frontside:
Article 180 - PART VI - THE STATES | CHAPTER III. - THE STATE LEGISLATURE
Prompt: What does Article 180 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Power of the Deputy Speaker or other person to perform the duties of the office of, or to act as, Speaker
Status: In Force
Mandate: Power of Deputy Speaker or other person to perform duties of office of, or to act as, Speaker.
Key Provisions: Discharges duties during vacancy or absence of Speaker.
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Matches Article 95 of Lok Sabha.

flashcard 200:
frontside:
Article 181 - PART VI - THE STATES | CHAPTER III. - THE STATE LEGISLATURE
Prompt: What does Article 181 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: The Speaker or the Deputy Speaker not to preside while a resolution for his removal from office is under consideration
Status: In Force
Mandate: Speaker or Deputy Speaker not to preside while resolution for his removal is under consideration.
Key Provisions: Has right to speak and vote in first instance, but not casting vote.
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Matches Article 96 of Lok Sabha.

flashcard 201:
frontside:
Article 182 - PART VI - THE STATES | CHAPTER III. - THE STATE LEGISLATURE
Prompt: What does Article 182 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: The Chairman and Deputy Chairman of the Legislative Council
Status: In Force
Mandate: Chairman and Deputy Chairman of Legislative Council: Both elected by the Council from among its members.
Key Provisions: Unlike Rajya Sabha (where Chairman is VP), the Chairman of Legislative Council IS an elected member of the Council.
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Chairman of Council is an MLC elected by MLCs; Chairman of RS is NOT an MP.

flashcard 202:
frontside:
Article 183 - PART VI - THE STATES | CHAPTER III. - THE STATE LEGISLATURE
Prompt: What does Article 183 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Vacation and resignation of, and removal from, the offices of Chairman and Deputy Chairman
Status: In Force
Mandate: Vacation, resignation and removal of Chairman and Deputy Chairman of Legislative Council.
Key Provisions: Resignation addressed to each other; removed by effective majority after 14 days notice.
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Matches internal council officer rules.

flashcard 203:
frontside:
Article 184 - PART VI - THE STATES | CHAPTER III. - THE STATE LEGISLATURE
Prompt: What does Article 184 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Power of the Deputy Chairman or other person to perform the duties of the office of, or to act as, Chairman
Status: In Force
Mandate: Power of Deputy Chairman or other person to act as Chairman of Council during vacancy/absence.
Key Provisions: Ensures continuous presiding officer in Council.
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Matches Article 91 of Rajya Sabha.

flashcard 204:
frontside:
Article 185 - PART VI - THE STATES | CHAPTER III. - THE STATE LEGISLATURE
Prompt: What does Article 185 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: The Chairman or the Deputy Chairman not to preside while a resolution for his removal from office is under consideration
Status: In Force
Mandate: Chairman or Deputy Chairman not to preside while removal resolution is under consideration.
Key Provisions: Chairman Council CAN vote in first instance on his removal (since he is an MLC), unlike Chairman of RS.
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Chairman of Council can vote in first instance; Chairman of Rajya Sabha cannot.

flashcard 205:
frontside:
Article 186 - PART VI - THE STATES | CHAPTER III. - THE STATE LEGISLATURE
Prompt: What does Article 186 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Salaries and allowances of the Speaker and Deputy Speaker and the Chairman and Deputy Chairman
Status: In Force
Mandate: Salaries and allowances of Speaker, Deputy Speaker, Chairman, and Deputy Chairman of State Legislature.
Key Provisions: Charged on the Consolidated Fund of the State (non-votable).
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Specified in Second Schedule and determined by State Legislature by law.

flashcard 206:
frontside:
Article 187 - PART VI - THE STATES | CHAPTER III. - THE STATE LEGISLATURE
Prompt: What does Article 187 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Secretariat of State Legislature. Conduct of Business
Status: In Force
Mandate: Secretariat of State Legislature: Separate secretarial staff for each House, independent of executive.
Key Provisions: Regulated by law made by State Legislature.
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Corresponds to Article 98 of Parliament.

flashcard 207:
frontside:
Article 188 - PART VI - THE STATES | CHAPTER III. - THE STATE LEGISLATURE
Prompt: What does Article 188 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Oath or affirmation by members
Status: In Force
Mandate: Oath or affirmation by members of State Legislature before Governor or person appointed by him.
Key Provisions: Form in Third Schedule. Mandatory before sitting or voting.
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Penalty of Rs 500 per day under Art 193 for sitting/voting without oath.

flashcard 208:
frontside:
Article 189 - PART VI - THE STATES | CHAPTER III. - THE STATE LEGISLATURE
Prompt: What does Article 189 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Voting in Houses, power of Houses to act notwithstanding vacancies and quorum. Disqualifications of Members
Status: In Force
Mandate: Voting in Houses, power to act notwithstanding vacancies, and quorum in State Legislature.
Key Provisions: Quorum = 10 members OR 1/10th of total membership of the House, WHICHEVER IS GREATER (Art 189(3)).
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Parliament quorum is strictly 1/10th (Art 100); State Legislature quorum is 10 members OR 1/10th, whichever is greater.

flashcard 209:
frontside:
Article 190 - PART VI - THE STATES | CHAPTER III. - THE STATE LEGISLATURE
Prompt: What does Article 190 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Vacation of seats
Status: In Force
Mandate: Vacation of seats in State Legislature: Ban on dual membership (both Houses or multiple States). 60 days continuous absence without permission vacates seat.
Key Provisions: Resignation must be voluntary and genuine (33rd Amdt 1974).
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: 60-day rule excludes periods of prorogation/adjournment > 4 consecutive days.

flashcard 210:
frontside:
Article 191 - PART VI - THE STATES | CHAPTER III. - THE STATE LEGISLATURE
Prompt: What does Article 191 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Disqualifications for membership
Status: In Force
Mandate: Disqualifications for membership of Assembly or Council: Office of profit, unsound mind, insolvent, non-citizen, statutory disqualification (RPA 1951), and Tenth Schedule (Anti-defection).
Key Provisions: Corresponds to Article 102 for MPs.
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Disqualification for anti-defection is decided by Speaker/Chairman under Tenth Schedule.

flashcard 211:
frontside:
Article 192 - PART VI - THE STATES | CHAPTER III. - THE STATE LEGISLATURE
Prompt: What does Article 192 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Decision on questions as to disqualifications of members
Status: In Force
Mandate: Decision on questions as to disqualifications of members (under Art 191(1)): Referred to the GOVERNOR, whose decision is final.
Key Provisions: Governor MUST obtain opinion of the ELECTION COMMISSION and act according to such opinion (binding).
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Governor acts on binding advice of EC for Art 191(1); Speaker decides Tenth Schedule anti-defection.

flashcard 212:
frontside:
Article 193 - PART VI - THE STATES | CHAPTER III. - THE STATE LEGISLATURE
Prompt: What does Article 193 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Penalty for sitting and voting before making oath or affirmation under article 188 or when not qualified or when disqualified. Powers, privileges and immunities of State Legislatures and their Members
Status: In Force
Mandate: Penalty for sitting and voting before making oath under Art 188 or when disqualified: Rs 500 per day, recoverable as debt to State.
Key Provisions: Corresponds to Article 104 of Parliament.
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Same Rs 500 daily penalty as Parliament.

flashcard 213:
frontside:
Article 194 - PART VI - THE STATES | CHAPTER III. - THE STATE LEGISLATURE
Prompt: What does Article 194 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Powers, privileges, etc., of the Houses of Legislatures and of the members and committees thereof
Status: In Force
Mandate: Powers, privileges and immunities of State Legislatures and their members and committees.
Key Provisions: 194(1) Freedom of speech; 194(2) Absolute immunity from court for speech/votes in Legislature or committees. Sita Soren (2024): Bribery for vote is NOT protected.
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Corresponds exactly to Article 105 of Parliament.

flashcard 214:
frontside:
Article 195 - PART VI - THE STATES | CHAPTER III. - THE STATE LEGISLATURE
Prompt: What does Article 195 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Salaries and allowances of members. Legislative Procedure
Status: In Force
Mandate: Salaries and allowances of members of Legislative Assembly and Council determined by State Legislature by law.
Key Provisions: Votable expenditure of the State.
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Determined by State Legislature itself.

flashcard 215:
frontside:
Article 196 - PART VI - THE STATES | CHAPTER III. - THE STATE LEGISLATURE
Prompt: What does Article 196 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Provisions as to introduction and passing of Bills
Status: In Force
Mandate: Provisions as to introduction and passing of Bills in State Legislature.
Key Provisions: Lapse rules: Bill pending in Assembly lapses; Bill passed by Assembly pending in Council lapses; Bill pending in Council not passed by Assembly does NOT lapse.
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Matches parliamentary lapse principles.

flashcard 216:
frontside:
Article 197 - PART VI - THE STATES | CHAPTER III. - THE STATE LEGISLATURE
Prompt: What does Article 197 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Restriction on powers of Legislative Council as to Bills other than Money Bills
Status: In Force
Mandate: Restriction on powers of Legislative Council as to Bills other than Money Bills.
Key Provisions: Council CANNOT reject an ordinary bill passed by Assembly. In first instance, Council can delay for max 3 MONTHS. If Assembly passes bill again, Council can delay for max 1 MONTH. Total maximum delay = 4 MONTHS. Thereafter, bill is deemed passed by both Houses in form passed by Assembly.
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: THERE IS NO JOINT SITTING IN STATE LEGISLATURES (Art 108 exists only for Parliament). Legislative Assembly has ultimate supremacy; Council is only a dilatory chamber (max 4 months delay).

flashcard 217:
frontside:
Article 198 - PART VI - THE STATES | CHAPTER III. - THE STATE LEGISLATURE
Prompt: What does Article 198 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Special procedure in respect of Money Bills
Status: In Force
Mandate: Special procedure in respect of Money Bills in State Legislature: Introduced only in Assembly on Governor's recommendation. Council has 14 DAYS to return.
Key Provisions: Assembly can accept or reject Council's recommendations. If not returned in 14 days, deemed passed.
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Matches Lok Sabha/Rajya Sabha procedure; Council has only 14 days delay power.

flashcard 218:
frontside:
Article 199 - PART VI - THE STATES | CHAPTER III. - THE STATE LEGISLATURE
Prompt: What does Article 199 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Definition of  - Money Bills - 
Status: In Force
Mandate: Definition of 'Money Bill' in State Legislature: Deals only with state taxes, borrowing, custody/appropriation of State Consolidated Fund.
Key Provisions: Speaker of Assembly certifies Money Bill; certificate is final.
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Corresponds to Article 110 of Parliament.

flashcard 219:
frontside:
Article 200 - PART VI - THE STATES | CHAPTER III. - THE STATE LEGISLATURE
Prompt: What does Article 200 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Assent to Bills
Status: In Force
Mandate: Assent to Bills by Governor: When bill passed, presented to Governor who has 4 options: (1) Assent; (2) Withhold assent; (3) Return for reconsideration (if not Money Bill); (4) RESERVE THE BILL FOR CONSIDERATION OF THE PRESIDENT.
Key Provisions: Mandatory reservation: Governor MUST reserve any bill which in his opinion so derogates from powers of the High Court as to endanger its constitutional position. Shamsher Singh & Tamil Nadu/Punjab Governor rulings (2023): Governor cannot sit indefinitely on bills.
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Reservation for President is discretionary, but mandatory if High Court's powers are endangered. If returned bill is repassed, Governor MUST assent (unless reserved).

flashcard 220:
frontside:
Article 201 - PART VI - THE STATES | CHAPTER III. - THE STATE LEGISLATURE
Prompt: What does Article 201 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Bills reserved for consideration. Procedure in Financial Matters
Status: In Force
Mandate: Bills reserved for consideration of the President: President may: (a) Assent; (b) Withhold assent; (c) Direct Governor to return bill to State Legislature for reconsideration.
Key Provisions: State Legislature must reconsider within 6 MONTHS. If passed again, presented again to PRESIDENT. CRITICAL: President is NOT bound to give assent even if the bill is passed again by State Legislature.
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Unlike Parliament bills (where President must assent on repassing under Art 111), for State bills under Art 201 the President can WITHHOLD ASSENT even on a repassed bill.

flashcard 221:
frontside:
Article 202 - PART VI - THE STATES | CHAPTER III. - THE STATE LEGISLATURE
Prompt: What does Article 202 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Annual financial statement
Status: In Force
Mandate: Annual Financial Statement of State (State Budget): Laid before House(s) by Governor. Distinguishes charged expenditure from votable expenditure.
Key Provisions: Corresponds to Article 112 for the Union.
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Charged on Consolidated Fund of State: Governor's emoluments, HC Judges' salaries/allowances, Speaker/Chairman salaries, debt charges.

flashcard 222:
frontside:
Article 203 - PART VI - THE STATES | CHAPTER III. - THE STATE LEGISLATURE
Prompt: What does Article 203 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Procedure in Legislature with respect to estimates
Status: In Force
Mandate: Procedure in Legislature with respect to estimates: Charged discussed but not voted. Demands for grants voted ONLY by Legislative Assembly.
Key Provisions: Legislative Council has no power to vote on demands for grants.
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Matches Lok Sabha exclusivity under Art 113.

flashcard 223:
frontside:
Article 204 - PART VI - THE STATES | CHAPTER III. - THE STATE LEGISLATURE
Prompt: What does Article 204 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Appropriation Bills
Status: In Force
Mandate: Appropriation Bills in State Legislature: Required to withdraw money from Consolidated Fund of the State.
Key Provisions: No money withdrawn without enacted Appropriation Act.
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Matches Article 114 of Parliament.

flashcard 224:
frontside:
Article 205 - PART VI - THE STATES | CHAPTER III. - THE STATE LEGISLATURE
Prompt: What does Article 205 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Supplementary, additional or excess grants
Status: In Force
Mandate: Supplementary, additional or excess grants in State Legislature.
Key Provisions: Corresponds to Article 115 of Parliament.
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Excess grant examined by State PAC before regularisation.

flashcard 225:
frontside:
Article 206 - PART VI - THE STATES | CHAPTER III. - THE STATE LEGISLATURE
Prompt: What does Article 206 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Votes on account, votes of credit and exceptional grants
Status: In Force
Mandate: Votes on account, votes of credit and exceptional grants in State Legislature.
Key Provisions: Corresponds to Article 116 of Parliament.
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Provides interim spending authority.

flashcard 226:
frontside:
Article 207 - PART VI - THE STATES | CHAPTER III. - THE STATE LEGISLATURE
Prompt: What does Article 207 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Special provisions as to financial Bills. Procedure Generally
Status: In Force
Mandate: Special provisions as to Financial Bills in State Legislature.
Key Provisions: Introduced only in Assembly on Governor's recommendation.
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Corresponds to Article 117 of Parliament.

flashcard 227:
frontside:
Article 208 - PART VI - THE STATES | CHAPTER III. - THE STATE LEGISLATURE
Prompt: What does Article 208 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Rules of procedure
Status: In Force
Mandate: Rules of procedure in State Legislature: Each House makes rules for regulating procedure and conduct of business.
Key Provisions: Corresponds to Article 118 of Parliament.
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Independent rule-making power for each House.

flashcard 228:
frontside:
Article 209 - PART VI - THE STATES | CHAPTER III. - THE STATE LEGISLATURE
Prompt: What does Article 209 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Regulation by law of procedure in the Legislature of the State in relation to financial business
Status: In Force
Mandate: Regulation by law of procedure in State Legislature in relation to financial business.
Key Provisions: Statutory law overrides internal rules for timely budget passing.
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Corresponds to Article 119.

flashcard 229:
frontside:
Article 210 - PART VI - THE STATES | CHAPTER III. - THE STATE LEGISLATURE
Prompt: What does Article 210 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Language to be used in the Legislature
Status: In Force
Mandate: Language to be used in State Legislature: Official language(s) of State or Hindi or English. Speaker/Chairman may permit member to speak in mother tongue.
Key Provisions: Corresponds to Article 120 of Parliament.
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Official language of the State is primary.

flashcard 230:
frontside:
Article 211 - PART VI - THE STATES | CHAPTER III. - THE STATE LEGISLATURE
Prompt: What does Article 211 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Restriction on discussion in the Legislature
Status: In Force
Mandate: Restriction on discussion in State Legislature: ABSOLUTE BAR on discussing conduct of any Judge of Supreme Court or High Court in discharge of duties.
Key Provisions: Notice the critical difference with Parliament: Parliament can discuss a judge's conduct during impeachment (Art 121); State Legislature CANNOT discuss a judge's conduct UNDER ANY CIRCUMSTANCES (no impeachment power in States).
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: State Legislature has NO impeachment power over judges; bar on discussion under Art 211 is completely absolute.

flashcard 231:
frontside:
Article 212 - PART VI - THE STATES | CHAPTER III. - THE STATE LEGISLATURE
Prompt: What does Article 212 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Courts not to inquire into proceedings of the Legislature
Status: In Force
Mandate: Courts not to inquire into proceedings of State Legislature on ground of alleged irregularity of procedure.
Key Provisions: Officers/MLAs immune from court jurisdiction for exercising constitutional procedural powers.
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Corresponds to Article 122 of Parliament.

flashcard 232:
frontside:
Article 213 - PART VI - THE STATES | CHAPTER IV. - LEGISLATIVE POWER OF THE
Prompt: What does Article 213 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Power of Governor to promulgate Ordinances during recess of Legislature
Status: In Force
Mandate: Power of Governor to promulgate Ordinances during recess of Legislature: When Assembly (or either House in bicameral) not in session and Governor satisfied immediate action needed.
Key Provisions: Same force as Act of Legislature. Ceases to operate at EXPIRATION OF 6 WEEKS from reassembly of Legislature (or earlier if disapproved). Proviso: Governor CANNOT promulgate without PRESIDENT'S INSTRUCTIONS if: (a) Bill containing same provisions would have required President's prior sanction; (b) Would have deemed necessary to reserve for President; (c) Act of Legislature would have been invalid without President's assent.
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Governor needs President's prior instructions for specified categories of ordinances. Cannot be used to bypass Legislature (DC Wadhwa case 1987).

flashcard 233:
frontside:
Article 214 - PART VI - THE STATES | CHAPTER V. - THE HIGH COURTS IN THE
Prompt: What does Article 214 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: High Courts for States
Status: In Force
Mandate: High Courts for States: There shall be a High Court for each State.
Key Provisions: Parliament may establish a common High Court for two or more States/UTs under Art 231 (e.g. Punjab & Haryana HC, Guwahati HC, Bombay HC).
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Currently 25 High Courts serve 28 States and 8 UTs.

flashcard 234:
frontside:
Article 215 - PART VI - THE STATES | CHAPTER V. - THE HIGH COURTS IN THE
Prompt: What does Article 215 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: High Courts to be courts of record
Status: In Force
Mandate: High Courts to be Courts of Record: Shall have all powers of such a court including power to punish for contempt of itself.
Key Provisions: Corresponds to Article 129 for Supreme Court.
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: High Court's contempt jurisdiction is a constitutional power that cannot be taken away by state law.

flashcard 235:
frontside:
Article 216 - PART VI - THE STATES | CHAPTER V. - THE HIGH COURTS IN THE
Prompt: What does Article 216 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Constitution of High Courts
Status: In Force
Mandate: Constitution of High Courts: Consists of a Chief Justice and such other Judges as the PRESIDENT may from time to time deem necessary.
Key Provisions: Constitution does NOT fix a maximum strength for High Courts; determined by President based on workload.
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: SC strength is fixed by PARLIAMENT by law (Art 124); HC strength is determined by the PRESIDENT (executive discretion).

flashcard 236:
frontside:
Article 217 - PART VI - THE STATES | CHAPTER V. - THE HIGH COURTS IN THE
Prompt: What does Article 217 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Appointment and conditions of the office of a Judge of a High Court
Status: In Force
Mandate: Appointment and conditions of office of High Court Judge: Appointed by PRESIDENT by warrant under hand and seal after consultation with CJI, Governor of State, and Chief Justice of HC (collegium: CJI + 2 senior SC judges).
Key Provisions: Qualifications: Citizen, 10 yrs judicial office in India OR 10 yrs advocate of High Court. Retirement age: 62 YEARS (raised from 60 by 15th Amdt 1963). Removal: Same procedure and grounds as SC Judge under Art 124(4) (special majority of Parliament).
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Distinguished jurist CANNOT be appointed HC judge (that category exists only for SC under Art 124). Retirement age is 62 (SC is 65). Appointed by PRESIDENT, not Governor.

flashcard 237:
frontside:
Article 218 - PART VI - THE STATES | CHAPTER V. - THE HIGH COURTS IN THE
Prompt: What does Article 218 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Application of certain provisions relating to Supreme Court to High Courts
Status: In Force
Mandate: Application of certain provisions relating to Supreme Court to High Courts: Applies Art 124(4)-(5) (removal procedure) to HC judges.
Key Provisions: High Court judges can only be removed by President after Parliamentary address.
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: State Legislature has zero role in removing a High Court judge.

flashcard 238:
frontside:
Article 219 - PART VI - THE STATES | CHAPTER V. - THE HIGH COURTS IN THE
Prompt: What does Article 219 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Oath or affirmation by Judges of High Courts
Status: In Force
Mandate: Oath or affirmation by Judges of High Courts: Administered by the GOVERNOR of the State or person appointed by him.
Key Provisions: Form set out in Third Schedule.
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: HC Judges are APPOINTED by the President (Art 217), but take OATH before the Governor (Art 219).

flashcard 239:
frontside:
Article 220 - PART VI - THE STATES | CHAPTER V. - THE HIGH COURTS IN THE
Prompt: What does Article 220 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Restriction on practice after being a permanent Judge
Status: In Force
Mandate: Restriction on practice after being a permanent Judge of High Court: Cannot plead/act in any court/authority except the Supreme Court and other High Courts.
Key Provisions: Barred from practicing in subordinate courts or in the same High Court where served permanently.
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Can practice in SC and OTHER High Courts, but not in the same HC or subordinate courts.

flashcard 240:
frontside:
Article 221 - PART VI - THE STATES | CHAPTER V. - THE HIGH COURTS IN THE
Prompt: What does Article 221 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Salaries, etc., of Judges
Status: In Force
Mandate: Salaries, allowances, and pensions of High Court Judges determined by Parliament.
Key Provisions: CRITICAL SPLIT: Salaries and allowances of HC Judges are charged upon the CONSOLIDATED FUND OF THE STATE (Art 202). BUT PENSIONS of HC Judges are charged upon the CONSOLIDATED FUND OF INDIA (Art 112(3)(d)(iii)).
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Highest-yield UPSC trap: HC Judge Salary = State CFI; HC Judge Pension = Union CFI (because judges may serve across multiple states).

flashcard 241:
frontside:
Article 222 - PART VI - THE STATES | CHAPTER V. - THE HIGH COURTS IN THE
Prompt: What does Article 222 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Transfer of a Judge from one High Court to another
Status: In Force
Mandate: Transfer of a Judge from one High Court to another: President may, after consultation with Chief Justice of India, transfer an HC Judge.
Key Provisions: Third Judges Case (1998): CJI must consult collegium of 4 senior-most SC judges + Chief Justices of both HCs (sending and receiving). Transferred judge entitled to compensatory allowance.
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Judicial transfer cannot be used as a punitive tool (Union of India v Sankalchand Sheth 1977).

flashcard 242:
frontside:
Article 223 - PART VI - THE STATES | CHAPTER V. - THE HIGH COURTS IN THE
Prompt: What does Article 223 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Appointment of acting Chief Justice
Status: In Force
Mandate: Appointment of Acting Chief Justice of High Court: Appointed by President from among HC judges when CJ office is vacant/absent.
Key Provisions: Corresponds to Article 126 for Supreme Court.
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Appointed by President, not Governor.

flashcard 243:
frontside:
Article 224 - PART VI - THE STATES | CHAPTER V. - THE HIGH COURTS IN THE
Prompt: What does Article 224 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Appointment of additional and acting Judges
Status: In Force
Mandate: Appointment of additional and acting Judges of High Court: Appointed by President for temporary increase in business (max 2 years) or temporary absence.
Key Provisions: Cannot hold office after reaching 62 YEARS of age.
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Additional and acting judges exist in High Courts (Art 224), NOT in Supreme Court (SC has ad hoc judges under Art 127).

flashcard 244:
frontside:
Article 224A - PART VI - THE STATES | CHAPTER V. - THE HIGH COURTS IN THE
Prompt: What does Article 224A provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Appointment of retired Judges at sittings of High Courts
Status: In Force
Mandate: Appointment of retired Judges at sittings of High Courts: Chief Justice of HC with previous consent of President may request retired HC judge to sit and act.
Key Provisions: Inserted by 15th Amdt 1963. Revived recently to clear mounting judicial arrears.
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Requires consent of the retired judge concerned.

flashcard 245:
frontside:
Article 225 - PART VI - THE STATES | CHAPTER V. - THE HIGH COURTS IN THE
Prompt: What does Article 225 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Jurisdiction of existing High Courts
Status: In Force
Mandate: Jurisdiction of existing High Courts: Preserves pre-constitutional jurisdiction and rule-making powers of High Courts.
Key Provisions: Removed pre-independence restriction on revenue jurisdiction.
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Preserves historical continuity of High Court authority.

flashcard 246:
frontside:
Article 226 - PART VI - THE STATES | CHAPTER V. - THE HIGH COURTS IN THE
Prompt: What does Article 226 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Power of High Courts to issue certain writs
Status: In Force
Mandate: Power of High Courts to issue certain writs: HC has power throughout its territorial jurisdiction to issue directions, orders or writs (Habeas Corpus, Mandamus, Prohibition, Quo Warranto, Certiorari) for enforcement of Fundamental Rights AND FOR ANY OTHER PURPOSE.
Key Provisions: CRITICAL COMPARISON WITH ART 32: (1) Art 226 is WIDER than Art 32 because it extends to FRs AND ordinary legal rights ('for any other purpose'); (2) Art 32 is itself a Fundamental Right (SC cannot refuse), whereas Art 226 is a discretionary constitutional remedy (HC may decline if alternative remedy exists); (3) Art 226(2) allows HC to issue writs outside its territory if CAUSE OF ACTION arises within territory. Basic structure (Chandra Kumar 1997).
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Art 226 is wider in scope than Art 32, but narrower in territorial reach (SC reaches all of India). Art 226 is discretionary; Art 32 is a guaranteed fundamental right.

flashcard 247:
frontside:
Article 226A - PART VI - THE STATES | CHAPTER V. - THE HIGH COURTS IN THE
Prompt: What does Article 226A provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Constitutional validity of Central laws not to be considered in proceedings under article 226 - [Omitted]
Status: [Omitted / Repealed]
Mandate: This article has been omitted or repealed from the Constitution (typically by the 7th Amendment Act 1956 or subsequent amendments on federal restructuring/privy purses).
Key Provisions: Retained in constitutional numbering to preserve the integrity of subsequent article references without renumbering the entire Constitution.
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Articles are omitted rather than renumbered to keep historical citations and cross-references constant across statutes.

flashcard 248:
frontside:
Article 227 - PART VI - THE STATES | CHAPTER V. - THE HIGH COURTS IN THE
Prompt: What does Article 227 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Power of superintendence over all courts by the High Court
Status: In Force
Mandate: Power of superintendence over all courts and tribunals by the High Court: Every HC has administrative and judicial superintendence over all courts and tribunals throughout its territory.
Key Provisions: Can call for returns, make general rules, regulate fees. Covers both judicial and administrative supervision. Exception: Clause (4) excludes military courts-martial.
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Superintendence under Art 227 extends to TRIBUNALS within state territory (except military tribunals); wider than ordinary revision power.

flashcard 249:
frontside:
Article 228 - PART VI - THE STATES | CHAPTER V. - THE HIGH COURTS IN THE
Prompt: What does Article 228 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Transfer of certain cases to High Court
Status: In Force
Mandate: Transfer of certain cases to High Court: If HC satisfied pending case in subordinate court involves substantial question of constitutional law, HC shall withdraw case and dispose of it or determine question and return.
Key Provisions: Ensures constitutional questions are decided by superior judiciary.
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Subordinate courts cannot interpret constitutional validity; HC withdraws the question.

flashcard 250:
frontside:
Article 228A - PART VI - THE STATES | CHAPTER V. - THE HIGH COURTS IN THE
Prompt: What does Article 228A provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Special provisions as to disposal of questions relating to constitutional validity of State laws - [Omitted]
Status: [Omitted / Repealed]
Mandate: This article has been omitted or repealed from the Constitution (typically by the 7th Amendment Act 1956 or subsequent amendments on federal restructuring/privy purses).
Key Provisions: Retained in constitutional numbering to preserve the integrity of subsequent article references without renumbering the entire Constitution.
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Articles are omitted rather than renumbered to keep historical citations and cross-references constant across statutes.

flashcard 251:
frontside:
Article 229 - PART VI - THE STATES | CHAPTER V. - THE HIGH COURTS IN THE
Prompt: What does Article 229 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Officers and servants and the expenses of High Courts
Status: In Force
Mandate: Officers and servants and the expenses of High Courts: Appointed by Chief Justice of HC. Administrative expenses charged on Consolidated Fund of State.
Key Provisions: Protects institutional independence of High Courts from state executive interference.
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Salaries charged on Consolidated Fund of State (non-votable).

flashcard 252:
frontside:
Article 230 - PART VI - THE STATES | CHAPTER V. - THE HIGH COURTS IN THE
Prompt: What does Article 230 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Extension of jurisdiction of High Courts to Union territories
Status: In Force
Mandate: Extension of jurisdiction of High Courts to Union territories: Parliament may by law extend or exclude HC jurisdiction for any UT.
Key Provisions: E.g. Calcutta HC over Andaman & Nicobar; Kerala HC over Lakshadweep; Bombay HC over Dadra & Nagar Haveli and Daman & Diu; Punjab & Haryana HC over Chandigarh.
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Delhi and J&K are the only UTs with their own independent High Courts.

flashcard 253:
frontside:
Article 231 - PART VI - THE STATES | CHAPTER V. - THE HIGH COURTS IN THE
Prompt: What does Article 231 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Establishment of a common High Court for two or more States
Status: In Force
Mandate: Establishment of a common High Court for two or more States: Parliament may by law establish a common HC for two or more States or States and a UT.
Key Provisions: E.g. Bombay HC (Maharashtra & Goa); Punjab and Haryana HC (Punjab & Haryana & Chandigarh); Guwahati HC (Assam, Nagaland, Mizoram, Arunachal Pradesh).
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Common High Court can be created ONLY by law made by PARLIAMENT, not by state agreements.

flashcard 254:
frontside:
Article 232 - PART VI - THE STATES | CHAPTER V. - THE HIGH COURTS IN THE
Prompt: What does Article 232 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Articles 230, 231 and 232 substituted by articles 230 and 231]
Status: In Force
Mandate: Official constitutional provision governing Articles 230, 231 and 232 substituted by articles 230 and 231] within PART VI - THE STATES | CHAPTER V. - THE HIGH COURTS IN THE.
Key Provisions: Prescribes institutional mechanisms, procedural rules, and statutory boundaries for constitutional administration.
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Read in conjunction with parent chapter provisions to verify whether power is discretionary or exercised on aid and advice.

flashcard 255:
frontside:
Article 233 - PART VI - THE STATES | CHAPTER VI. - SUBORDINATE COURTS
Prompt: What does Article 233 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Appointment of district judges
Status: In Force
Mandate: Appointment of district judges: Made by the GOVERNOR of the State in consultation with the HIGH COURT exercising jurisdiction.
Key Provisions: Person not in service of Union/State eligible if advocate/pleader for at least 7 YEARS and recommended by High Court.
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Governor appoints, but consultation with High Court is mandatory and binding. Direct recruit needs 7 years advocacy.

flashcard 256:
frontside:
Article 233A - PART VI - THE STATES | CHAPTER VI. - SUBORDINATE COURTS
Prompt: What does Article 233A provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Validation of appointments of, and judgments, etc., delivered by, certain district judges
Status: In Force
Mandate: Official constitutional provision governing Validation of appointments of, and judgments, etc., delivered by, certain district judges within PART VI - THE STATES | CHAPTER VI. - SUBORDINATE COURTS.
Key Provisions: Prescribes institutional mechanisms, procedural rules, and statutory boundaries for constitutional administration.
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Read in conjunction with parent chapter provisions to verify whether power is discretionary or exercised on aid and advice.

flashcard 257:
frontside:
Article 234 - PART VI - THE STATES | CHAPTER VI. - SUBORDINATE COURTS
Prompt: What does Article 234 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Recruitment of persons other than district judges to the judicial service
Status: In Force
Mandate: Recruitment of persons other than district judges to the judicial service: Made by Governor in accordance with rules made by him after consultation with State PSC and High Court.
Key Provisions: Governs entry-level judicial officers (Civil Judge Junior Division / Judicial Magistrate First Class).
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Conducted through State Judicial Service examinations.

flashcard 258:
frontside:
Article 235 - PART VI - THE STATES | CHAPTER VI. - SUBORDINATE COURTS
Prompt: What does Article 235 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Control over subordinate courts
Status: In Force
Mandate: Control over subordinate courts: Control over district courts and courts subordinate thereto (postings, promotions, leave) is VESTED IN THE HIGH COURT.
Key Provisions: Constitutional cornerstone of judicial independence at grassroots: High Court has exclusive administrative control over subordinate judiciary.
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Governor makes initial appointment; all subsequent postings, transfers, and disciplinary control belong exclusively to the HIGH COURT.

flashcard 259:
frontside:
Article 236 - PART VI - THE STATES | CHAPTER VI. - SUBORDINATE COURTS
Prompt: What does Article 236 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Interpretation
Status: In Force
Mandate: Interpretation: Defines 'district judge' (includes judge of city civil court, additional district judge, sessions judge, etc.) and 'judicial service'.
Key Provisions: Definition clause for Chapter VI.
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Broad inclusive definition covering all district-level judicial posts.

flashcard 260:
frontside:
Article 237 - PART VI - THE STATES | CHAPTER VI. - SUBORDINATE COURTS
Prompt: What does Article 237 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Application of the provisions of this Chapter to certain class or classes of magistrates. [PART VII - [Omitted]] THE STATES IN PART B OF THE FIRST SCHEDULE
Status: [Omitted / Repealed]
Mandate: Application of the provisions of this Chapter to certain class or classes of magistrates: Governor may by public notification direct Chapter VI applies to any class of magistrates.
Key Provisions: Used historically to separate Judicial Magistrates from Executive Magistrates under Art 50.
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Enables separation of judicial functions from executive administration.

## PART VII - THE STATES IN PART B OF THE FIRST SCHEDULE [Repealed]

flashcard 261:
frontside:
Article 238 - PART VII - THE STATES IN PART B OF THE FIRST SCHEDULE [Repealed] | CHAPTER VI. - SUBORDINATE COURTS
Prompt: What does Article 238 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Omitted
Status: [Omitted / Repealed]
Mandate: Application of provisions of Part VI to States in Part B of First Schedule [Omitted].
Key Provisions: Dealt with Rajpramukhs and Part B princely states. Completely REPEALED by the Constitution (Seventh Amendment) Act, 1956 on States Reorganisation.
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Part VII is the ONLY complete Part of the Constitution that has been repealed.

## PART VIII - THE UNION TERRITORIES

flashcard 262:
frontside:
Article 239 - PART VIII - THE UNION TERRITORIES
Prompt: What does Article 239 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Administration of Union territories
Status: In Force
Mandate: Administration of Union territories: Administered by the PRESIDENT acting through an Administrator appointed by him (designated as Lieutenant Governor, Chief Commissioner, or Administrator).
Key Provisions: President may appoint Governor of a neighbouring State as Administrator, who exercises functions independently of his State Council of Ministers (Art 239(2)).
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Administrator of a UT is an agent of the President, NOT a constitutional head like a State Governor.

flashcard 263:
frontside:
Article 239A - PART VIII - THE UNION TERRITORIES
Prompt: What does Article 239A provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Creation of local Legislatures or Council of Ministers or both for certain Union territories. 239AA. Special provisions with respect to Delhi. 239AB. Provision in case of failure of constitutional machinery
Status: In Force
Mandate: Creation of local Legislatures or Council of Ministers or both for certain Union territories: Parliament may by law create a legislature/ministry for Puducherry.
Key Provisions: Inserted by 14th Amendment Act, 1962. Puducherry operates under the Government of Union Territories Act, 1963.
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Created by ordinary parliamentary law; not a constitutional amendment under Art 368.

flashcard 264:
frontside:
Article 239B - PART VIII - THE UNION TERRITORIES
Prompt: What does Article 239B provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Power of administrator to promulgate Ordinances during recess of Legislature
Status: In Force
Mandate: Power of administrator to promulgate Ordinances during recess of Legislature in UTs having legislature (Puducherry).
Key Provisions: Requires prior instructions of the President. Max life 6 weeks from reassembly.
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Corresponds to Governor's ordinance power, but requires President's sanction.

flashcard 265:
frontside:
Article 240 - PART VIII - THE UNION TERRITORIES
Prompt: What does Article 240 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Power of President to make regulations for certain Union territories
Status: In Force
Mandate: Power of President to make regulations for peace, progress and good government of certain UTs: Andaman & Nicobar, Lakshadweep, Dadra & Nagar Haveli and Daman & Diu, Puducherry (only when assembly dissolved/suspended).
Key Provisions: A regulation made by President has same force and effect as an Act of Parliament and can amend or repeal any parliamentary Act applicable to the UT.
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: When a legislature is functioning in Puducherry, President's regulation-making power under Art 240 is suspended.

flashcard 266:
frontside:
Article 241 - PART VIII - THE UNION TERRITORIES
Prompt: What does Article 241 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: High Courts for Union territories
Status: In Force
Mandate: High Courts for Union territories: Parliament may by law constitute a High Court for a UT or declare any court to be a High Court.
Key Provisions: Delhi is the only UT with its own separate High Court (established by Delhi High Court Act 1966). J&K HC serves UT of J&K and UT of Ladakh.
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Parliament has exclusive power to create or extend HC jurisdiction over UTs.

flashcard 267:
frontside:
Article 242 - PART VIII - THE UNION TERRITORIES
Prompt: What does Article 242 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Coorg - [Omitted]
Status: [Omitted / Repealed]
Mandate: Coorg [Omitted].
Key Provisions: Repealed by 7th Amendment Act, 1956 on reorganisation of states.
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Historical Part C state merged into Mysore (Karnataka).

## PART IX - THE PANCHAYATS

flashcard 268:
frontside:
Article 243 - PART IX - THE PANCHAYATS
Prompt: What does Article 243 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Definitions
Status: In Force
Mandate: Definitions for Part IX: Defines District, Gram Sabha, Intermediate level, Panchayat, Panchayat area, Population, and Village.
Key Provisions: Inserted by 73rd Constitutional Amendment Act, 1992.
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Foundation definitions for local self-governance.

flashcard 269:
frontside:
Article 243A - PART IX - THE PANCHAYATS
Prompt: What does Article 243A provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Gram Sabha
Status: In Force
Mandate: Gram Sabha: A body consisting of persons registered in the electoral rolls relating to a village comprised within the area of Panchayat at the village level.
Key Provisions: Foundation of Panchayati Raj. Exercises powers and performs functions at village level as State Legislature provides by law.
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Gram Sabha includes ALL registered voters of the village, not just household heads or elected representatives.

flashcard 270:
frontside:
Article 243B - PART IX - THE PANCHAYATS
Prompt: What does Article 243B provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Constitution of Panchayats
Status: In Force
Mandate: Constitution of Panchayats: 3-tier system in every State - Village level, Intermediate level (block/taluka), and District level (Zilla).
Key Provisions: Proviso: Panchayats at intermediate level MAY NOT BE CONSTITUTED in a State having a population NOT EXCEEDING 20 LAKHS.
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: 3-tier structure is mandatory, but intermediate tier is optional for States with population <= 20 lakhs.

flashcard 271:
frontside:
Article 243C - PART IX - THE PANCHAYATS
Prompt: What does Article 243C provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Composition of Panchayats
Status: In Force
Mandate: Composition of Panchayats: All seats filled by direct election from territorial constituencies. Ratio between population and seats must be uniform.
Key Provisions: Chairperson of village panchayat elected as State law provides (direct or indirect); Chairperson of intermediate and district panchayats MUST BE ELECTED INDIRECTLY from amongst elected members.
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Chairperson of Intermediate and District Panchayats MUST be elected indirectly; Village Chairperson mode is left to State law.

flashcard 272:
frontside:
Article 243D - PART IX - THE PANCHAYATS
Prompt: What does Article 243D provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Reservation of seats
Status: In Force
Mandate: Reservation of seats in Panchayats: (1) SCs and STs: reserved in proportion to their population; (2) Women: NOT LESS THAN ONE-THIRD (33%) of total seats reserved for women (including SC/ST women); (3) Offices of Chairpersons reserved for SCs/STs and women (min 1/3rd).
Key Provisions: Many States (Bihar, MP, Rajasthan, Kerala, etc.) have increased women's reservation to 50% by state laws. State legislature may also provide reservation for backward classes (OBCs).
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: 1/3rd reservation for women in Panchayats is a MANDATORY constitutional minimum; it applies to both ordinary seats AND chairperson offices.

flashcard 273:
frontside:
Article 243E - PART IX - THE PANCHAYATS
Prompt: What does Article 243E provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Duration of Panchayats, etc
Status: In Force
Mandate: Duration of Panchayats: 5 years from date of first meeting. Elections must be completed: (a) before expiry of 5-year term; (b) before expiration of 6 MONTHS from dissolution.
Key Provisions: A Panchayat reconstituted upon premature dissolution serves ONLY FOR THE REMAINDER of the period for which the dissolved Panchayat would have continued.
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Dissolved Panchayat reconstituted does NOT get a fresh 5-year term; it serves only the remainder of the 5-year period (unless remainder is <6 months, in which case election is skipped).

flashcard 274:
frontside:
Article 243F - PART IX - THE PANCHAYATS
Prompt: What does Article 243F provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Disqualifications for membership
Status: In Force
Mandate: Disqualifications for membership of Panchayat: (a) Disqualified under any law for election to State Legislature; (b) Disqualified by law made by State Legislature.
Key Provisions: CRITICAL PROVISO: No person shall be disqualified on ground that he is less than 25 years of age, IF HE HAS ATTAINED THE AGE OF 21 YEARS.
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Minimum age to contest Panchayat election is 21 YEARS (not 25 years as for State Legislative Assembly).

flashcard 275:
frontside:
Article 243G - PART IX - THE PANCHAYATS
Prompt: What does Article 243G provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Powers, authority and responsibilities of Panchayats
Status: In Force
Mandate: Powers, authority and responsibilities of Panchayats: State Legislature may endow Panchayats with powers to function as institutions of self-government, including implementation of schemes for economic development and social justice in 29 SUBJECTS LISTED IN ELEVENTH SCHEDULE.
Key Provisions: Devolution of 3Fs (Functions, Finances, Functionaries) is enabling and dependent on State legislation.
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Eleventh Schedule lists 29 subjects; listing does not automatically transfer them - State law must devolve them.

flashcard 276:
frontside:
Article 243H - PART IX - THE PANCHAYATS
Prompt: What does Article 243H provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Powers to impose taxes by, and Funds of, the Panchayats. 243-I. Constitution of Finance Commission to review financial position
Status: In Force
Mandate: Powers to impose taxes by, and Funds of, the Panchayats: State Legislature may authorise Panchayats to levy taxes, assign state revenues, provide grants-in-aid, and create Panchayat funds.
Key Provisions: Financial viability depends on statutory tax powers and State Finance Commission recommendations.
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Panchayats have no inherent taxation power; derive all fiscal powers from State statute.

flashcard 277:
frontside:
Article 243J - PART IX - THE PANCHAYATS
Prompt: What does Article 243J provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Audit of accounts of Panchayats
Status: In Force
Mandate: Audit of accounts of Panchayats: State Legislature makes provisions regarding maintenance and auditing of accounts.
Key Provisions: State legislation prescribes accounting standards and audit authorities.
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Auditing is regulated by State law, often conducted by Examiner of Local Fund Accounts.

flashcard 278:
frontside:
Article 243K - PART IX - THE PANCHAYATS
Prompt: What does Article 243K provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Elections to the Panchayats
Status: In Force
Mandate: Elections to the Panchayats: Superintendence, direction and control of electoral rolls and conduct of all elections to Panchayats VESTED IN THE STATE ELECTION COMMISSION (SEC).
Key Provisions: State Election Commissioner appointed by the GOVERNOR. REMOVAL: Cannot be removed except in like manner and on like grounds as a JUDGE OF A HIGH COURT (proved misbehaviour/incapacity via Parliament). Conditions of service cannot be varied to disadvantage.
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: SEC is appointed by Governor, but can be removed ONLY by the President in the same manner as a High Court Judge (Governor CANNOT remove SEC).

flashcard 279:
frontside:
Article 243L - PART IX - THE PANCHAYATS
Prompt: What does Article 243L provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Application to Union territories
Status: In Force
Mandate: Application of Part IX to Union territories: Applies to UTs subject to modifications/exceptions directed by President by public notification.
Key Provisions: Enables establishment of Panchayats in UTs like Lakshadweep, Andaman, Puducherry.
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: President issues notification specifying modifications for UTs.

flashcard 280:
frontside:
Article 243M - PART IX - THE PANCHAYATS
Prompt: What does Article 243M provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Part not to apply to certain areas
Status: In Force
Mandate: Part IX not to apply to certain areas: (1) Scheduled Areas (5th Schedule) and Tribal Areas (6th Schedule); (2) States of Nagaland, Meghalaya, and Mizoram; (3) Hill areas of Manipur; Darjeeling Gorkha Hill Council.
Key Provisions: Parliament enacted Provisions of the Panchayats (Extension to the Scheduled Areas) Act, 1996 (PESA Act) to extend Part IX to Fifth Schedule areas with special tribal safeguards (Gram Sabha ownership of minor forest produce, land alienation control).
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Part IX did not automatically apply to 5th Schedule areas; PESA Act, 1996 was passed under Art 243M(4)(b) to extend it with modifications.

flashcard 281:
frontside:
Article 243N - PART IX - THE PANCHAYATS
Prompt: What does Article 243N provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Continuance of existing laws and Panchayats. 243-O. Bar to interference by courts in electoral matters
Status: In Force
Mandate: Continuance of existing laws and Panchayats: Allowed 1 year transition period for States to bring existing laws into conformity with 73rd Amdt.
Key Provisions: All States enacted conforming legislation within the 1-year window (by April 1994).
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Transitional provision ensuring smooth changeover.

## PART IXA - THE MUNICIPALITIES

flashcard 282:
frontside:
Article 243P - PART IXA - THE MUNICIPALITIES
Prompt: What does Article 243P provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Definitions
Status: In Force
Mandate: Definitions for Part IXA: Defines Committee, District, Metropolitan area (population 10 lakhs+), Municipal area, Municipality, Panchayat, Population.
Key Provisions: Inserted by 74th Constitutional Amendment Act, 1992.
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Foundation definitions for urban local bodies.

flashcard 283:
frontside:
Article 243Q - PART IXA - THE MUNICIPALITIES
Prompt: What does Article 243Q provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Constitution of Municipalities
Status: In Force
Mandate: Constitution of Municipalities: 3 types - (a) Nagar Panchayat (transitional area from rural to urban); (b) Municipal Council (smaller urban area); (c) Municipal Corporation (larger urban area).
Key Provisions: Governor specifies classification based on population, density, revenue, and non-agricultural employment. Industrial township can be exempt from elected municipality.
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Governor notifies transition criteria; industrial townships can have nominated statutory authorities.

flashcard 284:
frontside:
Article 243R - PART IXA - THE MUNICIPALITIES
Prompt: What does Article 243R provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Composition of Municipalities
Status: In Force
Mandate: Composition of Municipalities: All seats filled by direct election from territorial constituencies (wards). State law may provide representation for experts (without voting rights), MPs, MLAs, MLCs.
Key Provisions: Nominated experts in municipalities have NO right to vote in meetings.
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Nominated members cannot vote in municipality meetings.

flashcard 285:
frontside:
Article 243S - PART IXA - THE MUNICIPALITIES
Prompt: What does Article 243S provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Constitution and composition of Wards Committees, etc
Status: In Force
Mandate: Constitution and composition of Wards Committees: In all Municipalities having a POPULATION OF THREE LAKHS OR MORE.
Key Provisions: Consists of one or more wards within territorial area of municipality. Promotes citizen participation.
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Wards Committees are MANDATORY only for municipalities with population >= 3 LAKHS.

flashcard 286:
frontside:
Article 243T - PART IXA - THE MUNICIPALITIES
Prompt: What does Article 243T provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Reservation of seats
Status: In Force
Mandate: Reservation of seats in Municipalities: SCs and STs in proportion to population; NOT LESS THAN 1/3RD OF SEATS RESERVED FOR WOMEN (including SC/ST women).
Key Provisions: Offices of Chairpersons also reserved for SCs/STs and women in manner prescribed by State law.
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Matches Article 243D of Panchayats: min 1/3rd mandatory reservation for women.

flashcard 287:
frontside:
Article 243U - PART IXA - THE MUNICIPALITIES
Prompt: What does Article 243U provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Duration of Municipalities, etc
Status: In Force
Mandate: Duration of Municipalities: 5 years from date of first meeting. Elections within 6 months of dissolution; reconstituted municipality serves only remainder period.
Key Provisions: Matches Article 243E of Panchayats.
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Reconstituted municipality serves only remainder of 5-year term.

flashcard 288:
frontside:
Article 243V - PART IXA - THE MUNICIPALITIES
Prompt: What does Article 243V provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Disqualifications for membership
Status: In Force
Mandate: Disqualifications for membership of Municipality: Minimum age is 21 YEARS.
Key Provisions: Matches Article 243F: age 21+ cannot be disqualified on age grounds.
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Minimum age to contest municipality election is 21 years.

flashcard 289:
frontside:
Article 243W - PART IXA - THE MUNICIPALITIES
Prompt: What does Article 243W provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Powers, authority and responsibilities of Municipalities, etc
Status: In Force
Mandate: Powers, authority and responsibilities of Municipalities: State law may devolve functions for urban planning, economic development, and 18 SUBJECTS IN TWELFTH SCHEDULE.
Key Provisions: Twelfth Schedule lists 18 functional items (urban planning, roads, water supply, public health, fire services, slum improvement).
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Eleventh Schedule = 29 subjects (Panchayats); Twelfth Schedule = 18 subjects (Municipalities).

flashcard 290:
frontside:
Article 243X - PART IXA - THE MUNICIPALITIES
Prompt: What does Article 243X provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Power to impose taxes by, and Funds of, the Municipalities
Status: In Force
Mandate: Power to impose taxes by, and Funds of, the Municipalities: As provided by State Legislature.
Key Provisions: Property tax, user charges, entertainment tax, octroi/entry tax where applicable.
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Fiscal powers are purely statutory.

flashcard 291:
frontside:
Article 243Y - PART IXA - THE MUNICIPALITIES
Prompt: What does Article 243Y provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Finance Commission
Status: In Force
Mandate: Finance Commission: State Finance Commission constituted under Article 243I also reviews financial position of Municipalities.
Key Provisions: Single State Finance Commission reviews both rural (Panchayats) and urban (Municipalities) local bodies.
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Separate Finance Commission is NOT constituted for Municipalities; SFC under Art 243I handles both.

flashcard 292:
frontside:
Article 243Z - PART IXA - THE MUNICIPALITIES
Prompt: What does Article 243Z provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Audit of accounts of Municipalities. 243ZA. Elections to the Municipalities. 243ZB. Application to Union territories. 243ZC. Part not to apply to certain areas. 243ZD. Committee for district planning. 243ZE. Committee for Metropolitan planning. 243ZF. Continuance of existing laws and Municipalities. 243ZG. Bar to interference by courts in electoral matters
Status: In Force
Mandate: Audit of accounts of Municipalities: As provided by State Legislature.
Key Provisions: Regulated by State laws.
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Prescribed by state statute.

## PART X - THE SCHEDULED AND TRIBAL AREAS

flashcard 293:
frontside:
Article 244 - PART X - THE SCHEDULED AND TRIBAL AREAS
Prompt: What does Article 244 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Administration of Scheduled Areas and Tribal Areas
Status: In Force
Mandate: Administration of Scheduled Areas and Tribal Areas: 244(1) Fifth Schedule applies to Scheduled Areas and STs in all States EXCEPT Assam, Meghalaya, Tripura, Mizoram. 244(2) Sixth Schedule applies to Tribal Areas in Assam, Meghalaya, Tripura, Mizoram (AMTM).
Key Provisions: Fifth Schedule: Governor has special powers, Tribes Advisory Council (TAC), can direct Acts of Parliament/State not to apply. Sixth Schedule: Autonomous District Councils (ADCs) with legislative, judicial, and financial powers.
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Fifth Schedule = 10 States (AP, Telangana, Jharkhand, Odisha, Chhattisgarh, MP, Rajasthan, Gujarat, Maharashtra, HP). Sixth Schedule = 4 North-East States (AMTM: Assam, Meghalaya, Tripura, Mizoram).

flashcard 294:
frontside:
Article 244A - PART X - THE SCHEDULED AND TRIBAL AREAS
Prompt: What does Article 244A provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Formation of an autonomous State comprising certain tribal areas in Assam and creation of local Legislature or Council of Ministers or both therefor
Status: In Force
Mandate: Formation of an autonomous State comprising certain tribal areas in Assam and creation of local Legislature or Council of Ministers: Parliament may by law create it.
Key Provisions: Inserted by 22nd Amendment Act, 1969. Led historically to the creation of Meghalaya as an autonomous state within Assam before becoming a full state.
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Applies specifically to tribal areas in Assam.

## PART XI - RELATIONS BETWEEN THE UNION AND THE STATES

flashcard 295:
frontside:
Article 245 - PART XI - RELATIONS BETWEEN THE UNION AND THE STATES | CHAPTER I. - LEGISLATIVE RELATIONS
Prompt: What does Article 245 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Extent of laws made by Parliament and by the Legislatures of States
Status: In Force
Mandate: Extent of laws made by Parliament and by the Legislatures of States: Parliament may make laws for whole or any part of India (and has EXTRA-TERRITORIAL LEGISLATION power). State Legislature can make laws only for whole or part of the State.
Key Provisions: Extra-territorial legislation applies to Indian citizens and property anywhere in the world; State laws require 'territorial nexus' (State of Bombay v RMD Chamarbaugwala).
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Only Parliament has extra-territorial legislative competence; State laws cannot operate extra-territorially without territorial nexus.

flashcard 296:
frontside:
Article 246 - PART XI - RELATIONS BETWEEN THE UNION AND THE STATES | CHAPTER I. - LEGISLATIVE RELATIONS
Prompt: What does Article 246 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Subject-matter of laws made by Parliament and by the Legislatures of States
Status: In Force
Mandate: Subject-matter of laws made by Parliament and State Legislatures (Seventh Schedule): List I (Union List - Parliament exclusive), List II (State List - State exclusive), List III (Concurrent List - both Parliament and States).
Key Provisions: Federal supremacy: In case of overlapping, Union List prevails over State and Concurrent Lists; Concurrent List prevails over State List.
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: For Union Territories, Parliament can make laws on ANY subject in ALL 3 lists (including State List) under Art 246(4).

flashcard 297:
frontside:
Article 246A - PART XI - RELATIONS BETWEEN THE UNION AND THE STATES | CHAPTER I. - LEGISLATIVE RELATIONS
Prompt: What does Article 246A provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Special provision with respect to goods and services tax
Status: In Force
Mandate: Special provision with respect to Goods and Services Tax (GST): Inserted by 101st Constitutional Amendment Act, 2016.
Key Provisions: Confers SIMULTANEOUS LEGISLATIVE POWER on Parliament and State Legislatures to make laws regarding GST. Parliament has exclusive power over inter-state trade/commerce GST (IGST under Art 269A).
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Art 246A creates simultaneous power outside the traditional Seventh Schedule scheme; normal repugnancy rule of Art 254 does NOT apply in the same way (Mohit Minerals case 2022: GST Council recommendations are persuasive, not binding mandates).

flashcard 298:
frontside:
Article 247 - PART XI - RELATIONS BETWEEN THE UNION AND THE STATES | CHAPTER I. - LEGISLATIVE RELATIONS
Prompt: What does Article 247 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Power of Parliament to provide for the establishment of certain additional courts
Status: In Force
Mandate: Power of Parliament to provide for establishment of certain additional courts for better administration of Union laws.
Key Provisions: Enables creation of special central courts/tribunals for Union List statutes.
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Exercised via parliamentary statute.

flashcard 299:
frontside:
Article 248 - PART XI - RELATIONS BETWEEN THE UNION AND THE STATES | CHAPTER I. - LEGISLATIVE RELATIONS
Prompt: What does Article 248 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Residuary powers of legislation
Status: In Force
Mandate: Residuary powers of legislation: Parliament has exclusive power to make any law with respect to ANY MATTER NOT ENUMERATED in Concurrent List or State List.
Key Provisions: Includes power of imposing a tax not mentioned in either list. In India, residuary power belongs to the CENTRE (modelled on Canada), unlike USA and Australia where residuary powers belong to States.
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Residuary powers belong to PARLIAMENT in India, not to the States.

flashcard 300:
frontside:
Article 249 - PART XI - RELATIONS BETWEEN THE UNION AND THE STATES | CHAPTER I. - LEGISLATIVE RELATIONS
Prompt: What does Article 249 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Power of Parliament to legislate with respect to a matter in the State List in the national interest
Status: In Force
Mandate: Power of Parliament to legislate with respect to a matter in the State List in the NATIONAL INTEREST.
Key Provisions: CONDITIONS: RAJYA SABHA must pass a resolution supported by NOT LESS THAN 2/3RD OF MEMBERS PRESENT AND VOTING. Resolution remains in force for max 1 YEAR (can be renewed for 1 yr at a time). Law ceases to have effect 6 MONTHS after resolution expires.
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Resolution can be passed ONLY by Rajya Sabha (federal safeguard), by 2/3rd present and voting. State Legislature can still make law on same subject, but parliamentary law prevails in inconsistency (Art 251).

flashcard 301:
frontside:
Article 250 - PART XI - RELATIONS BETWEEN THE UNION AND THE STATES | CHAPTER I. - LEGISLATIVE RELATIONS
Prompt: What does Article 250 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Power of Parliament to legislate with respect to any matter in the State List if a Proclamation of Emergency is in operation
Status: In Force
Mandate: Power of Parliament to legislate on State List matters if a PROCLAMATION OF EMERGENCY (Art 352) is in operation.
Key Provisions: Applies automatically during National Emergency. Law ceases to operate 6 MONTHS after emergency ceases.
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: State legislature is NOT suspended; it can still pass laws, but Union law overrides state law in case of conflict (Art 251).

flashcard 302:
frontside:
Article 251 - PART XI - RELATIONS BETWEEN THE UNION AND THE STATES | CHAPTER I. - LEGISLATIVE RELATIONS
Prompt: What does Article 251 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Inconsistency between laws made by Parliament under articles 249 and 250 and laws made by the Legislatures of States
Status: In Force
Mandate: Inconsistency between laws made by Parliament under Articles 249 and 250 and laws made by State Legislatures: Parliamentary law prevails; State law void to extent of repugnancy, but only so long as parliamentary law remains in force.
Key Provisions: State law remains in abeyance during operational period and revives thereafter.
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Governs supremacy specifically for Arts 249 and 250, distinct from general Art 254 repugnancy.

flashcard 303:
frontside:
Article 252 - PART XI - RELATIONS BETWEEN THE UNION AND THE STATES | CHAPTER I. - LEGISLATIVE RELATIONS
Prompt: What does Article 252 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Power of Parliament to legislate for two or more States by consent and adoption of such legislation by any other State
Status: In Force
Mandate: Power of Parliament to legislate for two or more States by CONSENT and adoption of such legislation by any other State.
Key Provisions: If Legislatures of 2 OR MORE STATES pass resolutions requesting Parliament to legislate on a State List subject, Parliament can pass such Act. Act applies to consenting States and any other State that adopts it later. CRITICAL: SUCH ACT CAN BE AMENDED OR REPEALED ONLY BY PARLIAMENT, NOT BY STATE LEGISLATURES.
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Once States surrender legislative competence under Art 252, they CANNOT amend or repeal that Act; only Parliament can amend or repeal it (e.g. Wildlife Protection Act 1972, Water Pollution Act 1974).

flashcard 304:
frontside:
Article 253 - PART XI - RELATIONS BETWEEN THE UNION AND THE STATES | CHAPTER I. - LEGISLATIVE RELATIONS
Prompt: What does Article 253 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Legislation for giving effect to international agreements
Status: In Force
