Mandate: Legislation for giving effect to international agreements: Parliament has power to make ANY law for whole or part of India for implementing any treaty, agreement or convention with foreign countries or decisions of international conferences.
Key Provisions: Enables Parliament to legislate on STATE LIST subjects without consulting States if needed to implement international treaties (e.g. TRIPS patent laws, environmental conventions).
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Treaty implementation allows Parliament to override the State List completely without any State consent.

flashcard 305:
frontside:
Article 254 - PART XI - RELATIONS BETWEEN THE UNION AND THE STATES | CHAPTER I. - LEGISLATIVE RELATIONS
Prompt: What does Article 254 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Inconsistency between laws made by Parliament and laws made by the Legislatures of States
Status: In Force
Mandate: Inconsistency between laws made by Parliament and laws made by State Legislatures (Doctrine of Repugnancy): In Concurrent List, parliamentary law prevails over state law.
Key Provisions: CRITICAL EXCEPTION (Art 254(2)): If a State law on a Concurrent subject is repugnant to an earlier Parliamentary law, the State law SHALL PREVAIL IN THAT STATE IF IT HAS BEEN RESERVED FOR PRESIDENT'S CONSIDERATION AND RECEIVED PRESIDENTIAL ASSENT. Proviso: Parliament can at any time enact fresh law adding to, amending, varying or repealing such State law.
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Repugnancy under Art 254 applies ONLY to Concurrent List (List III) matters (Hoechst case). State law can override central law in that State if it received President's assent under 254(2).

flashcard 306:
frontside:
Article 255 - PART XI - RELATIONS BETWEEN THE UNION AND THE STATES | CHAPTER I. - LEGISLATIVE RELATIONS
Prompt: What does Article 255 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Requirements as to recommendations and previous sanctions to be regarded as matters of procedure only
Status: In Force
Mandate: Requirements as to recommendations and previous sanctions to be regarded as matters of procedure only: No Act invalidated merely for lack of prior recommendation/sanction if subsequently assented.
Key Provisions: Cures procedural defects of missing prior recommendation.
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Assent of President or Governor cures prior sanction defects.

flashcard 307:
frontside:
Article 256 - PART XI - RELATIONS BETWEEN THE UNION AND THE STATES | CHAPTER II. -  ADMINISTRATIVE RELATIONS
Prompt: What does Article 256 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Obligation of States and the Union
Status: In Force
Mandate: Obligation of States and the Union: Executive power of every State shall be so exercised as to ensure compliance with laws made by Parliament. Union executive can give directions to a State for that purpose.
Key Provisions: Constitutional duty of States to comply with Central laws.
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Failure to comply triggers Article 365, which can lead to President's Rule under Article 356.

flashcard 308:
frontside:
Article 257 - PART XI - RELATIONS BETWEEN THE UNION AND THE STATES | CHAPTER II. -  ADMINISTRATIVE RELATIONS
Prompt: What does Article 257 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Control of the Union over States in certain cases
Status: In Force
Mandate: Control of the Union over States in certain cases: State executive power cannot impede or prejudice Union executive power. Union can give directions on: (a) Construction and maintenance of means of communication of national or military importance; (b) Protection of railways.
Key Provisions: Union pays extra cost incurred by State in carrying out such directions (determined by arbitrator appointed by CJI if disputed).
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Communications and railways protection are specific grounds for binding central administrative directions.

flashcard 309:
frontside:
Article 257A - PART XI - RELATIONS BETWEEN THE UNION AND THE STATES | CHAPTER II. -  ADMINISTRATIVE RELATIONS
Prompt: What does Article 257A provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Assistance to States by deployment of armed forces or other forces of the Union - [Omitted]
Status: [Omitted / Repealed]
Mandate: This article has been omitted or repealed from the Constitution (typically by the 7th Amendment Act 1956 or subsequent amendments on federal restructuring/privy purses).
Key Provisions: Retained in constitutional numbering to preserve the integrity of subsequent article references without renumbering the entire Constitution.
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Articles are omitted rather than renumbered to keep historical citations and cross-references constant across statutes.

flashcard 310:
frontside:
Article 258 - PART XI - RELATIONS BETWEEN THE UNION AND THE STATES | CHAPTER II. -  ADMINISTRATIVE RELATIONS
Prompt: What does Article 258 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Power of the Union to confer powers, etc., on States in certain cases
Status: In Force
Mandate: Power of the Union to confer powers, etc., on States in certain cases: President may, with CONSENT of State Government, entrust Union executive functions to State officers.
Key Provisions: Parliament may also by law confer powers on State officers without State consent (Art 258(2)).
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Executive entrustment by President needs State consent; statutory entrustment by Parliament does not require consent.

flashcard 311:
frontside:
Article 258A - PART XI - RELATIONS BETWEEN THE UNION AND THE STATES | CHAPTER II. -  ADMINISTRATIVE RELATIONS
Prompt: What does Article 258A provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Power of the States to entrust functions to the Union
Status: In Force
Mandate: Power of the States to entrust functions to the Union: Governor may, with CONSENT of Government of India, entrust State executive functions to Union officers.
Key Provisions: Inserted by 7th Amendment Act, 1956. Reciprocal federal delegation.
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Requires consent of Government of India.

flashcard 312:
frontside:
Article 259 - PART XI - RELATIONS BETWEEN THE UNION AND THE STATES | CHAPTER II. -  ADMINISTRATIVE RELATIONS
Prompt: What does Article 259 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Armed Forces in States in Part B of the First Schedule - [Omitted]
Status: [Omitted / Repealed]
Mandate: This article has been omitted or repealed from the Constitution (typically by the 7th Amendment Act 1956 or subsequent amendments on federal restructuring/privy purses).
Key Provisions: Retained in constitutional numbering to preserve the integrity of subsequent article references without renumbering the entire Constitution.
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Articles are omitted rather than renumbered to keep historical citations and cross-references constant across statutes.

flashcard 313:
frontside:
Article 260 - PART XI - RELATIONS BETWEEN THE UNION AND THE STATES | CHAPTER II. -  ADMINISTRATIVE RELATIONS
Prompt: What does Article 260 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Jurisdiction of the Union in relation to territories outside India
Status: In Force
Mandate: Jurisdiction of the Union in relation to territories outside India: GoI may by agreement undertake executive, legislative or judicial functions in foreign territories.
Key Provisions: Administered under Foreign Jurisdiction Act, 1947.
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Applies to foreign territories under bilateral treaty/agreement.

flashcard 314:
frontside:
Article 261 - PART XI - RELATIONS BETWEEN THE UNION AND THE STATES | CHAPTER II. -  ADMINISTRATIVE RELATIONS
Prompt: What does Article 261 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Public acts, records and judicial proceedings. Disputes relating to Waters
Status: In Force
Mandate: Public acts, records and judicial proceedings: Full faith and credit shall be given throughout India to public acts, records and judicial proceedings of Union and every State.
Key Provisions: Civil court final judgments and orders are executable anywhere in India according to law.
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Full faith and credit applies to CIVIL judgments; does NOT apply to criminal judgments.

flashcard 315:
frontside:
Article 262 - PART XI - RELATIONS BETWEEN THE UNION AND THE STATES | CHAPTER II. -  ADMINISTRATIVE RELATIONS
Prompt: What does Article 262 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Adjudication of disputes relating to waters of inter-State rivers or river valleys. Co-ordination between States
Status: In Force
Mandate: Adjudication of disputes relating to waters of inter-State rivers or river valleys: Parliament may by law provide for adjudication of any dispute or complaint with respect to use, distribution or control of waters of inter-State river/valley.
Key Provisions: Art 262(2): Parliament may by law enact that NEITHER THE SUPREME COURT NOR ANY OTHER COURT SHALL EXERCISE JURISDICTION in respect of any such dispute. Parliament passed Inter-State River Water Disputes Act, 1956 creating ad hoc tribunals (Cauvery, Krishna, Godavari tribunals).
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Supreme Court's original jurisdiction under Art 131 is EXCLUDED from inter-State river water disputes if barred by Parliamentary law under Art 262(2).

flashcard 316:
frontside:
Article 263 - PART XI - RELATIONS BETWEEN THE UNION AND THE STATES | CHAPTER II. -  ADMINISTRATIVE RELATIONS
Prompt: What does Article 263 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Provisions with respect to an inter-State Council
Status: In Force
Mandate: Provisions with respect to an Inter-State Council: If at any time it appears to PRESIDENT that public interests would be served, President may by order establish an Inter-State Council.
Key Provisions: Functions: (a) Inquiring into and advising on disputes between States; (b) Investigating and discussing subjects of common interest; (c) Making recommendations for better co-ordination of policy. Sarkaria Commission recommended activation; Inter-State Council established in 1990 by Presidential order. Chaired by PM, includes all CMs, administrators, and 6 Cabinet Ministers.
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Inter-State Council is an ADVISORY body, not an adjudicatory body. Established by the PRESIDENT, not Parliament.

## PART XII - FINANCE, PROPERTY, CONTRACTS AND SUITS

flashcard 317:
frontside:
Article 264 - PART XII - FINANCE, PROPERTY, CONTRACTS AND SUITS | CHAPTER I. - FINANCE
Prompt: What does Article 264 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Interpretation
Status: In Force
Mandate: Official constitutional provision governing Interpretation within PART XII - FINANCE, PROPERTY, CONTRACTS AND SUITS | CHAPTER I. - FINANCE.
Key Provisions: Prescribes institutional mechanisms, procedural rules, and statutory boundaries for constitutional administration.
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Read in conjunction with parent chapter provisions to verify whether power is discretionary or exercised on aid and advice.

flashcard 318:
frontside:
Article 265 - PART XII - FINANCE, PROPERTY, CONTRACTS AND SUITS | CHAPTER I. - FINANCE
Prompt: What does Article 265 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Taxes not to be imposed save by authority of law
Status: In Force
Mandate: Taxes not to be imposed save by authority of law: No tax shall be levied or collected except by authority of law.
Key Provisions: 'Law' means valid legislation enacted by a competent legislature, NOT an executive notification or order.
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Executive cannot impose a tax through subordinate rules or notifications without explicit parent statutory backing.

flashcard 319:
frontside:
Article 266 - PART XII - FINANCE, PROPERTY, CONTRACTS AND SUITS | CHAPTER I. - FINANCE
Prompt: What does Article 266 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Consolidated Funds and public accounts of India and of the States
Status: In Force
Mandate: Consolidated Funds and public accounts of India and of the States: All revenues, loans, and loan repayments form the Consolidated Fund of India/State. All other public moneys received form Public Account of India/State.
Key Provisions: Consolidated Fund: No money withdrawn except under parliamentary/state appropriation law. Public Account: Executive operated (provident funds, small savings, judicial deposits) - no legislative vote needed for disbursements.
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Consolidated Fund requires legislative appropriation (Art 114/204); Public Account is operated by executive action.

flashcard 320:
frontside:
Article 267 - PART XII - FINANCE, PROPERTY, CONTRACTS AND SUITS | CHAPTER I. - FINANCE
Prompt: What does Article 267 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Contingency Fund. Distribution of Revenues between the Union and the States
Status: In Force
Mandate: Contingency Fund: Parliament/State Legislature may by law establish a Contingency Fund in the nature of an imprest, placed at disposal of President/Governor.
Key Provisions: Used to meet unforeseen expenditure pending authorisation by Parliament/Legislature under Art 115/205. Corpus of CFI raised to Rs 30,000 crore.
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Held by Finance Secretary on behalf of the President; recouped through supplementary grants.

flashcard 321:
frontside:
Article 268 - PART XII - FINANCE, PROPERTY, CONTRACTS AND SUITS | CHAPTER I. - FINANCE
Prompt: What does Article 268 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Duties levied by the Union but collected and appropriated by the States
Status: In Force
Mandate: Duties levied by the Union but collected and appropriated by the States: Stamp duties on bills of exchange, cheques, promissory notes, etc.
Key Provisions: Levied by Union; collected by States within their territory and retained by them.
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Proceeds do NOT form part of the Consolidated Fund of India; retained directly by States.

flashcard 322:
frontside:
Article 268A - PART XII - FINANCE, PROPERTY, CONTRACTS AND SUITS | CHAPTER I. - FINANCE
Prompt: What does Article 268A provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Service tax levied by Union and collected by the Union and the States - [Omitted]
Status: [Omitted / Repealed]
Mandate: This article has been omitted or repealed from the Constitution (typically by the 7th Amendment Act 1956 or subsequent amendments on federal restructuring/privy purses).
Key Provisions: Retained in constitutional numbering to preserve the integrity of subsequent article references without renumbering the entire Constitution.
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Articles are omitted rather than renumbered to keep historical citations and cross-references constant across statutes.

Prompt: What does Article 269A provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Levy and collection of goods and services tax in course of inter- State trade or commerce
Status: In Force
Mandate: Levy and collection of Goods and Services Tax in course of inter-State trade or commerce (IGST): Levied and collected by Government of India, apportioned between Union and States based on GST Council recommendations.
Key Provisions: Inserted by 101st Amendment Act, 2016. Destination-based consumption tax.
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Apportioned amount does NOT form part of Consolidated Fund of India (goes to consuming State).

flashcard 325:
frontside:
Article 270 - PART XII - FINANCE, PROPERTY, CONTRACTS AND SUITS | CHAPTER I. - FINANCE
Prompt: What does Article 270 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Taxes levied and distributed between the Union and the States
Status: In Force
Mandate: Taxes levied and distributed between the Union and the States (Divisible Pool): All Union taxes/duties (except 268, 269, 269A, surcharges under 271, and earmarked cesses) are distributed between Union and States.
Key Provisions: 15th Finance Commission recommended 41% devolution to States from the divisible pool (1% adjusted for J&K/Ladakh UTs).
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Cesses and surcharges are EXCLUDED from the divisible pool and retained 100% by the Union.

flashcard 326:
frontside:
Article 271 - PART XII - FINANCE, PROPERTY, CONTRACTS AND SUITS | CHAPTER I. - FINANCE
Prompt: What does Article 271 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Surcharge on certain duties and taxes for purposes of the Union
Status: In Force
Mandate: Surcharge on certain duties and taxes for purposes of the Union: Parliament may increase any duties/taxes by a surcharge, and entire proceeds go to Union.
Key Provisions: 101st Amdt excluded GST from surcharge under Art 271. Surcharges do not enter the divisible pool.
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Surcharges are exclusive revenue of the Union and cannot be levied on GST.

Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Art 275 grants are statutory grants based on Finance Commission advice; Art 282 grants are discretionary.

Key Provisions: Total amount payable by any one person cannot exceed RS 2,500 PER ANNUM (raised from Rs 250 by 60th Amdt 1988).
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Constitutional ceiling of Rs 2,500 per year per person. Levying professional tax does not limit Parliament's income tax powers.

flashcard 335:
frontside:
Article 279A - PART XII - FINANCE, PROPERTY, CONTRACTS AND SUITS | CHAPTER I. - FINANCE
Prompt: What does Article 279A provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Goods and Services Tax Council
Status: In Force
Mandate: Goods and Services Tax Council (GST Council): Constituted by President within 60 days of 101st Amdt 2016.
Key Provisions: COMPOSITION: Union Finance Minister (Chairperson), Union MoS Finance, and State Finance/Taxation Ministers. VOTING FORMULA: Central Government vote weight = 1/3RD (33.3%); All State Governments together vote weight = 2/3RD (66.7%). Decision requires a majority of NOT LESS THAN 3/4TH (75%) OF WEIGHTED VOTES of members present and voting. Quorum = 1/2 members.
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Centre has veto power (holds 33.3%, while 75% needed); States collectively also have veto power (hold 66.7%). Supreme Court in Mohit Minerals (2022) held GST Council recommendations have persuasive value, not legally binding on legislatures.

flashcard 336:
frontside:
Article 280 - PART XII - FINANCE, PROPERTY, CONTRACTS AND SUITS | CHAPTER I. - FINANCE
Prompt: What does Article 280 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Finance Commission
Status: In Force
Mandate: Finance Commission: Constituted by the PRESIDENT every FIFTH YEAR or earlier. Consists of a Chairman and 4 OTHER MEMBERS appointed by President.
Key Provisions: Qualifications determined by Parliament (Finance Commission Act 1951: Judge of HC, specialised finance/accounts, public affairs, economics). DUTIES: (a) Distribution of net tax proceeds between Union and States (vertical devolution) and allocation between States (horizontal devolution); (b) Principles governing grants-in-aid under Art 275; (c) Measures to augment State Consolidated Fund to supplement Panchayat/Municipality resources based on SFC recommendations.
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Recommendations of the Finance Commission are ADVISORY in nature; not legally binding on Government (though accepted by convention). First FC chaired by KC Neogy; 15th FC chaired by NK Singh; 16th FC chaired by Arvind Panagariya.

Status: In Force
Mandate: Recommendations of the Finance Commission: President causes every recommendation together with an explanatory memorandum of action taken to be laid before each House of Parliament.
Key Provisions: Ensures parliamentary scrutiny of executive response to FC recommendations.
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Laid before Parliament by President with an Action Taken Report (ATR).

flashcard 349:
frontside:
Article 292 - PART XII - FINANCE, PROPERTY, CONTRACTS AND SUITS | CHAPTER II. -  BORROWING
Prompt: What does Article 292 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Borrowing by the Government of India
Status: In Force
Mandate: Borrowing by the Government of India: Executive power of Union extends to borrowing upon security of Consolidated Fund of India within limits fixed by Parliament.
Key Provisions: Parliament passed the Fiscal Responsibility and Budget Management (FRBM) Act, 2003 under this authority.
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Union can borrow both internally and externally (from foreign governments/institutions).

flashcard 350:
frontside:
Article 293 - PART XII - FINANCE, PROPERTY, CONTRACTS AND SUITS | CHAPTER II. -  BORROWING
Prompt: What does Article 293 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Borrowing by States
Status: In Force
Mandate: Borrowing by States: State executive power extends to borrowing within territory of India upon security of Consolidated Fund of State within legislative limits.
Key Provisions: CRITICAL RESTRICTION: (1) State CANNOT borrow outside India (no foreign borrowing); (2) State CANNOT raise any loan without CONSENT OF GOVERNMENT OF INDIA if there is still outstanding any part of a central loan or guaranteed loan (Art 293(3)).
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: States CANNOT borrow directly from foreign countries or international financial institutions. Centre's consent is mandatory if previous central loans are outstanding.

flashcard 356:
frontside:
Article 299 - PART XII - FINANCE, PROPERTY, CONTRACTS AND SUITS | CHAPTER III. -  PROPERTY, CONTRACTS, RIGHTS, LIABILITIES,
Prompt: What does Article 299 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Contracts
Status: In Force
Mandate: Contracts: All contracts made in exercise of executive power of Union or State shall be expressed to be made by the President or Governor and executed by authorised persons.
Key Provisions: President and Governor are NOT personally liable for contracts made on behalf of Government.
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Formal compliance with Art 299 (in writing, in name of President/Governor, by authorised officer) is mandatory; oral government contracts are void.

flashcard 357:
frontside:
Article 300 - PART XII - FINANCE, PROPERTY, CONTRACTS AND SUITS | CHAPTER III. -  PROPERTY, CONTRACTS, RIGHTS, LIABILITIES,
Prompt: What does Article 300 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Suits and proceedings
Status: In Force
Mandate: Suits and proceedings: Government of India may sue or be sued by the name of the Union of India; State Government by name of the State.
Key Provisions: Sovereign Immunity doctrine: Historically distinguished sovereign functions (defence, justice) from non-sovereign/commercial functions (Kasturi Lal case 1965). However, Supreme Court has severely curtailed sovereign immunity in fundamental rights violations, awarding compensation under Art 32/226 (Rudul Sah, Nilabati Behera cases).
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Government is legally suable in its constitutional name: 'Union of India' or 'State of [Name]'.

flashcard 358:
frontside:
Article 300A - PART XII - FINANCE, PROPERTY, CONTRACTS AND SUITS | CHAPTER IV. -  RIGHT TO PROPERTY
Prompt: What does Article 300A provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Persons not to be deprived of property save by authority of law
Status: In Force
Mandate: Persons not to be deprived of property save by authority of law: No person shall be deprived of his property save by authority of law.
Key Provisions: Inserted by the 44th Constitutional Amendment Act, 1978 after repealing Article 19(1)(f) and Article 31. Property is now a CONSTITUTIONAL/LEGAL RIGHT, NOT A FUNDAMENTAL RIGHT. Applies to citizens and non-citizens. Executive cannot seize property without statutory backing (Vidya Devi v State of HP 2020: state cannot grab private property without paying compensation).
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Violation of Art 300A cannot be challenged directly in Supreme Court under Art 32 (since it is in Part XII, not Part III); remedy lies in High Court under Art 226 or civil suit.

## PART XIII - TRADE, COMMERCE AND INTERCOURSE WITHIN THE TERRITORY OF INDIA

flashcard 359:
frontside:
Article 301 - PART XIII - TRADE, COMMERCE AND INTERCOURSE WITHIN THE TERRITORY OF INDIA
Prompt: What does Article 301 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Freedom of trade, commerce and intercourse
Status: In Force
Mandate: Freedom of trade, commerce and intercourse: Subject to other provisions of this Part, trade, commerce and intercourse throughout territory of India shall be FREE.
Key Provisions: Modelled on Section 92 of Australian Constitution. Covers both inter-State and intra-State trade. Prohibits fiscal and physical barriers to trade.
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Art 301 is not a Fundamental Right (found in Part XIII), but an independent constitutional limitation on legislative power.

flashcard 360:
frontside:
Article 302 - PART XIII - TRADE, COMMERCE AND INTERCOURSE WITHIN THE TERRITORY OF INDIA
Prompt: What does Article 302 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Power of Parliament to impose restrictions on trade, commerce
Status: In Force
Mandate: Power of Parliament to impose restrictions on trade, commerce and intercourse in the PUBLIC INTEREST.
Key Provisions: Parliament can enact laws like Essential Commodities Act restricting movement of goods.
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Parliament can impose restrictions by ordinary law in public interest.

flashcard 361:
frontside:
Article 303 - PART XIII - TRADE, COMMERCE AND INTERCOURSE WITHIN THE TERRITORY OF INDIA
Prompt: What does Article 303 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: and intercourse. Restrictions on the legislative powers of the Union and of the States with regard to trade and commerce
Status: In Force
Mandate: Restrictions on legislative powers of Union and States regarding trade: Neither Parliament nor State Legislature can make law giving preference to one State over another or discriminating between States.
Key Provisions: Clause (2) allows Parliament to discriminate ONLY for dealing with a situation arising from SCARCITY OF GOODS in any part of India.
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Non-discrimination is the rule; scarcity of goods is the sole exception allowing central preference.

flashcard 362:
frontside:
Article 304 - PART XIII - TRADE, COMMERCE AND INTERCOURSE WITHIN THE TERRITORY OF INDIA
Prompt: What does Article 304 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Restrictions on trade, commerce and intercourse among States
Status: In Force
Mandate: Restrictions on trade, commerce and intercourse among States by State Legislatures: (a) State can impose non-discriminatory taxes on goods imported from other States; (b) State can impose reasonable restrictions in public interest.
Key Provisions: CRITICAL PROVISO to 304(b): No Bill for imposing restrictions under 304(b) shall be introduced in State Legislature WITHOUT PREVIOUS SANCTION OF THE PRESIDENT (Atiabari Tea Co, Automobile Transport cases).
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: State bills restricting inter-state trade require PRIOR SANCTION OF THE PRESIDENT before introduction in Assembly.

## PART XIV - SERVICES UNDER THE UNION AND THE STATES

flashcard 366:
frontside:
Article 308 - PART XIV - SERVICES UNDER THE UNION AND THE STATES | CHAPTER I. -  SERVICES
Prompt: What does Article 308 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Interpretation
Status: In Force
Mandate: Official constitutional provision governing Interpretation within PART XIV - SERVICES UNDER THE UNION AND THE STATES | CHAPTER I. -  SERVICES.
Key Provisions: Prescribes institutional mechanisms, procedural rules, and statutory boundaries for constitutional administration.
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Read in conjunction with parent chapter provisions to verify whether power is discretionary or exercised on aid and advice.

flashcard 367:
frontside:
Article 309 - PART XIV - SERVICES UNDER THE UNION AND THE STATES | CHAPTER I. -  SERVICES
Prompt: What does Article 309 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Recruitment and conditions of service of persons serving the Union or a State
Status: In Force
Mandate: Recruitment and conditions of service of persons serving the Union or a State: Regulated by Acts of Parliament or State Legislature.
Key Provisions: President/Governor makes service rules until legislative enactment (e.g. Fundamental Rules, CCS Rules).
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Executive rules under Art 309 proviso operate until legislature passes statutory service law.

flashcard 368:
frontside:
Article 310 - PART XIV - SERVICES UNDER THE UNION AND THE STATES | CHAPTER I. -  SERVICES
Prompt: What does Article 310 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Tenure of office of persons serving the Union or a State
Status: In Force
Mandate: Tenure of office of persons serving the Union or a State (Doctrine of Pleasure): Civil and defence servants hold office during the PLEASURE of the President or Governor.
Key Provisions: Derived from British common law. Subject to constitutional exceptions: Judges, CAG, CEC, PSC members do not hold office at pleasure.
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Pleasure is subject to mandatory constitutional procedural safeguards in Article 311.

flashcard 369:
frontside:
Article 311 - PART XIV - SERVICES UNDER THE UNION AND THE STATES | CHAPTER I. -  SERVICES
Prompt: What does Article 311 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Dismissal, removal or reduction in rank of persons employed in civil capacities under the Union or a State
Status: In Force
Mandate: Dismissal, removal or reduction in rank of persons employed in civil capacities under Union or State: Provides two mandatory procedural safeguards.
Key Provisions: SAFEGUARDS: 311(1) Cannot be dismissed or removed by an authority SUBORDINATE to the appointing authority; 311(2) No dismissal, removal, or reduction in rank EXCEPT AFTER AN INQUIRY in which he is informed of charges and given a REASONABLE OPPORTUNITY OF BEING HEARD. 3 EXCEPTIONS TO INQUIRY (311(2) proviso): (a) Conviction on a criminal charge; (b) Where appointing authority records in writing that it is not reasonably practicable to hold inquiry; (c) Where President or Governor is satisfied that in the interest of SECURITY OF THE STATE it is not expedient to hold inquiry.
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Art 311 applies ONLY to civil servants; it does NOT apply to defence personnel or civilian employees of defence forces. Clause (c) security exception requires subjective satisfaction of President/Governor personally.

flashcard 370:
frontside:
Article 312 - PART XIV - SERVICES UNDER THE UNION AND THE STATES | CHAPTER I. -  SERVICES
Prompt: What does Article 312 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: All-India services
Status: In Force
Mandate: All-India Services: Parliament may by law create one or more All-India Services (common to Union and States) including an all-India judicial service.
Key Provisions: MANDATORY PREREQUISITE: RAJYA SABHA must pass a resolution supported by NOT LESS THAN 2/3RD OF MEMBERS PRESENT AND VOTING declaring it necessary in national interest. Existing AIS: IAS, IPS (pre-1950), and Indian Forest Service (IFS, created in 1966). All-India Judicial Service (AIJS) was added by 42nd Amdt 1976 (cannot include posts inferior to district judge).
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Resolution can be initiated ONLY in Rajya Sabha (federal safeguard protecting States' administrative autonomy). Requires 2/3rd present and voting.

flashcard 371:
frontside:
Article 312A - PART XIV - SERVICES UNDER THE UNION AND THE STATES | CHAPTER I. -  SERVICES

backside:
Title: Power of Parliament to vary or revoke conditions of service of officers of certain services
Status: In Force
Mandate: Official constitutional provision governing Power of Parliament to vary or revoke conditions of service of officers of certain services within PART XIV - SERVICES UNDER THE UNION AND THE STATES | CHAPTER I. -  SERVICES.
Key Provisions: Prescribes institutional mechanisms, procedural rules, and statutory boundaries for constitutional administration.
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Read in conjunction with parent chapter provisions to verify whether power is discretionary or exercised on aid and advice.

Article 313 - PART XIV - SERVICES UNDER THE UNION AND THE STATES | CHAPTER I. -  SERVICES

Key Provisions: Prescribes institutional mechanisms, procedural rules, and statutory boundaries for constitutional administration.
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Read in conjunction with parent chapter provisions to verify whether power is discretionary or exercised on aid and advice.

flashcard 373:
