Article 314 - PART XIV - SERVICES UNDER THE UNION AND THE STATES | CHAPTER I. -  SERVICES
Prompt: What does Article 314 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Provision for protection of existing officers of certain services - [Omitted]
Status: [Omitted / Repealed]
Mandate: This article has been omitted or repealed from the Constitution (typically by the 7th Amendment Act 1956 or subsequent amendments on federal restructuring/privy purses).
Key Provisions: Retained in constitutional numbering to preserve the integrity of subsequent article references without renumbering the entire Constitution.
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Articles are omitted rather than renumbered to keep historical citations and cross-references constant across statutes.

flashcard 374:
frontside:
Article 315 - PART XIV - SERVICES UNDER THE UNION AND THE STATES | CHAPTER II. - PUBLIC SERVICE COMMISSIONS
Prompt: What does Article 315 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Public Service Commissions for the Union and for the States
Status: In Force
Mandate: Public Service Commissions for the Union and for the States: UPSC for Union; State PSC for each State; Joint PSC for two or more States (created by Parliament on resolution by concerned State Legislatures).
Key Provisions: UPSC and SPSC are constitutional bodies directly created by Art 315. JSPSC is a statutory body created by parliamentary statute.
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: UPSC and SPSC are constitutional; Joint State PSC (JSPSC) is a STATUTORY body created by an Act of Parliament.

flashcard 375:
frontside:
Article 316 - PART XIV - SERVICES UNDER THE UNION AND THE STATES | CHAPTER II. - PUBLIC SERVICE COMMISSIONS
Prompt: What does Article 316 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Appointment and term of office of members
Status: In Force
Mandate: Appointment and term of office of members of Public Service Commissions: UPSC/JSPSC members appointed by PRESIDENT; SPSC members appointed by GOVERNOR. Half of members must have held government office for at least 10 years.
Key Provisions: TENURE: UPSC: 6 YEARS or until age of 65 YEARS, whichever is earlier. SPSC/JSPSC: 6 YEARS or until age of 62 YEARS, whichever is earlier.
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: UPSC retirement age = 65 years; SPSC retirement age = 62 years (raised from 60 by 41st Amdt 1976).

flashcard 376:
frontside:
Article 317 - PART XIV - SERVICES UNDER THE UNION AND THE STATES | CHAPTER II. - PUBLIC SERVICE COMMISSIONS
Prompt: What does Article 317 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Removal and suspension of a member of a Public Service Commission
Status: In Force
Mandate: Removal and suspension of a member of a Public Service Commission: Chairman or member of UPSC or SPSC can be removed ONLY BY THE PRESIDENT.
Key Provisions: GROUNDS: (1) Proved misbehaviour: President refers matter to SUPREME COURT for inquiry under Art 145; if SC reports that member ought to be removed, President MUST remove (SC advice is binding); (2) Insolvent; (3) Paid employment outside office; (4) Infirmity of mind or body (no SC inquiry needed for 2, 3, 4).
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: SPSC members are APPOINTED by the Governor (Art 316), but can be REMOVED ONLY BY THE PRESIDENT (Governor CANNOT remove an SPSC member; Governor can only suspend pending inquiry).

flashcard 377:
frontside:
Article 318 - PART XIV - SERVICES UNDER THE UNION AND THE STATES | CHAPTER II. - PUBLIC SERVICE COMMISSIONS
Prompt: What does Article 318 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Power to make regulations as to conditions of service of members and staff of the Commission
Status: In Force
Mandate: Official constitutional provision governing Power to make regulations as to conditions of service of members and staff of the Commission within PART XIV - SERVICES UNDER THE UNION AND THE STATES | CHAPTER II. - PUBLIC SERVICE COMMISSIONS.
Key Provisions: Prescribes institutional mechanisms, procedural rules, and statutory boundaries for constitutional administration.
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Read in conjunction with parent chapter provisions to verify whether power is discretionary or exercised on aid and advice.

flashcard 378:
frontside:
Article 319 - PART XIV - SERVICES UNDER THE UNION AND THE STATES | CHAPTER II. - PUBLIC SERVICE COMMISSIONS
Prompt: What does Article 319 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Prohibition as to the holding of offices by members of Commission on ceasing to be such members
Status: In Force
Mandate: Prohibition as to the holding of offices by members of Commission on ceasing to be such members.
Key Provisions: Chairman UPSC ineligible for further employment under GoI or States. Chairman SPSC eligible only for Chairman/member UPSC or Chairman of other SPSC. Members eligible only for Chairman posts.
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Ensures absolute independence of PSC members from executive temptation.

flashcard 379:
frontside:
Article 320 - PART XIV - SERVICES UNDER THE UNION AND THE STATES | CHAPTER II. - PUBLIC SERVICE COMMISSIONS
Prompt: What does Article 320 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Functions of Public Service Commissions
Status: In Force
Mandate: Functions of Public Service Commissions: Conduct examinations for appointment. Consulted on recruitment methods, principles of appointments/promotions/transfers, and disciplinary matters.
Key Provisions: Consultative body: Advice is advisory, not mandatory on government (State of UP v Manbodhan Lal 1957). Government must place memorandum before Parliament/Legislature explaining reasons for non-acceptance of advice.
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: UPSC is a central recruiting agency; Department of Personnel and Training (DoPT) is the central personnel management agency.

flashcard 380:
frontside:
Article 321 - PART XIV - SERVICES UNDER THE UNION AND THE STATES | CHAPTER II. - PUBLIC SERVICE COMMISSIONS
Prompt: What does Article 321 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Power to extend functions of Public Service Commissions
Status: In Force
Mandate: Official constitutional provision governing Power to extend functions of Public Service Commissions within PART XIV - SERVICES UNDER THE UNION AND THE STATES | CHAPTER II. - PUBLIC SERVICE COMMISSIONS.
Key Provisions: Prescribes institutional mechanisms, procedural rules, and statutory boundaries for constitutional administration.
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Read in conjunction with parent chapter provisions to verify whether power is discretionary or exercised on aid and advice.

flashcard 381:
frontside:
Article 322 - PART XIV - SERVICES UNDER THE UNION AND THE STATES | CHAPTER II. - PUBLIC SERVICE COMMISSIONS
Prompt: What does Article 322 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Expenses of Public Service Commissions
Status: In Force
Mandate: Expenses of Public Service Commissions: Charged on Consolidated Fund of India (UPSC) or Consolidated Fund of State (SPSC).
Key Provisions: Non-votable by legislature, safeguarding financial autonomy.
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: All administrative expenses including salaries and pensions are charged expenditure.

flashcard 382:
frontside:
Article 323 - PART XIV - SERVICES UNDER THE UNION AND THE STATES | CHAPTER II. - PUBLIC SERVICE COMMISSIONS
Prompt: What does Article 323 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Reports of Public Service Commissions
Status: In Force
Mandate: Reports of Public Service Commissions: UPSC submits annual report to PRESIDENT; SPSC to GOVERNOR. Laid before legislature with memorandum explaining any case of non-acceptance.
Key Provisions: Guarantees legislative transparency regarding executive adherence to merit.
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Reports submitted to executive head (President/Governor), who causes them to be laid before legislature.

## PART XIVA - TRIBUNALS

flashcard 383:
frontside:
Article 323A - PART XIVA - TRIBUNALS
Prompt: What does Article 323A provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Administrative tribunals
Status: In Force
Mandate: Administrative Tribunals: PARLIAMENT MAY BY LAW establish administrative tribunals for adjudication of disputes regarding recruitment and conditions of service of public servants.
Key Provisions: Inserted by 42nd Amendment Act, 1976. Parliament enacted Administrative Tribunals Act, 1985 creating Central Administrative Tribunal (CAT) and State Administrative Tribunals (SATs). Members have judicial/administrative background. L. Chandra Kumar v Union of India (1997): Supreme Court struck down exclusion of judicial review; decisions of CAT/SAT are subject to scrutiny before a Division Bench of the High Court under Article 226/227.
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Administrative Tribunals under Art 323A can be established ONLY BY PARLIAMENT (State Legislatures have no power under 323A). Litigants cannot approach Supreme Court directly from CAT; must first go to High Court Division Bench.

flashcard 384:
frontside:
Article 323B - PART XIVA - TRIBUNALS
Prompt: What does Article 323B provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Tribunals for other matters
Status: In Force
Mandate: Tribunals for other matters: Parliament or State Legislature may by law establish tribunals for: (a) Taxation; (b) Foreign exchange/customs; (c) Industrial and labour disputes; (d) Land reforms; (e) Urban property ceiling; (f) Elections to Parliament/Legislatures (excluding election petitions under 329); (g) Rent and tenancy.
Key Provisions: Inserted by 42nd Amendment Act, 1976. Unlike 323A, tribunals under 323B can be created by BOTH Parliament and State Legislatures (within their legislative competence).
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: 323A is for public service matters ONLY and created ONLY by Parliament; 323B is for multiple subject-matters and can be created by BOTH Parliament and State Legislatures.

## PART XV - ELECTIONS

flashcard 385:
frontside:
Article 324 - PART XV - ELECTIONS
Prompt: What does Article 324 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Superintendence, direction and control of elections to be vested in an Election Commission
Status: In Force
Mandate: Superintendence, direction and control of elections to be vested in an Election Commission: Conducts elections to Parliament, State Legislatures, President, and Vice-President.
Key Provisions: COMPOSITION: Chief Election Commissioner (CEC) + other Election Commissioners (ECs) appointed by President. Anoop Baranwal v Union of India (2023): SC directed appointment by committee of PM, CJI, and Leader of Opposition. Chief Election Commissioner and other Election Commissioners (Appointment, Conditions of Service and Term of Office) Act, 2023 replaced CJI with a Union Cabinet Minister. REMOVAL: CEC can be removed ONLY in like manner and on like grounds as an SC Judge (Art 124(4) special majority). Other ECs cannot be removed EXCEPT on the recommendation of the CEC.
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Election Commission of India does NOT conduct elections to Panchayats and Municipalities (conducted by State Election Commissions under Art 243K/243ZA). CEC has removal parity with SC Judge; other ECs do NOT have independent security of tenure (can be removed on CEC recommendation).

flashcard 386:
frontside:
Article 325 - PART XV - ELECTIONS
Prompt: What does Article 325 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: No person to be ineligible for inclusion in, or to claim to be included in a special, electoral roll on grounds of religion, race, caste or sex
Status: In Force
Mandate: No person to be ineligible for inclusion in, or to claim to be included in a special, electoral roll on grounds of religion, race, caste or sex.
Key Provisions: Establishes a single, unified general electoral roll for every territorial constituency in India.
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Prohibits separate electorates based on religion or caste; secular basis of Indian democracy.

flashcard 387:
frontside:
Article 326 - PART XV - ELECTIONS
Prompt: What does Article 326 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Elections to the House of the People and to the Legislative Assemblies of States to be on the basis of adult suffrage
Status: In Force
Mandate: Elections to the House of the People and to the Legislative Assemblies of States to be on the basis of ADULT SUFFRAGE: Minimum voting age is 18 YEARS.
Key Provisions: 61st Constitutional Amendment Act, 1988 (operational 1989) lowered the voting age from 21 years to 18 years.
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Voting is a CONSTITUTIONAL/STATUTORY RIGHT under Art 326 and Section 62 of RPA 1951, NOT a Fundamental Right (PUCL case, Anoop Baranwal case).

flashcard 388:
frontside:
Article 327 - PART XV - ELECTIONS
Prompt: What does Article 327 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Power of Parliament to make provision with respect to elections to Legislatures
Status: In Force
Mandate: Power of Parliament to make provision with respect to elections to Legislatures: Enact laws for delimitation, electoral rolls, conduct of elections.
Key Provisions: Parliament passed Representation of the People Act 1950, RPA 1951, and Delimitation Commission Acts under this power.
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Parliament has primary legislative competence for parliamentary and state assembly elections.

flashcard 389:
frontside:
Article 328 - PART XV - ELECTIONS
Prompt: What does Article 328 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Power of Legislature of a State to make provision with respect to elections to such Legislature
Status: In Force
Mandate: Power of Legislature of a State to make provision with respect to elections to such Legislature, in so far as provision is not made by Parliament.
Key Provisions: Subordinate/supplementary legislative power of States regarding state elections.
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: State legislation can operate only on matters not covered by parliamentary law.

flashcard 390:
frontside:
Article 329 - PART XV - ELECTIONS
Prompt: What does Article 329 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Bar to interference by courts in electoral matters
Status: In Force
Mandate: Bar to interference by courts in electoral matters: Delimitation of constituencies or allotment of seats cannot be questioned in any court. No election can be called in question EXCEPT BY AN ELECTION PETITION presented to High Court (RPA 1951).
Key Provisions: Courts cannot interfere during the ongoing electoral process from notification to declaration of results (Mohinder Singh Gill case 1978).
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Judicial review is post-election only, via an election petition before the High Court.

flashcard 391:
frontside:
Article 329A - PART XV - ELECTIONS
Prompt: What does Article 329A provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Special provision as to elections to Parliament in the case of Prime Minister and Speaker - [Omitted]
Status: [Omitted / Repealed]
Mandate: Special provision as to elections to Parliament in the case of Prime Minister and Speaker [Omitted].
Key Provisions: Inserted by 39th Amendment Act, 1975 during Emergency to immunise Indira Gandhi's election from judicial review. Core clause STRUCK DOWN in landmark Indira Nehru Gandhi v Raj Narain (1975) as violating free and fair elections and basic structure. Article omitted completely by 44th Amendment Act, 1978.
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: First case where a Constitutional Amendment was struck down using the Basic Structure doctrine.

## PART XVI - SPECIAL PROVISIONS RELATING TO CERTAIN CLASSES

flashcard 392:
frontside:
Article 330 - PART XVI - SPECIAL PROVISIONS RELATING TO CERTAIN CLASSES
Prompt: What does Article 330 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: 
Status: In Force
Mandate: Reservation of seats for Scheduled Castes and Scheduled Tribes in the House of the People (Lok Sabha) in proportion to their population.
Key Provisions: Currently 84 seats reserved for SCs and 47 seats reserved for STs in Lok Sabha (total 131 reserved seats out of 543).
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Reservation is strictly based on population proportion in each State/UT.

flashcard 393:
frontside:
Article 330A - PART XVI - SPECIAL PROVISIONS RELATING TO CERTAIN CLASSES
Prompt: What does Article 330A provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Reservation of seats for Scheduled Castes and Scheduled Tribes in the House of the People. Reservation of seats for women in the House of the People
Status: In Force
Mandate: Official constitutional provision governing Reservation of seats for Scheduled Castes and Scheduled Tribes in the House of the People. Reservation of seats for women in the House of the People within PART XVI - SPECIAL PROVISIONS RELATING TO CERTAIN CLASSES.
Key Provisions: Prescribes institutional mechanisms, procedural rules, and statutory boundaries for constitutional administration.
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Read in conjunction with parent chapter provisions to verify whether power is discretionary or exercised on aid and advice.

flashcard 394:
frontside:
Article 331 - PART XVI - SPECIAL PROVISIONS RELATING TO CERTAIN CLASSES
Prompt: What does Article 331 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Representation of the Anglo-Indian community in the House of the People
Status: In Force
Mandate: Representation of the Anglo-Indian Community in the House of the People: President could nominate up to 2 members if inadequately represented.
Key Provisions: DISCONTINUED: The 104th Constitutional Amendment Act, 2019 did not extend the Anglo-Indian nomination beyond January 2020. Hence, Anglo-Indian nomination in Lok Sabha stands abolished.
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Anglo-Indian nomination in Lok Sabha no longer exists post-104th Amendment Act, 2019.

flashcard 395:
frontside:
Article 332 - PART XVI - SPECIAL PROVISIONS RELATING TO CERTAIN CLASSES
Prompt: What does Article 332 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: 
Status: In Force
Mandate: Reservation of seats for Scheduled Castes and Scheduled Tribes in Legislative Assemblies of the States in proportion to population.
Key Provisions: Mandatory in all State Legislative Assemblies based on Census figures.
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Applies to Legislative Assemblies (Vidhan Sabha); there is NO reservation for SC/ST in Legislative Councils (Vidhan Parishad) or Rajya Sabha.

flashcard 396:
frontside:
Article 332A - PART XVI - SPECIAL PROVISIONS RELATING TO CERTAIN CLASSES
Prompt: What does Article 332A provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Reservation of seats for Scheduled Castes and Scheduled Tribes in the Legislative Assemblies of the States. Reservation of seats for women in the Legislative Assemblies of the States
Status: In Force
Mandate: Official constitutional provision governing Reservation of seats for Scheduled Castes and Scheduled Tribes in the Legislative Assemblies of the States. Reservation of seats for women in the Legislative Assemblies of the States within PART XVI - SPECIAL PROVISIONS RELATING TO CERTAIN CLASSES.
Key Provisions: Prescribes institutional mechanisms, procedural rules, and statutory boundaries for constitutional administration.
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Read in conjunction with parent chapter provisions to verify whether power is discretionary or exercised on aid and advice.

flashcard 397:
frontside:
Article 333 - PART XVI - SPECIAL PROVISIONS RELATING TO CERTAIN CLASSES
Prompt: What does Article 333 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Representation of the Anglo-Indian community in the Legislative Assemblies of the States
Status: In Force
Mandate: Representation of the Anglo-Indian community in Legislative Assemblies of the States: Governor could nominate 1 member.
Key Provisions: DISCONTINUED by the 104th Constitutional Amendment Act, 2019 along with Article 331.
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Governor's nomination of 1 Anglo-Indian to State Assemblies stands abolished.

flashcard 398:
frontside:
Article 334 - PART XVI - SPECIAL PROVISIONS RELATING TO CERTAIN CLASSES
Prompt: What does Article 334 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: 
Status: In Force
Mandate: Reservation of seats and special representation to cease after eighty years: Originally provided for 10 years (until 1960). Extended every 10 years by constitutional amendments.
Key Provisions: The 104th Amendment Act, 2019 extended SC/ST reservations in Lok Sabha and State Assemblies until 25 JANUARY 2030 (80 years from 1950), while letting Anglo-Indian nominations lapse.
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Only SC and ST political reservation was extended to 2030; Anglo-Indian nomination was deliberately omitted.

flashcard 399:
frontside:
Article 334A - PART XVI - SPECIAL PROVISIONS RELATING TO CERTAIN CLASSES
Prompt: What does Article 334A provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Reservation of seats and special representation to cease after certain period. Reservation of seats for women take effect
Status: In Force
Mandate: Official constitutional provision governing Reservation of seats and special representation to cease after certain period. Reservation of seats for women take effect within PART XVI - SPECIAL PROVISIONS RELATING TO CERTAIN CLASSES.
Key Provisions: Prescribes institutional mechanisms, procedural rules, and statutory boundaries for constitutional administration.
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Read in conjunction with parent chapter provisions to verify whether power is discretionary or exercised on aid and advice.

flashcard 400:
frontside:
Article 335 - PART XVI - SPECIAL PROVISIONS RELATING TO CERTAIN CLASSES
Prompt: What does Article 335 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Claims of Scheduled Castes and Scheduled Tribes to services and posts
Status: In Force
Mandate: Claims of Scheduled Castes and Scheduled Tribes to services and posts: Taken into consideration, CONSISTENTLY WITH THE MAINTENANCE OF EFFICIENCY OF ADMINISTRATION.
Key Provisions: Proviso added by 82nd Amendment Act, 2000: Enables State to make relaxations in qualifying marks or standards of evaluation in examinations/promotions for SCs and STs.
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Administrative efficiency is a constitutional balance, but 82nd Amendment allows lowering qualifying marks for SC/ST candidates.

flashcard 401:
frontside:
Article 336 - PART XVI - SPECIAL PROVISIONS RELATING TO CERTAIN CLASSES
Prompt: What does Article 336 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Special provision for Anglo-Indian community in certain services
Status: In Force
Mandate: Official constitutional provision governing Special provision for Anglo-Indian community in certain services within PART XVI - SPECIAL PROVISIONS RELATING TO CERTAIN CLASSES.
Key Provisions: Prescribes institutional mechanisms, procedural rules, and statutory boundaries for constitutional administration.
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Read in conjunction with parent chapter provisions to verify whether power is discretionary or exercised on aid and advice.

flashcard 402:
frontside:
Article 337 - PART XVI - SPECIAL PROVISIONS RELATING TO CERTAIN CLASSES
Prompt: What does Article 337 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Special provision with respect to educational grants for the benefit of Anglo-Indian Community
Status: In Force
Mandate: Official constitutional provision governing Special provision with respect to educational grants for the benefit of Anglo-Indian Community within PART XVI - SPECIAL PROVISIONS RELATING TO CERTAIN CLASSES.
Key Provisions: Prescribes institutional mechanisms, procedural rules, and statutory boundaries for constitutional administration.
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Read in conjunction with parent chapter provisions to verify whether power is discretionary or exercised on aid and advice.

flashcard 403:
frontside:
Article 338 - PART XVI - SPECIAL PROVISIONS RELATING TO CERTAIN CLASSES
Prompt: What does Article 338 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: National Commission for Scheduled Castes
Status: In Force
Mandate: National Commission for Scheduled Castes (NCSC): Constitutional body created by Art 338. Consists of Chairperson, Vice-Chairperson, and 3 other Members appointed by President by warrant.
Key Provisions: 89th Amendment Act, 2003 bifurcated the erstwhile joint Commission into two separate bodies: NCSC (Art 338) and NCST (Art 338A). Vested with POWERS OF A CIVIL COURT while investigating complaints (summoning witnesses, discovery of documents, receiving affidavits). Submits annual report to President.
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Has powers of a Civil Court for inquiry/investigation, but its recommendations are ADVISORY, not binding adjudicatory orders.

flashcard 404:
frontside:
Article 338A - PART XVI - SPECIAL PROVISIONS RELATING TO CERTAIN CLASSES
Prompt: What does Article 338A provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: National Commission for Scheduled Tribes
Status: In Force
Mandate: National Commission for Scheduled Tribes (NCST): Created as a separate constitutional body by the 89th Constitutional Amendment Act, 2003.
Key Provisions: Same composition (Chairperson, VC, 3 Members) and Civil Court powers as NCSC. Special duties: Protection, rehabilitation, ownership rights over minor forest produce, mineral/water rights for STs under PESA and Forest Rights Act 2006.
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Separate from NCSC since 2003; works under Ministry of Tribal Affairs.

flashcard 405:
frontside:
Article 338B - PART XVI - SPECIAL PROVISIONS RELATING TO CERTAIN CLASSES
Prompt: What does Article 338B provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: National Commission for Backward Classes
Status: In Force
Mandate: National Commission for Backward Classes (NCBC): Accorded CONSTITUTIONAL STATUS by the 102nd Constitutional Amendment Act, 2018.
Key Provisions: Replaced statutory NCBC (set up under 1993 Act post-Indra Sawhney). Same composition (Chairperson, VC, 3 Members) and Civil Court powers. Inquires into complaints of SEBCs, advises on socio-economic development.
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: NCBC was statutory from 1993 to 2018; became a full Constitutional Body under Art 338B via 102nd Amendment Act, 2018.

flashcard 406:
frontside:
Article 339 - PART XVI - SPECIAL PROVISIONS RELATING TO CERTAIN CLASSES
Prompt: What does Article 339 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Control of the Union over the administration of Scheduled Areas and the welfare of Scheduled Tribes
Status: In Force
Mandate: Official constitutional provision governing Control of the Union over the administration of Scheduled Areas and the welfare of Scheduled Tribes within PART XVI - SPECIAL PROVISIONS RELATING TO CERTAIN CLASSES.
Key Provisions: Prescribes institutional mechanisms, procedural rules, and statutory boundaries for constitutional administration.
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Read in conjunction with parent chapter provisions to verify whether power is discretionary or exercised on aid and advice.

flashcard 407:
frontside:
Article 340 - PART XVI - SPECIAL PROVISIONS RELATING TO CERTAIN CLASSES
Prompt: What does Article 340 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Appointment of a Commission to investigate the conditions of backward classes
Status: In Force
Mandate: Appointment of a Commission to investigate the conditions of backward classes: President may appoint a Commission to investigate conditions of socially and educationally backward classes and make recommendations.
Key Provisions: Two historic Commissions appointed: (1) First Backward Classes Commission chaired by Kaka Kalelkar (1953); (2) Second Backward Classes Commission chaired by B.P. Mandal (1979 - recommended 27% OBC quota, implemented in 1990). Justice Rohini Commission (2017) examined sub-categorisation of OBCs.
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Article 340 is the constitutional root of all national backward class commissions.

flashcard 408:
frontside:
Article 341 - PART XVI - SPECIAL PROVISIONS RELATING TO CERTAIN CLASSES
Prompt: What does Article 341 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Scheduled Castes
Status: In Force
Mandate: Scheduled Castes: President, after consultation with Governor of a State, specifies by public notification the castes, races, or tribes deemed to be Scheduled Castes in relation to that State/UT.
Key Provisions: CRITICAL CLAUSE (2): Once notified by President, the list CAN BE INCLUDED IN OR EXCLUDED FROM ONLY BY LAW MADE BY PARLIAMENT. The President CANNOT subsequently alter the list by notification.
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Only PARLIAMENT by law can add or remove a caste from the SC list; neither the President nor the State Government can alter the list once notified.

flashcard 409:
frontside:
Article 342 - PART XVI - SPECIAL PROVISIONS RELATING TO CERTAIN CLASSES
Prompt: What does Article 342 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Scheduled Tribes
Status: In Force
Mandate: Scheduled Tribes: President specifies by public notification after consulting Governor; only PARLIAMENT by law can include or exclude tribes from the ST list.
Key Provisions: Matches Article 341 procedure for Scheduled Castes.
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: President notifies initially; only Parliament can amend thereafter.

flashcard 410:
frontside:
Article 342A - PART XVI - SPECIAL PROVISIONS RELATING TO CERTAIN CLASSES
Prompt: What does Article 342A provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Socially and educationally backward classes
Status: In Force
Mandate: Socially and Educationally Backward Classes (SEBCs): Inserted by 102nd Amdt 2018. President notifies Central List of SEBCs.
Key Provisions: 105th Constitutional Amendment Act, 2021 amended Art 342A to clarify that States and UTs have the power to prepare and maintain their OWN STATE LIST of SEBCs for state-level reservations (overcoming SC ruling in Maratha reservation case).
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: 105th Amendment, 2021 restored the power of States to maintain their own state OBC lists.

## PART XVII - OFFICIAL LANGUAGE

flashcard 411:
frontside:
Article 343 - PART XVII - OFFICIAL LANGUAGE | CHAPTER I. - LANGUAGE OF THE UNION
Prompt: What does Article 343 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Official language of the Union
Status: In Force
Mandate: Official language of the Union: Hindi in Devanagari script. Form of numerals for official purposes shall be the INTERNATIONAL FORM of Indian numerals.
Key Provisions: English permitted to continue for 15 years (until 1965). Official Languages Act, 1963 permitted continued use of English indefinitely alongside Hindi for official work of Union and Parliament.
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: India has NO 'National Language' in the Constitution; Hindi is the 'Official Language of the Union'. Numerals are international Arabic (1, 2, 3), not Devanagari.

flashcard 412:
frontside:
Article 344 - PART XVII - OFFICIAL LANGUAGE | CHAPTER I. - LANGUAGE OF THE UNION
Prompt: What does Article 344 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Commission and Committee of Parliament on official language
Status: In Force
Mandate: Commission and Committee of Parliament on Official Language: President constitutes Commission after 5 and 10 years (BG Kher Commission 1955). Committee of Parliament consists of 30 MEMBERS (20 from Lok Sabha, 10 from Rajya Sabha) elected by PR-STV.
Key Provisions: Parliamentary Committee on Official Language reviews progress of Hindi in Union administration and reports to President.
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Committee ratio: 20 Lok Sabha + 10 Rajya Sabha (total 30 members), elected by proportional representation.

flashcard 413:
frontside:
Article 345 - PART XVII - OFFICIAL LANGUAGE | CHAPTER II. -  REGIONAL LANGUAGES
Prompt: What does Article 345 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Official language or languages of a State
Status: In Force
Mandate: Official language or languages of a State: State Legislature may by law adopt any one or more languages used in the State or Hindi as official language.
Key Provisions: English continues as official language within State until State Legislature provides otherwise by law.
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: State is free to choose any language; not restricted to languages in the Eighth Schedule.

flashcard 414:
frontside:
Article 346 - PART XVII - OFFICIAL LANGUAGE | CHAPTER II. -  REGIONAL LANGUAGES
Prompt: What does Article 346 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Official language for communication between one State and another or between a State and the Union
Status: In Force
Mandate: Official language for communication between one State and another or between a State and the Union: Language authorised for Union (English/Hindi).
Key Provisions: Two or more States may agree that Hindi shall be the official language for communication between them.
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Inter-state official communications default to the Union official language.

flashcard 415:
frontside:
Article 347 - PART XVII - OFFICIAL LANGUAGE | CHAPTER II. -  REGIONAL LANGUAGES
Prompt: What does Article 347 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Special provision relating to language spoken by a section of the population of a State
Status: In Force
Mandate: Special provision relating to language spoken by a section of the population of a State: President may direct that such language be officially recognised in the State.
Key Provisions: Requires substantial proportion of state population desiring recognition.
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Presidential power to protect minority language within a State.

flashcard 416:
frontside:
Article 348 - PART XVII - OFFICIAL LANGUAGE | CHAPTER III. - LANGUAGE OF THE SUPREME COURT,
Prompt: What does Article 348 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Language to be used in the Supreme Court and in the High Courts and for Acts, Bills, etc
Status: In Force
Mandate: Language to be used in Supreme Court and High Courts and for Acts, Bills, etc.: SHALL BE IN THE ENGLISH LANGUAGE until Parliament by law otherwise provides.
Key Provisions: Governor of a State may, with PREVIOUS CONSENT OF THE PRESIDENT, authorise use of Hindi or state official language in HC proceedings (proviso). BUT judgments, decrees, and orders of High Court MUST STILL BE IN ENGLISH (Art 348(1)(a)). Authoritative texts of Bills and Acts in Parliament/Legislatures must be in English.
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Judgments, decrees and orders of Supreme Court and High Courts MUST be in English. Governor can authorise Hindi only for proceedings/arguments, not judgments, and only with President's prior consent.

flashcard 417:
frontside:
Article 349 - PART XVII - OFFICIAL LANGUAGE | CHAPTER III. - LANGUAGE OF THE SUPREME COURT,
Prompt: What does Article 349 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Special procedure for enactment of certain laws relating to language
Status: In Force
Mandate: Official constitutional provision governing Special procedure for enactment of certain laws relating to language within PART XVII - OFFICIAL LANGUAGE | CHAPTER III. - LANGUAGE OF THE SUPREME COURT,.
Key Provisions: Prescribes institutional mechanisms, procedural rules, and statutory boundaries for constitutional administration.
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Read in conjunction with parent chapter provisions to verify whether power is discretionary or exercised on aid and advice.

flashcard 418:
frontside:
Article 350 - PART XVII - OFFICIAL LANGUAGE | CHAPTER IV. - SPECIAL DIRECTIVES
Prompt: What does Article 350 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Language to be used in representations for redress of grievances
Status: In Force
Mandate: Language to be used in representations for redress of grievances: Every person entitled to submit representation to any officer/authority of Union or State in ANY OF THE LANGUAGES USED IN THE UNION OR IN THE STATE.
Key Provisions: Constitutional grievance redressal right: cannot be rejected merely because it is not in official language.
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Citizen can write grievance in ANY language used in Union or State.

flashcard 419:
frontside:
Article 350A - PART XVII - OFFICIAL LANGUAGE | CHAPTER IV. - SPECIAL DIRECTIVES
Prompt: What does Article 350A provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Facilities for instruction in mother-tongue at primary stage
Status: In Force
Mandate: Facilities for instruction in mother-tongue at primary stage: Every State and local authority shall endeavour to provide adequate facilities for instruction in mother-tongue at primary stage to children belonging to linguistic minority groups.
Key Provisions: Inserted by 7th Amendment Act, 1956. President may issue directions to any State for securing facilities.
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Focuses on primary stage education for linguistic minority children.

flashcard 420:
frontside:
Article 350B - PART XVII - OFFICIAL LANGUAGE | CHAPTER IV. - SPECIAL DIRECTIVES
Prompt: What does Article 350B provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Special Officer for linguistic minorities
Status: In Force
Mandate: Special Officer for Linguistic Minorities: Appointed by the PRESIDENT. Duty to investigate all matters relating to safeguards provided for linguistic minorities under Constitution.
Key Provisions: Inserted by 7th Amendment Act, 1956. Submits annual reports to PRESIDENT, which are laid before each House of Parliament and sent to concerned State Governments.
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Appointed by President; operates under Ministry of Minority Affairs; headquarters at Prayagraj (Allahabad).

flashcard 421:
frontside:
Article 351 - PART XVII - OFFICIAL LANGUAGE | CHAPTER IV. - SPECIAL DIRECTIVES
Prompt: What does Article 351 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Directive for development of the Hindi language
Status: In Force
Mandate: Directive for development of the Hindi language: Duty of Union to promote spread of Hindi and develop it so that it serves as medium of expression for all elements of the COMPOSITE CULTURE of India.
Key Provisions: Directs securing enrichment of Hindi by assimilating forms, style and expressions used in Hindustani and Eighth Schedule languages, drawing vocabulary primarily from SANSKRIT.
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Constitutional directive for Hindi: vocabulary primarily from Sanskrit, secondary from other Eighth Schedule languages.

## PART XVIII - EMERGENCY PROVISIONS

flashcard 422:
frontside:
Article 352 - PART XVIII - EMERGENCY PROVISIONS
Prompt: What does Article 352 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Proclamation of Emergency
Status: In Force
Mandate: Proclamation of Emergency (National Emergency): President may proclaim if satisfied that security of India or any part is threatened by WAR, EXTERNAL AGGRESSION, or ARMED REBELLION.
Key Provisions: 44th Amdt 1978: (1) Replaced 'internal disturbance' with 'ARMED REBELLION'; (2) President can issue proclamation ONLY on WRITTEN RECOMMENDATION OF THE CABINET (PM + Cabinet ministers; only place 'Cabinet' appears in Constitution); (3) Must be approved by BOTH Houses of Parliament by SPECIAL MAJORITY (majority of total + 2/3 present & voting) WITHIN 1 MONTH (was 2 months); (4) Stays in force for 6 MONTHS at a time (infinite extensions possible, 6 months each); (5) Revocation by President at any time OR if Lok Sabha passes a resolution disapproving it by SIMPLE MAJORITY (1/10th LS members can requisition special sitting). Imposed 3 times: 1962 (China), 1971 (Pakistan), 1975 (internal disturbance).
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Requires SPECIAL MAJORITY to approve/extend (special majority in both Houses); requires only SIMPLE MAJORITY of Lok Sabha alone to revoke.

flashcard 423:
frontside:
Article 353 - PART XVIII - EMERGENCY PROVISIONS
Prompt: What does Article 353 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Effect of Proclamation of Emergency
Status: In Force
Mandate: Effect of Proclamation of Emergency: (a) Executive power of Union extends to giving directions to ANY State on manner of exercising executive power; (b) Parliament has power to make laws on State List subjects.
Key Provisions: Federal structure transformed into a unitary system without formal constitutional amendment.
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: State Governments are NOT dismissed and State Legislatures are NOT dissolved; they remain in existence, but are subject to concurrent central legislative/executive control.

flashcard 424:
frontside:
Article 354 - PART XVIII - EMERGENCY PROVISIONS
Prompt: What does Article 354 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Application of provisions relating to distribution of revenues while a Proclamation of Emergency is in operation
Status: In Force
Mandate: Official constitutional provision governing Application of provisions relating to distribution of revenues while a Proclamation of Emergency is in operation within PART XVIII - EMERGENCY PROVISIONS.
Key Provisions: Prescribes institutional mechanisms, procedural rules, and statutory boundaries for constitutional administration.
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Read in conjunction with parent chapter provisions to verify whether power is discretionary or exercised on aid and advice.

flashcard 425:
frontside:
Article 355 - PART XVIII - EMERGENCY PROVISIONS
Prompt: What does Article 355 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Duty of the Union to protect States against external aggression and internal disturbance
Status: In Force
Mandate: Duty of the Union to protect States against external aggression and internal disturbance and to ensure that government of every State is carried on in accordance with provisions of Constitution.
Key Provisions: Constitutional justification and duty under which Union deploys central forces (CRPF, BSF) and imposes Article 356.
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Art 355 is the governing DUTY of the Union; Art 352 and 356 are the emergency POWERS triggered when that duty must be enforced.

flashcard 426:
frontside:
Article 356 - PART XVIII - EMERGENCY PROVISIONS
Prompt: What does Article 356 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Provisions in case of failure of constitutional machinery in States
Status: In Force
Mandate: Provisions in case of failure of constitutional machinery in States (President's Rule / State Emergency): President can assume functions of State Government if satisfied governance cannot be carried on in accordance with Constitution.
Key Provisions: Triggered on GOVERNOR'S REPORT OR SUO MOTU (Art 356(1)), or failure to comply with Union directions (Art 365). APPROVAL: Approved by BOTH Houses by SIMPLE MAJORITY WITHIN 2 MONTHS. Stays in force for 6 MONTHS at a time; MAXIMUM LIMIT = 3 YEARS. 44th Amdt: Beyond 1 year, can be extended only if: (a) National Emergency in operation; AND (b) Election Commission certifies elections cannot be held. S.R. BOMMAI CASE (1994): (1) Subject to judicial review; (2) Assembly cannot be dissolved until Parliament approves (only suspended animation); (3) Floor test is mandatory; (4) Secularism is basic structure; (5) Court can revive dissolved assembly if imposition held unconstitutional.
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Approved by SIMPLE MAJORITY (unlike Art 352 special majority); max 3 years (unlike Art 352 which has no max limit); State Government is dismissed and Assembly is dissolved/suspended.

flashcard 427:
frontside:
Article 357 - PART XVIII - EMERGENCY PROVISIONS
Prompt: What does Article 357 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Exercise of legislative powers under Proclamation issued under article 356
Status: In Force
Mandate: Exercise of legislative powers under Proclamation issued under Article 356: Parliament may declare powers of State Legislature exercisable by or under authority of President.
Key Provisions: President can delegate law-making power to Parliament or authorise expenditure from State Consolidated Fund pending sanction.
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Laws made by Parliament/President during Art 356 remain in force even after proclamation ceases, until altered/repealed by State Legislature (44th Amdt).

flashcard 428:
frontside:
Article 358 - PART XVIII - EMERGENCY PROVISIONS
Prompt: What does Article 358 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Suspension of provisions of article 19 during emergencies
Status: In Force
Mandate: Suspension of provisions of Article 19 during emergencies: AUTOMATIC SUSPENSION of 6 freedoms under Article 19.
Key Provisions: CRITICAL 44TH AMENDMENT SAFEGUARDS: (1) Art 19 is suspended ONLY during emergency proclaimed on grounds of WAR or EXTERNAL AGGRESSION (EXTERNAL EMERGENCY); Art 19 is NOT suspended if emergency is on ground of Armed Rebellion (INTERNAL EMERGENCY); (2) Only laws containing a specific recital that they are in relation to emergency are protected from Art 19 challenge.
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Art 358 is AUTOMATIC (no presidential order needed), but applies ONLY to External Emergency (war/aggression), NEVER to Armed Rebellion.

flashcard 429:
frontside:
Article 359 - PART XVIII - EMERGENCY PROVISIONS
Prompt: What does Article 359 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Suspension of the enforcement of the rights conferred by Part III during emergencies
Status: In Force
Mandate: Suspension of the enforcement of the rights conferred by Part III during emergencies: President may by order declare that the right to move any court for enforcement of specified FRs shall remain suspended.
Key Provisions: NON-AUTOMATIC (requires a specific Presidential Order specifying which rights are suspended). CRITICAL 44TH AMENDMENT EXCEPTION: The President CANNOT SUSPEND THE ENFORCEMENT OF ARTICLES 20 AND 21 UNDER ANY CIRCUMSTANCES. ADM Jabalpur (1976) was completely neutralized by 44th Amdt and later formally overruled in Puttaswamy (2017).
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Art 359 suspends the ENFORCEMENT of rights (remedial right under Art 32), not the substantive rights themselves. Articles 20 and 21 can NEVER be suspended.

flashcard 430:
frontside:
Article 359A - PART XVIII - EMERGENCY PROVISIONS
Prompt: What does Article 359A provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Application of this Part to the State of Punjab - [Omitted]
Status: [Omitted / Repealed]
Mandate: This article has been omitted or repealed from the Constitution (typically by the 7th Amendment Act 1956 or subsequent amendments on federal restructuring/privy purses).
Key Provisions: Retained in constitutional numbering to preserve the integrity of subsequent article references without renumbering the entire Constitution.
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Articles are omitted rather than renumbered to keep historical citations and cross-references constant across statutes.

flashcard 431:
frontside:
Article 360 - PART XVIII - EMERGENCY PROVISIONS
Prompt: What does Article 360 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Provisions as to financial emergency
Status: In Force
Mandate: Provisions as to Financial Emergency: If President satisfied that financial stability or credit of India or any part is threatened.
Key Provisions: Approved by BOTH Houses by SIMPLE MAJORITY WITHIN 2 MONTHS. Continues indefinitely until revoked by President (no max period, no periodic re-approval needed). EFFECTS: (1) Union can direct States to observe canons of financial propriety; (2) President can direct reduction of salaries/allowances of all persons serving Union or States, INCLUDING JUDGES OF SUPREME COURT AND HIGH COURTS; (3) State Money Bills reserved for President's consideration.
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Financial Emergency has NEVER BEEN DECLARED in India so far. Supreme Court and High Court judges' salaries can be reduced during Financial Emergency.

## PART XIX - MISCELLANEOUS

flashcard 432:
frontside:
Article 361 - PART XIX - MISCELLANEOUS
Prompt: What does Article 361 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Protection of President and Governors and Rajpramukhs
Status: In Force
Mandate: Protection of President and Governors: Immune from court proceedings for exercise of powers of office. No criminal proceedings whatsoever can be instituted or continued during term of office. No process for arrest or imprisonment.
Key Provisions: Civil proceedings can be instituted for personal acts ONLY after 2 MONTHS written notice specifying cause of action.
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Criminal immunity is absolute during term of office; civil immunity is subject to 2 months notice for personal acts.

flashcard 433:
frontside:
Article 361A - PART XIX - MISCELLANEOUS
Prompt: What does Article 361A provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Protection of publication of proceedings of Parliament and State Legislatures
Status: In Force
Mandate: Protection of publication of proceedings of Parliament and State Legislatures: No person liable to civil/criminal proceedings for publishing substantially true report.
Key Provisions: Inserted by 44th Amendment Act, 1978 (statutory foundation earlier created by Feroze Gandhi Act 1956). Must be without malice and in public good. Does not protect secret sittings.
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Constitutional protection for media reporting on parliamentary proceedings; does not apply to secret sittings.

flashcard 434:
frontside:
Article 361B - PART XIX - MISCELLANEOUS
Prompt: What does Article 361B provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Disqualification for appointment on remunerative political post
Status: In Force
Mandate: Disqualification for appointment on remunerative political post: A member disqualified under Tenth Schedule (Anti-defection) is disqualified from holding any remunerative political post.
Key Provisions: Inserted by 91st Amendment Act, 2003. Bars disqualified defectors from being appointed Ministers or heads of statutory bodies/corporations until next election.
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Prevents defectors from receiving ministerial berths or lucrative chairman posts.

flashcard 435:
frontside:
Article 362 - PART XIX - MISCELLANEOUS
Prompt: What does Article 362 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:

backside:
Title: Effect of failure to comply wi th, or to give effect to, directions given by the Union
Status: In Force
Mandate: Effect of failure to comply with, or to give effect to, directions given by the Union: Where any State fails to comply with lawful central direction, President may hold that a situation has arisen in which governance CANNOT BE CARRIED ON IN ACCORDANCE WITH THE CONSTITUTION.
Key Provisions: Constitutional trigger connecting Union administrative directions (Arts 256, 257) directly with President's Rule under Article 356.
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Provides the constitutional justification for imposing President's Rule under Art 356 when a State defies central lawful directions.

## PART XX - AMENDMENT OF THE CONSTITUTION

flashcard 442:
frontside:
Article 368 - PART XX - AMENDMENT OF THE CONSTITUTION
Prompt: What does Article 368 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Power of Parliament to amend the Constitution and procedure therefor
Status: In Force
Mandate: Power of Parliament to amend the Constitution and procedure therefor: Parliament may in exercise of its constituent power amend by way of addition, variation or repeal any provision of the Constitution.
Key Provisions: PROCEDURE: (1) Introduced in EITHER House (not in State Legislatures); (2) No prior Presidential recommendation needed; (3) Passed in EACH House by a SPECIAL MAJORITY: Majority of total membership + 2/3rd of members present and voting; (4) NO PROVISION FOR JOINT SITTING (each House must pass separately); (5) If it amends FEDERAL PROVISIONS (Arts 54, 55, 73, 162, 241, Part V Ch IV, Part VI Ch V, Part XI Ch I, 7th Schedule, representation in Parliament, Art 368), it must also be RATIFIED BY LEGISLATURES OF NOT LESS THAN HALF OF THE STATES by simple majority; (6) President MUST GIVE ASSENT (24th Amdt made assent mandatory). BASIC STRUCTURE DOCTRINE: Kesavananda Bharati v State of Kerala (1973) held constituent power is NOT unlimited; Parliament cannot destroy the 'Basic Structure' of the Constitution. Minerva Mills (1980) struck down Art 368(4)-(5) which claimed unlimited amendment power.
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: President CANNOT withhold assent and CANNOT return a Constitutional Amendment Bill (mandatory assent). No joint sitting exists. Simple majority amendments (Arts 2, 3, 169) are NOT deemed amendments under Art 368.

## PART XXI - TEMPORARY, TRANSITIONAL AND SPECIAL PROVISIONS

Key Provisions: Prescribes institutional mechanisms, procedural rules, and statutory boundaries for constitutional administration.
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Read in conjunction with parent chapter provisions to verify whether power is discretionary or exercised on aid and advice.

flashcard 444:
frontside:
Article 370 - PART XXI - TEMPORARY, TRANSITIONAL AND SPECIAL PROVISIONS
Prompt: What does Article 370 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Temporary provisions with respect to the State of Jammu and Kashmir
Status: In Force
Mandate: Temporary provisions with respect to the State of Jammu and Kashmir: Conferred special autonomous status, restricted Parliament's law-making power, and had separate state constitution.
Key Provisions: RENDERED INOPERATIVE: On 5-6 August 2019, President issued Constitutional Orders (CO 272 and CO 273) with concurrence of Parliament, making all provisions of Indian Constitution applicable to J&K and rendering Art 370 inoperative. J&K Reorganisation Act, 2019 bifurcated state into UT of J&K (with legislature) and UT of Ladakh (without legislature). Upheld unanimously by 5-judge Constitution Bench in Re Article 370 (2023).
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Article 370 was not textually erased from the Constitution, but rendered operative in an entirely new form where all provisions of the Indian Constitution apply unconditionally.

flashcard 445:
frontside:
Article 371 - PART XXI - TEMPORARY, TRANSITIONAL AND SPECIAL PROVISIONS
Prompt: What does Article 371 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Special provision with respect to t he States of Maharashtra and Gujarat
Status: In Force
Mandate: Special provision with respect to the States of Maharashtra and Gujarat: President may provide for separate development boards for Vidarbha, Marathwada, Saurashtra, Kutch.
Key Provisions: Equitable allocation of development funds and technical education/vocational training facilities.
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Relates to developmental regional disparities in Maharashtra and Gujarat.

flashcard 446:
frontside:
Article 371A - PART XXI - TEMPORARY, TRANSITIONAL AND SPECIAL PROVISIONS
Prompt: What does Article 371A provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Special provision with respect to the State of Nagaland. 371B . Special provision with respect to the State of Assam
Status: In Force
Mandate: Special provision with respect to the State of Nagaland: No Act of Parliament in respect of: (1) Religious or social practices of Nagas; (2) Naga customary law and procedure; (3) Administration of civil and criminal justice involving decisions according to Naga customary law; (4) Ownership and transfer of land and its resources, SHALL APPLY TO NAGALAND UNLESS THE LEGISLATIVE ASSEMBLY OF NAGALAND BY RESOLUTION SO DECIDES.
Key Provisions: Inserted by 13th Amendment Act, 1962. Strongest state autonomy safeguard protecting indigenous customary ownership and mineral/land rights.
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Ownership of land AND mineral resources in Nagaland is protected; parliamentary laws do not apply unless State Assembly passes a resolution.

Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Special reporting responsibility of Governor regarding Manipur hill areas.

Status: In Force
Mandate: Special provisions with respect to the State of Sikkim: 22nd State of India. Assembly shall consist of not less than 32 members; 1 seat for Sangha (monks).
Key Provisions: Inserted by 36th Constitutional Amendment Act, 1975. Governor has special responsibility for peace and equitable social/economic advancement. Pre-existing laws of Sikkim continue until amended.
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Sikkim Assembly has min 32 members (exception to Art 170 minimum of 60); includes unique Sangha Buddhist monk constituency.

Key Provisions: Inserted by 53rd Amendment Act, 1986 following Mizo Peace Accord.
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Matches Nagaland's customary law protection, but does NOT include 'resources' in land ownership text.
