Prompt: What does Article 391 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Power of the President to amend the First and Fourth Schedules in certain contingencies - [Omitted]
Status: [Omitted / Repealed]
Mandate: This article has been omitted or repealed from the Constitution (typically by the 7th Amendment Act 1956 or subsequent amendments on federal restructuring/privy purses).
Key Provisions: Retained in constitutional numbering to preserve the integrity of subsequent article references without renumbering the entire Constitution.
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Articles are omitted rather than renumbered to keep historical citations and cross-references constant across statutes.

flashcard 476:
frontside:
Article 392 - PART XXI - TEMPORARY, TRANSITIONAL AND SPECIAL PROVISIONS
Prompt: What does Article 392 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Power of the President to remove difficulties
Status: In Force
Mandate: Power of the President to remove difficulties (Henry VIII clause): President may by order direct Constitution shall have effect subject to adaptations/modifications to remove transition difficulties.
Key Provisions: Power expired after first meeting of Parliament elected under the Constitution.
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Temporary transitional power of the President, now historical.

## PART XXII - SHORT TITLE, COMMENCEMENT, AUTHORITATIVE TEXT IN HINDI AND REPEALS

flashcard 477:
frontside:
Article 393 - PART XXII - SHORT TITLE, COMMENCEMENT, AUTHORITATIVE TEXT IN HINDI AND REPEALS
Prompt: What does Article 393 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Short title
Status: In Force
Mandate: Short title: This Constitution may be called 'The Constitution of India'.
Key Provisions: Official constitutional name of the supreme law of the Republic.
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Constitutional title is 'The Constitution of India'.

flashcard 478:
frontside:
Article 394 - PART XXII - SHORT TITLE, COMMENCEMENT, AUTHORITATIVE TEXT IN HINDI AND REPEALS
Prompt: What does Article 394 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Commencement
Status: In Force
Mandate: Commencement: Declares that the Constitution shall come into force on 26 JANUARY 1950 (Republic Day).
Key Provisions: CRITICAL PROVISO: Specified articles came into force IMMEDIATELY on 26 NOVEMBER 1949: Articles 5, 6, 7, 8, 9 (Citizenship), 60 (President's oath), 324 (Election Commission), 366, 367, 379, 380, 388, 391, 392, 393 and 394 itself.
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Citizenship (5-9), President's oath (60), and Election Commission (324) came into force on 26 November 1949, NOT 26 January 1950.

flashcard 479:
frontside:
Article 394A - PART XXII - SHORT TITLE, COMMENCEMENT, AUTHORITATIVE TEXT IN HINDI AND REPEALS
Prompt: What does Article 394A provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Authoritative text in the Hindi language
Status: In Force
Mandate: Authoritative text in the Hindi language: President causes to be published authoritative Hindi translation of the Constitution and all amendments.
Key Provisions: Inserted by the 58th Constitutional Amendment Act, 1987. Hindi translation has same legal authority as English original.
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Authoritative Hindi text was given constitutional standing by 58th Amendment, 1987.

flashcard 480:
frontside:
Article 395 - PART XXII - SHORT TITLE, COMMENCEMENT, AUTHORITATIVE TEXT IN HINDI AND REPEALS
Prompt: What does Article 395 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?

backside:
Title: Repeals
Status: In Force
Mandate: Repeals: The Indian Independence Act, 1947, and the Government of India Act, 1935, together with all enactments amending or supplementing the latter Act, are HEREBY REPEALED.
Key Provisions: Exception: Abolition of Privy Council Jurisdiction Act, 1949 was NOT repealed (ensuring continuity of Privy Council appeals transfer).
Exam Trap and High-Yield Distinction: Severed India's constitutional taproot from British parliamentary statutes, establishing indigenous constitutional sovereignty.

 - -

# SECTION 2: THE TWELVE SCHEDULES OF THE CONSTITUTION

flashcard 481:
frontside:
First Schedule of the Constitution of India
Prompt: What does the First Schedule deal with? Which articles are linked, what is the complete breakdown of subjects/offices, and what is the primary UPSC trap?

backside:
Title: The States and The Union Territories
Linked Articles: Articles 1 and 4
Mandate and Scope: Contains: (I) The States (names and territorial extents of all 28 States); (II) The Union Territories (names and extents of all 8 UTs).
Complete Provisions and Details: Whenever a new State or UT is created, or boundaries/names are altered (under Art 2 or Art 3), the First Schedule is amended by parliamentary law. Under Art 4(2), such an amendment is NOT deemed an amendment under Art 368 and needs only a SIMPLE MAJORITY.
Exam Trap and Critical Distinction: Amending the First Schedule on state reorganisation does NOT require a special majority under Art 368.

flashcard 482:
frontside:
Second Schedule of the Constitution of India
Prompt: What does the Second Schedule deal with? Which articles are linked, what is the complete breakdown of subjects/offices, and what is the primary UPSC trap?

backside:
Title: Emoluments, Allowances and Privileges of Constitutional Dignitaries
Linked Articles: Articles 59(3), 65(3), 75(6), 97, 125, 148(3), 158(3), 164(5), 186 and 221
Mandate and Scope: Specifies salaries, allowances and privileges of: Part A: President and Governors; Part C: Speaker/Deputy Speaker of Lok Sabha & Chairman/Deputy Chairman of Rajya Sabha, Speaker/Deputy Speaker of State Assemblies & Chairman/Deputy Chairman of Councils; Part D: Judges of Supreme Court and High Courts; Part E: Comptroller and Auditor-General of India.
Complete Provisions and Details: All salaries listed here are CHARGED on the Consolidated Fund of India or State and cannot be varied to their disadvantage during their term of office.
Exam Trap and Critical Distinction: CRITICAL EXCLUSIONS: Does NOT contain the salaries of: Prime Minister, Union Ministers, Chief Ministers, State Ministers, Members of Parliament (MPs), or MLAs. Their salaries are determined by ordinary parliamentary/state legislation.

flashcard 483:
frontside:
Third Schedule of the Constitution of India
Prompt: What does the Third Schedule deal with? Which articles are linked, what is the complete breakdown of subjects/offices, and what is the primary UPSC trap?

backside:
Title: Forms of Oaths or Affirmations
Linked Articles: Articles 75(4), 84(a), 99, 124(6), 148(2), 164(3), 173(a), 188 and 219
Mandate and Scope: Contains standard forms of oath/affirmation for 8 constitutional offices: (1) Union Ministers (office & secrecy); (2) Candidates for Parliament election; (3) Members of Parliament (MPs); (4) Judges of Supreme Court; (5) Comptroller and Auditor-General of India; (6) State Ministers (office & secrecy); (7) Candidates for State Legislature election; (8) Members of State Legislature (MLAs/MLCs); (9) Judges of High Courts.
Complete Provisions and Details: Candidates for election must take oath before an Election Commission authorised officer; after election, take oath before President/Governor or nominee before taking seat.
Exam Trap and Critical Distinction: CRITICAL EXCLUSIONS: Does NOT contain oaths of: (1) PRESIDENT (oath is in Art 60); (2) VICE-PRESIDENT (oath is in Art 69); (3) GOVERNOR (oath is in Art 159). These three dignitaries have their oaths directly in the body text of the Constitution.

flashcard 484:
frontside:
Fourth Schedule of the Constitution of India
Prompt: What does the Fourth Schedule deal with? Which articles are linked, what is the complete breakdown of subjects/offices, and what is the primary UPSC trap?

backside:
Title: Allocation of Seats in the Council of States (Rajya Sabha)
Linked Articles: Articles 4(1) and 80(2)
Mandate and Scope: Allocates Rajya Sabha seats to States and Union Territories based on population. Total seats currently allocated = 233 elected + 12 nominated = 245.
Complete Provisions and Details: Seat distribution ranges from 31 seats (Uttar Pradesh, highest) to 1 seat (smaller northeastern states, Goa, Puducherry). Only 3 UTs currently have representation in Rajya Sabha: Delhi (3), Puducherry (1), and Jammu & Kashmir (4).
Exam Trap and Critical Distinction: Unlike the US Senate (where every state gets equal representation of 2 senators regardless of size), Rajya Sabha seats are allocated on POPULATION proportion, resulting in unequal representation.

flashcard 485:
frontside:
Fifth Schedule of the Constitution of India
Prompt: What does the Fifth Schedule deal with? Which articles are linked, what is the complete breakdown of subjects/offices, and what is the primary UPSC trap?

backside:
Title: Administration and Control of Scheduled Areas and Scheduled Tribes
Linked Articles: Article 244(1)
Mandate and Scope: Applies to Scheduled Areas and STs in 10 States: Andhra Pradesh, Telangana, Jharkhand, Odisha, Chhattisgarh, Madhya Pradesh, Rajasthan, Gujarat, Maharashtra, Himachal Pradesh.
Complete Provisions and Details: KEY PROVISIONS: (1) President declares, increases, alters boundaries of Scheduled Areas in consultation with Governor; (2) Governor submits ANNUAL REPORT to President regarding administration; (3) TRIBES ADVISORY COUNCIL (TAC): Mandatory in each state having Scheduled Areas (max 20 members, 3/4th of whom must be ST MLAs); (4) Governor has legislative filtering power: can direct that any Act of Parliament or State Legislature shall NOT APPLY or apply with modifications to a Scheduled Area, and make regulations prohibiting transfer of tribal land.
Exam Trap and Critical Distinction: Fifth Schedule applies to 10 States OUTSIDE the Northeast; does NOT apply to Assam, Meghalaya, Tripura, and Mizoram (which are governed by the Sixth Schedule).

flashcard 486:
frontside:
Sixth Schedule of the Constitution of India
Prompt: What does the Sixth Schedule deal with? Which articles are linked, what is the complete breakdown of subjects/offices, and what is the primary UPSC trap?

backside:
Title: Administration of Tribal Areas in Assam, Meghalaya, Tripura and Mizoram (AMTM)
Linked Articles: Articles 244(2) and 275(1)
Mandate and Scope: Creates AUTONOMOUS DISTRICT COUNCILS (ADCs) and Regional Councils in the tribal areas of 4 North-Eastern States: Assam (Bodoland, Karbi Anglong, Dima Hasao), Meghalaya (Khasi, Jaintia, Garo Hills), Tripura (Tripura Tribal Areas), Mizoram (Chakma, Mara, Lai).
Complete Provisions and Details: ADCs have extensive LEGISLATIVE, EXECUTIVE, JUDICIAL, and FINANCIAL powers: can make laws on land, forests, village councils, inheritance, marriage; can try petty civil/criminal cases according to tribal customary law; can assess and collect land revenue and levy taxes. ADC consists of MAX 30 MEMBERS (26 elected by adult suffrage for 5 years, 4 nominated by Governor). Acts of Parliament/State do not apply or apply with modifications (directed by Governor in Assam/Meghalaya, or President in Tripura/Mizoram).
Exam Trap and Critical Distinction: Sixth Schedule applies ONLY to 'AMTM' (Assam, Meghalaya, Tripura, Mizoram). Manipur, Nagaland, and Arunachal Pradesh are NOT covered by the Sixth Schedule.

flashcard 487:
frontside:
Seventh Schedule of the Constitution of India
Prompt: What does the Seventh Schedule deal with? Which articles are linked, what is the complete breakdown of subjects/offices, and what is the primary UPSC trap?

backside:
Title: Union, State and Concurrent Legislative Lists
Linked Articles: Article 246
Mandate and Scope: Distributes legislative subjects into 3 Lists: (1) List I - Union List (originally 97 items, now ~100): Defence, atomic energy, foreign affairs, war/peace, railways, banking, insurance, currency, inter-state trade, census, income tax, customs; (2) List II - State List (originally 66 items, now ~61): Public order, police, public health, agriculture, prisons, local government, land, liquor, fisheries, state taxes; (3) List III - Concurrent List (originally 47 items, now ~52): Criminal law, marriage, civil procedure, economic planning, trade unions, social security, stamp duties.
Complete Provisions and Details: 42nd Amendment Act, 1976 transferred 5 SUBJECTS from State List to Concurrent List: (1) Education; (2) Forests; (3) Weights and measures; (4) Protection of wild animals and birds; (5) Administration of justice, constitution and organisation of all courts except SC and High Courts.
Exam Trap and Critical Distinction: Residuary powers (subjects not in any list) belong exclusively to PARLIAMENT under Article 248. GST was inserted through Article 246A as a concurrent power outside the Seventh Schedule lists.

flashcard 488:
frontside:
Eighth Schedule of the Constitution of India
Prompt: What does the Eighth Schedule deal with? Which articles are linked, what is the complete breakdown of subjects/offices, and what is the primary UPSC trap?

backside:
Title: Recognised Languages of India
Linked Articles: Articles 344(1) and 351
Mandate and Scope: Lists the 22 languages constitutionally recognised in India. Originally contained 14 languages; 8 added by subsequent amendments.
Complete Provisions and Details: CHRONOLOGY: (1) Original 14 (1950): Assamese, Bengali, Gujarati, Hindi, Kannada, Kashmiri, Malayalam, Marathi, Odia, Punjabi, Sanskrit, Tamil, Telugu, Urdu; (2) 21st Amendment, 1967: Added SINDHI (15th); (3) 71st Amendment, 1992: Added KONKANI, MANIPURI, NEPALI (KMN - 16th, 17th, 18th); (4) 92nd Amendment, 2003: Added BODO, DOGRI, MAITHILI, SANTHALI (BDMS - 19th, 20th, 21st, 22nd); (5) 96th Amendment, 2011: Renamed 'Oriya' to 'ODIA'.
Exam Trap and Critical Distinction: ENGLISH is NOT in the Eighth Schedule. RAJASTHANI and BHOJPURI are also NOT in the Eighth Schedule. Inclusion in the 8th Schedule does not make a language an official language of the Union.

flashcard 489:
frontside:
Ninth Schedule of the Constitution of India
Prompt: What does the Ninth Schedule deal with? Which articles are linked, what is the complete breakdown of subjects/offices, and what is the primary UPSC trap?

backside:
Title: Validation of Certain Acts and Regulations
Linked Articles: Article 31B
Mandate and Scope: Created by the 1st Constitutional Amendment Act, 1951 to shield land reform, zamindari abolition, and agrarian/economic laws from challenge on grounds of violation of Fundamental Rights (Articles 14, 19, 31).
Complete Provisions and Details: Originally contained 13 Acts; now contains 284 Acts (including state land reform laws, nationalisation acts, and Tamil Nadu's 69% reservation law). LANDMARK RULING: I.R. Coelho v State of Tamil Nadu (2007) held that Ninth Schedule immunity is NOT absolute. Any law added to Ninth Schedule ON OR AFTER 24 APRIL 1973 (date of Kesavananda Bharati judgment) is OPEN TO JUDICIAL REVIEW on the touchstone of the Basic Structure doctrine.
Exam Trap and Critical Distinction: Laws in the Ninth Schedule are NOT immune from judicial review post-Coelho; pre-1973 laws are protected, but post-24 April 1973 additions must pass the basic structure test.

flashcard 490:
frontside:
Tenth Schedule of the Constitution of India
Prompt: What does the Tenth Schedule deal with? Which articles are linked, what is the complete breakdown of subjects/offices, and what is the primary UPSC trap?

backside:
Title: Provisions as to Disqualification on Ground of Defection (Anti-Defection Law)
Linked Articles: Articles 102(2) and 191(2)
Mandate and Scope: Added by the 52nd Constitutional Amendment Act, 1985 to combat the 'Aaya Ram, Gaya Ram' political defection culture. Disqualifies legislators on 4 grounds: (1) Voluntarily giving up membership of political party; (2) Voting/abstaining contrary to party whip without prior permission and not condoned within 15 days; (3) Independent member joining ANY political party after election; (4) Nominated member joining a political party AFTER 6 MONTHS from taking seat.
Complete Provisions and Details: DECIDING AUTHORITY: Speaker of Lok Sabha / Chairman of Rajya Sabha (or Assembly Speaker / Council Chairman). 91st Amendment Act, 2003 DELETED the 1/3rd 'split' exemption (splits are no longer protected; only 2/3rd 'merger' of legislature party is exempt). KIHOTO HOLLOHAN v ZACHILLHU (1992): Presiding officer acts as a Tribunal; decision is SUBJECT TO JUDICIAL REVIEW on grounds of mala fides, perversity, or violation of natural justice (court cannot intervene before decision).
Exam Trap and Critical Distinction: Independent member is disqualified if he joins ANY political party; Nominated member can join a political party WITHIN 6 MONTHS without disqualification (disqualified only if joining after 6 months). Speaker's decision is subject to judicial review.

flashcard 491:
frontside:
Eleventh Schedule of the Constitution of India
Prompt: What does the Eleventh Schedule deal with? Which articles are linked, what is the complete breakdown of subjects/offices, and what is the primary UPSC trap?

backside:
Title: Powers, Authority and Responsibilities of Panchayats
Linked Articles: Article 243G
Mandate and Scope: Added by the 73rd Constitutional Amendment Act, 1992. Specifies 29 FUNCTIONAL MATTERS that State Legislatures may devolve to Panchayati Raj Institutions.
Complete Provisions and Details: THE 29 SUBJECTS: (1) Agriculture and agricultural extension; (2) Land improvement and soil conservation; (3) Minor irrigation and watershed; (4) Animal husbandry, dairying, poultry; (5) Fisheries; (6) Social and farm forestry; (7) Minor forest produce; (8) Small-scale industries; (9) Khadi and village industries; (10) Rural housing; (11) Drinking water; (12) Fuel and fodder; (13) Roads, culverts, bridges, waterways; (14) Rural electrification; (15) Non-conventional energy; (16) Poverty alleviation; (17) Education including primary and secondary; (18) Technical and vocational training; (19) Adult and non-formal education; (20) Libraries; (21) Cultural activities; (22) Markets and fairs; (23) Health and sanitation, hospitals, PHCs; (24) Family welfare; (25) Women and child development; (26) Social welfare (differently-abled); (27) Welfare of weaker sections (SC/ST); (28) Public distribution system (PDS); (29) Maintenance of community assets.
Exam Trap and Critical Distinction: Eleventh Schedule has 29 SUBJECTS (Panchayats); Twelfth Schedule has 18 SUBJECTS (Municipalities). Listing is enabling, not automatic devolution.

flashcard 492:
frontside:
Twelfth Schedule of the Constitution of India
Prompt: What does the Twelfth Schedule deal with? Which articles are linked, what is the complete breakdown of subjects/offices, and what is the primary UPSC trap?

backside:
Title: Powers, Authority and Responsibilities of Municipalities
Linked Articles: Article 243W
Mandate and Scope: Added by the 74th Constitutional Amendment Act, 1992. Specifies 18 FUNCTIONAL MATTERS that State Legislatures may devolve to Urban Local Bodies (Municipalities).
Complete Provisions and Details: THE 18 SUBJECTS: (1) Urban planning including town planning; (2) Regulation of land-use and construction of buildings; (3) Planning for economic and social development; (4) Roads and bridges; (5) Water supply for domestic, industrial and commercial purposes; (6) Public health, sanitation, conservancy and solid waste management; (7) Fire services; (8) Urban forestry, protection of environment and ecology; (9) Safeguarding interests of weaker sections; (10) Slum improvement and upgradation; (11) Urban poverty alleviation; (12) Provision of urban amenities (parks, gardens, playgrounds); (13) Promotion of cultural, educational and aesthetic aspects; (14) Burials and burial grounds, cremations and electric crematoriums; (15) Cattle pounds, prevention of cruelty to animals; (16) Vital statistics including registration of births and deaths; (17) Public amenities including street lighting, parking lots, bus stops; (18) Regulation of slaughter houses and tanneries.
Exam Trap and Critical Distinction: Fire services, slum improvement, and registration of births/deaths are in 12th Schedule (Municipalities), NOT in 11th Schedule (Panchayats).

 - -

# SECTION 3: TOP CONSTITUTIONAL AMENDMENTS (1ST TO 106TH)

flashcard 493:
frontside:
The 1st Amendment Act, 1951 - First Constitutional Amendment
Prompt: What did the 1st Amendment Act, 1951 change in the Constitution? Why was it enacted, which articles were affected, and what is the key UPSC trap?

backside:
Amendment: 1st Amendment Act, 1951
Title: First Constitutional Amendment
Constitutional Changes: Enacted to overcome judicial decisions invalidating land reforms (Kameshwar Singh) and restrictions on speech (Romesh Thappar). Inserted Articles 31A, 31B, and the NINTH SCHEDULE. Added 3 new grounds of reasonable restriction on freedom of speech under Art 19(2): public order, friendly relations with foreign states, and incitement to an offence. Added Article 15(4) enabling special provisions for advancement of socially and educationally backward classes (SEBCs) and SCs/STs.
Key Context and Judicial Interface: Passed by the Provisional Parliament (the Constituent Assembly acting as legislature before the first general election). Upheld in Shankari Prasad v Union of India (1951).
Exam Trap: Ninth Schedule was NOT part of the original Constitution (1950); it was created by the 1st Amendment in 1951.

flashcard 494:
frontside:
The 7th Amendment Act, 1956 - States Reorganisation Amendment
Prompt: What did the 7th Amendment Act, 1956 change in the Constitution? Why was it enacted, which articles were affected, and what is the key UPSC trap?

backside:
Amendment: 7th Amendment Act, 1956
Title: States Reorganisation Amendment
Constitutional Changes: Implemented the recommendations of the States Reorganisation Commission (Fazal Ali Commission). Abolished the 4-fold classification of States (Part A, Part B, Part C, Part D) and replaced them with 14 STATES and 6 UNION TERRITORIES. Repealed Part VII (Art 238). Inserted Art 350A (mother-tongue primary instruction) and Art 350B (Special Officer for Linguistic Minorities). Amended Art 153 to allow same person as Governor of multiple States, and Art 231 to establish common High Courts.
Key Context and Judicial Interface: Most structural reorganisation of the Indian federal map; amended First and Fourth Schedules extensively.
Exam Trap: Repealed Part VII completely; established the modern framework of States and Union Territories.

flashcard 495:
frontside:
The 24th Amendment Act, 1971 - Restoration of Constituent Power to Parliament
Prompt: What did the 24th Amendment Act, 1971 change in the Constitution? Why was it enacted, which articles were affected, and what is the key UPSC trap?

backside:
Amendment: 24th Amendment Act, 1971
Title: Restoration of Constituent Power to Parliament
Constitutional Changes: Enacted in response to the Golak Nath case (1967), which had held that Parliament cannot abridge Fundamental Rights under Art 368. Amended Article 13 by adding 13(4) ('nothing in this article shall apply to any amendment under Art 368') and amended Article 368 to declare Parliament's constituent power to amend ANY provision including Fundamental Rights. Made PRESIDENT'S ASSENT MANDATORY for Constitutional Amendment Bills.
Key Context and Judicial Interface: Challenged and reviewed in Kesavananda Bharati (1973): Supreme Court upheld the validity of the 24th Amendment, but established that constituent power cannot alter the 'Basic Structure' of the Constitution.
Exam Trap: Made President's assent to Constitutional Amendment Bills mandatory; President has no veto power under Art 368.

flashcard 496:
frontside:
The 25th Amendment Act, 1971 - Property Compensation and DPSP Protection
Prompt: What did the 25th Amendment Act, 1971 change in the Constitution? Why was it enacted, which articles were affected, and what is the key UPSC trap?

backside:
Amendment: 25th Amendment Act, 1971
Title: Property Compensation and DPSP Protection
Constitutional Changes: Amended Article 31: Replaced the word 'compensation' with 'amount' for acquired property. Inserted ARTICLE 31C: Protected laws implementing Directive Principles in Article 39(b) and (c) from challenge under Articles 14, 19, and 31. Stated that no such law could be questioned in court on ground that it does not give effect to such policy.
Key Context and Judicial Interface: In Kesavananda Bharati (1973), the first part of Art 31C was UPHELD, but the judicial-bar clause ('no law shall be questioned in any court') was STRUCK DOWN as violating judicial review.
Exam Trap: Art 31C gives supremacy only to Arts 39(b) and 39(c) over Arts 14 and 19.

flashcard 497:
frontside:
The 26th Amendment Act, 1971 - Abolition of Privy Purses and Princely Privileges
Prompt: What did the 26th Amendment Act, 1971 change in the Constitution? Why was it enacted, which articles were affected, and what is the key UPSC trap?

backside:
Amendment: 26th Amendment Act, 1971
Title: Abolition of Privy Purses and Princely Privileges
Constitutional Changes: Abolished the privy purses and special privileges of the former rulers of princely states. Omitted Articles 291 and 362; inserted Article 363A declaring that recognition granted to rulers ceased and privy purses were abolished.
Key Context and Judicial Interface: Enacted after the Supreme Court in Madhav Rao Scindia case (1970) struck down the presidential derecognition order. The 26th Amendment was subsequently upheld by the Supreme Court (Raghunathrao Ganpatrao case 1993).
Exam Trap: Terminated royal pensions and feudal privileges permanently.

flashcard 498:
frontside:
The 36th Amendment Act, 1975 - Sikkim Admitted as 22nd State of India
Prompt: What did the 36th Amendment Act, 1975 change in the Constitution? Why was it enacted, which articles were affected, and what is the key UPSC trap?

backside:
Amendment: 36th Amendment Act, 1975
Title: Sikkim Admitted as 22nd State of India
Constitutional Changes: Made Sikkim a full-fledged State of the Indian Union and included it in the First Schedule. Inserted ARTICLE 371F containing special provisions for Sikkim (minimum 32 assembly seats, special Governor responsibility, Sangha seat). Repealed Article 2A and the Tenth Schedule as then constituted (which had made Sikkim an 'Associate State' under the 35th Amendment).
Key Context and Judicial Interface: Followed a referendum in Sikkim where people voted overwhelmingly to join the Indian Union and abolish the Chogyal monarchy.
Exam Trap: 35th Amendment made Sikkim an 'Associate State'; 36th Amendment made Sikkim a 'Full State'.

flashcard 499:
frontside:
The 42nd Amendment Act, 1976 - The 'Mini-Constitution'
Prompt: What did the 42nd Amendment Act, 1976 change in the Constitution? Why was it enacted, which articles were affected, and what is the key UPSC trap?

backside:
Amendment: 42nd Amendment Act, 1976
Title: The 'Mini-Constitution'
Constitutional Changes: Passed during the Internal Emergency under Indira Gandhi; most comprehensive amendment in Indian history (amended 53 articles, added 2 new Parts). KEY PROVISIONS: (1) Preamble: Added 'SOCIALIST', 'SECULAR', and 'INTEGRITY'; (2) Added PART IVA (Article 51A - 10 Fundamental Duties on Swaran Singh Committee recommendation); (3) Added PART XIVA (Articles 323A & 323B - Tribunals); (4) Added 3 new DPSPs: Art 39A (Free legal aid), Art 43A (Workers' participation in management), Art 48A (Environment/wildlife); (5) Transferred 5 subjects from State List to Concurrent List (Education, Forests, Weights/measures, Wildlife, Subordinate courts organisation); (6) Made President bound by Cabinet advice under Art 74; (7) Extended Lok Sabha and Assembly terms from 5 to 6 years; (8) Froze delimitation based on 1971 census; (9) Inserted Arts 368(4)-(5) claiming unlimited amendment power immune from courts.
Key Context and Judicial Interface: Minerva Mills v Union of India (1980) struck down Art 368(4)-(5) and the expansion of Art 31C as destructive of the Basic Structure. Many distortions were reversed by 43rd and 44th Amendments.
Exam Trap: Fundamental Duties, Socialist/Secular/Integrity, and Tribunals were all added by the 42nd Amendment.

flashcard 500:
frontside:
The 44th Amendment Act, 1978 - Restoration of Democratic Safeguards Post-Emergency
Prompt: What did the 44th Amendment Act, 1978 change in the Constitution? Why was it enacted, which articles were affected, and what is the key UPSC trap?

backside:
Amendment: 44th Amendment Act, 1978
Title: Restoration of Democratic Safeguards Post-Emergency
Constitutional Changes: Enacted by the Janata Government to prevent future abuse of emergency powers and restore civil liberties. KEY PROVISIONS: (1) Emergency safeguards in Art 352: Replaced 'internal disturbance' with 'ARMED REBELLION'; Cabinet's written recommendation required; special majority approval within 1 month; periodic 6-month renewals; (2) Fundamental Rights protection in Art 359: ARTICLES 20 AND 21 CANNOT BE SUSPENDED EVEN DURING AN EMERGENCY; (3) Art 358: Art 19 suspended only during External Emergency (war/aggression), not Armed Rebellion; (4) Property removed from Part III (Arts 19(1)(f) and 31 repealed; Art 300A inserted as legal right); (5) Art 74 proviso: President can return Cabinet advice ONCE for reconsideration; (6) Restored 5-year terms for Lok Sabha and State Assemblies; (7) Inserted Art 361A protecting media reporting of Parliament; (8) Added Art 38(2) DPSP (minimise income inequalities).
Key Context and Judicial Interface: Completely dismantled the authoritarian legal machinery created during the Emergency.
Exam Trap: Right to Property ceased to be a Fundamental Right under the 44th Amendment, NOT the 42nd Amendment. Arts 20 & 21 made non-suspendable under Art 359.

flashcard 501:
frontside:
The 52nd Amendment Act, 1985 - Anti-Defection Law
Prompt: What did the 52nd Amendment Act, 1985 change in the Constitution? Why was it enacted, which articles were affected, and what is the key UPSC trap?

backside:
Amendment: 52nd Amendment Act, 1985
Title: Anti-Defection Law
Constitutional Changes: Inserted the TENTH SCHEDULE and amended Articles 101, 102, 190, 191 to provide for disqualification of legislators on ground of defection from their political party. Disqualifies for: voluntary giving up membership, defying party whip, independents joining a party, nominated members joining after 6 months.
Key Context and Judicial Interface: Originally exempted splits (if 1/3rd members split) and mergers (if 2/3rd members merged). Decided by Speaker/Chairman. Upheld in Kihoto Hollohan (1992), which made Speaker's decision subject to judicial review.
Exam Trap: 52nd Amendment introduced the Tenth Schedule in 1985; 91st Amendment (2003) later deleted the 1/3rd split defence.

flashcard 502:
frontside:
The 61st Amendment Act, 1988 - Lowering of Voting Age from 21 to 18 Years
Prompt: What did the 61st Amendment Act, 1988 change in the Constitution? Why was it enacted, which articles were affected, and what is the key UPSC trap?

backside:
Amendment: 61st Amendment Act, 1988
Title: Lowering of Voting Age from 21 to 18 Years
Constitutional Changes: Amended Article 326 of the Constitution to reduce the minimum voting age for elections to the Lok Sabha and State Legislative Assemblies from 21 YEARS TO 18 YEARS.
Key Context and Judicial Interface: Enacted under Rajiv Gandhi government; operationalised on 28 March 1989. Substantially expanded the youth electorate in Indian democracy.
Exam Trap: Amended Article 326. Does not affect the minimum age to contest elections (which remains 25 for LS/Assembly, 30 for RS/Council, 21 for Panchayats).

flashcard 503:
frontside:
The 69th Amendment Act, 1991 - Special Status to Delhi as National Capital Territory (NCT)
Prompt: What did the 69th Amendment Act, 1991 change in the Constitution? Why was it enacted, which articles were affected, and what is the key UPSC trap?

backside:
Amendment: 69th Amendment Act, 1991
Title: Special Status to Delhi as National Capital Territory (NCT)
Constitutional Changes: Inserted ARTICLES 239AA AND 239AB to confer special constitutional status on the Union Territory of Delhi, designating it as the 'National Capital Territory of Delhi' with an elected Legislative Assembly and Council of Ministers headed by a Chief Minister.
Key Context and Judicial Interface: Assembly can legislate on State and Concurrent Lists EXCEPT: (1) Public Order (Entry 1); (2) Police (Entry 2); (3) Land (Entry 18). Total ministers capped at 10% of Assembly (max 7 ministers). Lieutenant Governor has power to refer differences of opinion to President.
Exam Trap: Delhi CoM is capped at 10% (Art 239AA(4)), unlike the 15% rule for States under Art 164. 3 subjects excluded from assembly: Police, Public Order, Land.

flashcard 504:
frontside:
The 73rd Amendment Act, 1992 - Constitutional Status to Panchayati Raj (Rural Local Bodies)
Prompt: What did the 73rd Amendment Act, 1992 change in the Constitution? Why was it enacted, which articles were affected, and what is the key UPSC trap?

backside:
Amendment: 73rd Amendment Act, 1992
Title: Constitutional Status to Panchayati Raj (Rural Local Bodies)
Constitutional Changes: Gave constitutional recognition to rural local self-governance. Inserted PART IX (Articles 243 to 243-O) and the ELEVENTH SCHEDULE (29 functional subjects). Enforced from 24 April 1993 (celebrated as National Panchayati Raj Day).
Key Context and Judicial Interface: MANDATORY PROVISIONS: (1) 3-tier system (intermediate tier optional for states <20 lakh pop); (2) Direct elections for all seats; (3) 1/3rd reservation for women (seats and chairpersons); (4) Proportional SC/ST reservation; (5) Fixed 5-year tenure; (6) State Election Commission (Art 243K); (7) State Finance Commission every 5 years (Art 243I).
Exam Trap: Gram Sabha is constitutional under Art 243A. Intermediate tier is optional for states with population <= 20 lakhs.

flashcard 505:
frontside:
The 74th Amendment Act, 1992 - Constitutional Status to Municipalities (Urban Local Bodies)
Prompt: What did the 74th Amendment Act, 1992 change in the Constitution? Why was it enacted, which articles were affected, and what is the key UPSC trap?

backside:
Amendment: 74th Amendment Act, 1992
Title: Constitutional Status to Municipalities (Urban Local Bodies)
Constitutional Changes: Gave constitutional status to urban local self-governance. Inserted PART IXA (Articles 243P to 243ZG) and the TWELFTH SCHEDULE (18 functional subjects). Enforced from 1 June 1993.
Key Context and Judicial Interface: MANDATORY PROVISIONS: (1) 3 types of urban bodies: Nagar Panchayat, Municipal Council, Municipal Corporation; (2) Direct ward elections; (3) Min 1/3rd reservation for women; (4) Fixed 5-year tenure; (5) Wards Committees mandatory for population >= 3 lakhs (Art 243S); (6) District Planning Committee (DPC - Art 243ZD, 4/5th elected); (7) Metropolitan Planning Committee (MPC - Art 243ZE, 2/3rd elected).
Exam Trap: DPC requires 4/5th elected members; MPC requires 2/3rd elected members. 12th Schedule has 18 subjects.

flashcard 506:
frontside:
The 84th Amendment Act, 2001 - Delimitation Freeze Extended to 2026
Prompt: What did the 84th Amendment Act, 2001 change in the Constitution? Why was it enacted, which articles were affected, and what is the key UPSC trap?

backside:
Amendment: 84th Amendment Act, 2001
Title: Delimitation Freeze Extended to 2026
Constitutional Changes: Amended Articles 81, 82, 170, and 330 to extend the freeze on the readjustment of total seats in the Lok Sabha and State Legislative Assemblies for another 25 years (until after the first Census taken after the year 2026).
Key Context and Judicial Interface: Allowed internal delimitation (readjusting boundaries of territorial constituencies within each state) based on the 1991 census (later updated to 2001 census by the 87th Amendment Act, 2003).
Exam Trap: Total seats frozen on 1971 census until post-2026 census; internal boundary delimitation conducted on 2001 census.

flashcard 507:
frontside:
The 86th Amendment Act, 2002 - Right to Education (Fundamental Right)
Prompt: What did the 86th Amendment Act, 2002 change in the Constitution? Why was it enacted, which articles were affected, and what is the key UPSC trap?

backside:
Amendment: 86th Amendment Act, 2002
Title: Right to Education (Fundamental Right)
Constitutional Changes: Made elementary education a Fundamental Right. KEY 3-WAY AMENDMENT: (1) Inserted ARTICLE 21A: Free and compulsory education to all children aged 6 to 14 years; (2) Substituted ARTICLE 45 (DPSP): State shall endeavour to provide early childhood care and education for children below 6 years; (3) Added ARTICLE 51A(k) (Fundamental Duty): Duty of parent/guardian to provide education opportunities to child aged 6 to 14 years.
Key Context and Judicial Interface: Led to the enactment of the Right of Children to Free and Compulsory Education (RTE) Act, 2009, operationalised on 1 April 2010.
Exam Trap: Simultaneously amended Part III (Art 21A - FR), Part IV (Art 45 - DPSP), and Part IVA (Art 51A(k) - 11th Fundamental Duty).

flashcard 508:
frontside:
The 89th Amendment Act, 2003 - Bifurcation of National Commission for SCs and STs
Prompt: What did the 89th Amendment Act, 2003 change in the Constitution? Why was it enacted, which articles were affected, and what is the key UPSC trap?

backside:
Amendment: 89th Amendment Act, 2003
Title: Bifurcation of National Commission for SCs and STs
Constitutional Changes: Bifurcated the combined National Commission for Scheduled Castes and Scheduled Tribes into two separate constitutional commissions: (1) National Commission for Scheduled Castes (NCSC) under ARTICLE 338; (2) National Commission for Scheduled Tribes (NCST) under new ARTICLE 338A.
Key Context and Judicial Interface: Each Commission consists of a Chairperson, Vice-Chairperson, and 3 other Members appointed by President by warrant under hand and seal. Vested with powers of a Civil Court.
Exam Trap: Bifurcated in 2003; NCST works under Ministry of Tribal Affairs; NCSC under Ministry of Social Justice.

flashcard 509:
frontside:
The 91st Amendment Act, 2003 - Cabinet Size Ceiling and Tightening of Anti-Defection
Prompt: What did the 91st Amendment Act, 2003 change in the Constitution? Why was it enacted, which articles were affected, and what is the key UPSC trap?

backside:
Amendment: 91st Amendment Act, 2003
Title: Cabinet Size Ceiling and Tightening of Anti-Defection
Constitutional Changes: Enacted on recommendations of NCRWC (Venkatachaliah Commission) to curb jumbo cabinets and defections. KEY PROVISIONS: (1) Article 75(1A): Total Union Ministers including PM cannot exceed 15% OF LOK SABHA STRENGTH; (2) Article 164(1A): Total State Ministers including CM cannot exceed 15% OF ASSEMBLY STRENGTH (minimum 12 ministers in States); (3) Tenth Schedule amendment: DELETED THE 1/3RD 'SPLIT' DEFENCE (splits are no longer exempt; only 2/3rd merger is exempt); (4) Articles 75(1B), 164(1B), and 361B: Defectors disqualified from holding any ministerial office or remunerative political post.
Key Context and Judicial Interface: Ended the loophole where 1/3rd MPs/MLAs could engineer a split and escape disqualification. 15% cap applies to Lower House strength.
Exam Trap: 

flashcard 510:
frontside:
The 92nd Amendment Act, 2003 - Four Languages Added to Eighth Schedule (Bodo, Dogri, Maithili, Santhali)
Prompt: What did the 92nd Amendment Act, 2003 change in the Constitution? Why was it enacted, which articles were affected, and what is the key UPSC trap?

backside:
Amendment: 92nd Amendment Act, 2003
Title: Four Languages Added to Eighth Schedule (Bodo, Dogri, Maithili, Santhali)
Constitutional Changes: Amended the Eighth Schedule to add 4 NEW LANGUAGES: BODO, DOGRI, MAITHILI, and SANTHALI (popular mnemonic: BDMS). Raised total recognised languages from 18 to 22.
Key Context and Judicial Interface: Sanathali is the only tribal language in the 8th Schedule; Dogri is spoken in Jammu region; Maithili in Bihar; Bodo in Assam.
Exam Trap: Brought total Eighth Schedule languages to the current 22.

flashcard 511:
frontside:
The 97th Amendment Act, 2011 - Constitutional Status to Co-operative Societies
Prompt: What did the 97th Amendment Act, 2011 change in the Constitution? Why was it enacted, which articles were affected, and what is the key UPSC trap?

backside:
Amendment: 97th Amendment Act, 2011
Title: Constitutional Status to Co-operative Societies
Constitutional Changes: Gave constitutional recognition to Co-operative Societies through 3 changes: (1) Amended Article 19(1)(c) to include right to form 'co-operative societies' as a Fundamental Right; (2) Inserted Article 43B (DPSP) for promotion of co-operatives; (3) Added PART IXB (Articles 243ZH to 243ZT) regulating co-operative societies.
Key Context and Judicial Interface: LANDMARK JUDGMENT: Union of India v Rajendra Shah (2021) struck down Part IXB as applied to State co-operatives because it affected State legislative power (Entry 32 List II) and was NOT RATIFIED BY 50% OF STATES under Art 368(2). However, SC UPHELD Part IXB for Multi-State Co-operatives, and upheld Art 19(1)(c) and Art 43B.
Exam Trap: Part IXB is invalid for single-state co-operatives (post-2021 SC ruling); valid only for multi-state co-operatives. Art 19(1)(c) and 43B remain fully valid.

flashcard 512:
frontside:
The 99th Amendment Act, 2014 - National Judicial Appointments Commission (NJAC) [Struck Down]
Prompt: What did the 99th Amendment Act, 2014 change in the Constitution? Why was it enacted, which articles were affected, and what is the key UPSC trap?

backside:
Amendment: 99th Amendment Act, 2014
Title: National Judicial Appointments Commission (NJAC) [Struck Down]
Constitutional Changes: Created the National Judicial Appointments Commission (NJAC) to replace the 22-year-old Collegium system for appointment of SC and HC judges. Inserted Articles 124A, 124B, and 124C. Composed of CJI (Chairperson), 2 senior SC judges, Union Law Minister, and 2 eminent persons.
Key Context and Judicial Interface: STRUCK DOWN: In the Fourth Judges Case (Supreme Court Advocates-on-Record Association v Union of India, 2015), a 5-judge Constitution Bench by 4:1 majority struck down the 99th Amendment and the NJAC Act as UNCONSTITUTIONAL for violating JUDICIAL INDEPENDENCE, a part of the Basic Structure. The Collegium system was revived.
Exam Trap: Rare instance where a Constitutional Amendment was struck down in its ENTIRETY by the Supreme Court as violating Basic Structure.

flashcard 513:
frontside:
The 100th Amendment Act, 2015 - Land Boundary Agreement (LBA) with Bangladesh
Prompt: What did the 100th Amendment Act, 2015 change in the Constitution? Why was it enacted, which articles were affected, and what is the key UPSC trap?

backside:
Amendment: 100th Amendment Act, 2015
Title: Land Boundary Agreement (LBA) with Bangladesh
Constitutional Changes: Operationalised the 1974 Land Boundary Agreement and 2011 Protocol between India and Bangladesh. Amended the First Schedule to exchange 111 Indian enclaves in Bangladesh for 51 Bangladeshi enclaves in India, and settled undemarcated boundary sections across Assam, West Bengal, Meghalaya, and Tripura.
Key Context and Judicial Interface: Followed the principle laid down in Berubari Union case (1960): Cession of Indian territory to a foreign state requires a Constitutional Amendment under Article 368.
Exam Trap: Required Art 368 constitutional amendment because it involved cession of Indian enclave territory to Bangladesh.

flashcard 514:
frontside:
The 101st Amendment Act, 2016 - Goods and Services Tax (GST)
Prompt: What did the 101st Amendment Act, 2016 change in the Constitution? Why was it enacted, which articles were affected, and what is the key UPSC trap?

backside:
Amendment: 101st Amendment Act, 2016
Title: Goods and Services Tax (GST)
Constitutional Changes: Introduced the Goods and Services Tax (GST), India's most significant indirect tax reform ('One Nation, One Tax'). KEY PROVISIONS: (1) Inserted ARTICLE 246A: Simultaneous legislative power to Parliament and State Legislatures on GST; (2) Inserted ARTICLE 269A: Levying and collection of Integrated GST (IGST) on inter-state supply by Union, apportioned to consuming State; (3) Inserted ARTICLE 279A: GST Council (chaired by Union FM, weighted voting: Union 1/3rd, States 2/3rd, 3/4th majority to decide); (4) Omitted Art 268A and amended 7th Schedule.
Key Context and Judicial Interface: Destination-based consumption tax subsuming Central excise, service tax, state VAT, entry tax, octroi, and luxury tax. Enforced from 1 July 2017.
Exam Trap: Art 246A creates simultaneous power; GST Council recommendations are persuasive, not legally binding on legislatures (Mohit Minerals case 2022).

flashcard 515:
frontside:
The 102nd Amendment Act, 2018 - Constitutional Status to National Commission for Backward Classes (NCBC)
Prompt: What did the 102nd Amendment Act, 2018 change in the Constitution? Why was it enacted, which articles were affected, and what is the key UPSC trap?

backside:
Amendment: 102nd Amendment Act, 2018
Title: Constitutional Status to National Commission for Backward Classes (NCBC)
Constitutional Changes: Accorded constitutional status to the National Commission for Backward Classes (NCBC) by inserting ARTICLE 338B. Inserted ARTICLE 342A empowering the President to notify the list of Socially and Educationally Backward Classes (SEBCs) in consultation with Governors.
Key Context and Judicial Interface: Elevated NCBC from a statutory body (created in 1993) to a constitutional body with same status and Civil Court powers as NCSC (338) and NCST (338A).
Exam Trap: NCBC gained constitutional status under Art 338B via 102nd Amendment in 2018.

flashcard 516:
frontside:
The 103rd Amendment Act, 2019 - 10% EWS Reservation for Economically Weaker Sections
Prompt: What did the 103rd Amendment Act, 2019 change in the Constitution? Why was it enacted, which articles were affected, and what is the key UPSC trap?

backside:
Amendment: 103rd Amendment Act, 2019
Title: 10% EWS Reservation for Economically Weaker Sections
Constitutional Changes: Introduced up to 10% RESERVATION FOR ECONOMICALLY WEAKER SECTIONS (EWS) of citizens other than SC, ST, and SEBCs/OBCs. Inserted ARTICLE 15(6) (reservation in educational institutions including private unaided, except minority institutions) and ARTICLE 16(6) (reservation in public employment/posts).
Key Context and Judicial Interface: LANDMARK JUDGMENT: Upheld by 3:2 majority in Janhit Abhiyan v Union of India (2022). Supreme Court held that: (1) Reservation based solely on economic criteria does not violate Basic Structure; (2) Excluding SC/ST/OBC from EWS is reasonable; (3) 50% reservation ceiling (from Indra Sawhney) is not inflexible and applies to backward class reservations, not EWS.
Exam Trap: EWS reservation applies ONLY to general category poor (excludes SCs, STs, and OBCs who are covered by other reservations). 10% EWS is in addition to the existing 50% quota.

flashcard 517:
frontside:
The 104th Amendment Act, 2020 - Extension of SC/ST Reservations and Abolition of Anglo-Indian Nomination
Prompt: What did the 104th Amendment Act, 2020 change in the Constitution? Why was it enacted, which articles were affected, and what is the key UPSC trap?

backside:
Amendment: 104th Amendment Act, 2020
Title: Extension of SC/ST Reservations and Abolition of Anglo-Indian Nomination
Constitutional Changes: Amended ARTICLE 334 to extend the reservation of seats for Scheduled Castes and Scheduled Tribes in the Lok Sabha and State Legislative Assemblies for another 10 YEARS (UNTIL 25 JANUARY 2030). Abolished the nominated seats for the Anglo-Indian community in Lok Sabha (Art 331) and State Assemblies (Art 333) by letting their reservation expire.
Key Context and Judicial Interface: Enacted in January 2020 before the 70-year deadline of 25 Jan 2020 expired.
Exam Trap: SC/ST political reservation extended to 2030; Anglo-Indian nomination in Lok Sabha and Assemblies was terminated.

flashcard 518:
frontside:
The 105th Amendment Act, 2021 - Restoration of States' Power to Maintain State OBC Lists
Prompt: What did the 105th Amendment Act, 2021 change in the Constitution? Why was it enacted, which articles were affected, and what is the key UPSC trap?

backside:
Amendment: 105th Amendment Act, 2021
Title: Restoration of States' Power to Maintain State OBC Lists
Constitutional Changes: Amended Articles 338B, 342A, and 366(26C) to clarify that every State and Union Territory has the power to prepare and maintain its OWN STATE LIST of Socially and Educationally Backward Classes (SEBCs/OBCs) for the purposes of state-level reservations and schemes.
Key Context and Judicial Interface: Passed unanimously to overcome the Supreme Court's May 2021 ruling in the Maratha Reservation case (Jaishri Laxmanrao Patil), which had interpreted the 102nd Amendment as taking away States' power to identify OBCs, leaving that power solely with the President. The 105th Amendment restored state federal competence over OBC identification.
Exam Trap: Re-established the dual-list system: Central List prepared by President under 342A(1); State Lists prepared by respective State Governments under 342A(3).

flashcard 519:
frontside:
The 106th Amendment Act, 2023 - Nari Shakti Vandan Adhiniyam (Women's Reservation Act)
Prompt: What did the 106th Amendment Act, 2023 change in the Constitution? Why was it enacted, which articles were affected, and what is the key UPSC trap?

backside:
Amendment: 106th Amendment Act, 2023
Title: Nari Shakti Vandan Adhiniyam (Women's Reservation Act)
Constitutional Changes: Provides ONE-THIRD (33%) RESERVATION FOR WOMEN in the House of the People (Lok Sabha), State Legislative Assemblies, and the Legislative Assembly of NCT of Delhi. Inserted ARTICLE 330A (women in Lok Sabha), ARTICLE 332A (women in Assemblies), and amended ARTICLE 239AA. Includes sub-reservation of 1/3rd for SC and ST women within the reserved SC/ST seats.
Key Context and Judicial Interface: COMMENCEMENT CONDITION (Art 334A): The reservation shall come into effect AFTER an exercise of DELIMITATION is undertaken for this purpose after the relevant figures for the first CENSUS taken after commencement have been published. Duration: Initially for 15 YEARS (extendable by Parliament).
Exam Trap: Women's reservation is NOT immediately operational: it takes effect only after the next Census and subsequent Delimitation. Applies to Lok Sabha and State Assemblies; does NOT apply to Rajya Sabha or State Legislative Councils.
